# Full transverse coupling: exact separation of the actual 63-step parent

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, worker max,
ninth launch, cumulative round 20. The verifier has not accepted this result.
The current TASK and public ledger allow zero verification calls. The only
accepted fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for the definition and necessity of the
original smooth-convex inequalities Q. Every other assertion used in this
package is derived below, including the construction of the input children.

## 1. Statement and scope

Put s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda. Choose a>0 and xi>=theta,
and define H=a+1, b=1+H*xi, D=1+3xi+xi^2. The seed word is (a,b,a).
Its actual orthogonal-core transcript is given in section 3. Recursively
form the words h[0]=(a,b,a) and

    A_j=1+sum h[j]=lambda^j A_0,  A_0=H(2+xi),
    r_j=theta A_j,  h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]).              (1)

Thus h[j] has 4*2^j-1 steps. At every join use isometric copies of the
complete child transcripts, leave the left copy unscaled, scale the right
copy by alpha=r_j ||w||/||x_0||, and align its initial point with -r_j w.
Keep the projection values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and the optimum jet 0.
No extra gradient coordinate, translation, or function-value change is made.

The following are the claims of this joint candidate.

1. The most general real cross Gram for this operation is parameterized
   by a completely unrestricted real contraction between the two fixed
   transverse spaces. Section 2 gives its exact PSD condition, both sets of
   cross normals, all bridge/optimum constraints, explicit realization,
   common projection function when feasible, and true distance.
2. For zero transverse cross map at the first three joins, the complete
   7-, 15-, and 31-step children are legal. If xi>theta they have strictly
   positive core defect. Nevertheless, for the two actual 31-step children,
   **every** real transverse map at the 63-step join violates

       Q_(36,30)=e_30 [lambda T(xi)-1]>0,
       T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/D^2,  e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.   (2)

   This includes all maps satisfying the full PSD condition, without a rank,
   sign, symmetry, or boundary-positivity restriction.
3. The previously observed failed row Q_(32,25) is NOT invariant. Its
   transverse coefficient is nonzero. The different row (2) is the exact
   separator; it was not licensed to be inferred from the planar example.
4. If xi>theta, infeasibility persists even when arbitrary transverse maps
   are chosen at every earlier join, and even if different choices are made
   in different subtrees. Every such 63-step construction, with all earlier
   children legal if possible, satisfies the two-row identity

       [(1+xi) Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
          = lambda q-1
          = (xi-theta)(s*xi+lambda)/D >0,
       q=xi(2+xi)/D.                                    (3)

   The two weights are strictly positive. Thus the claimed parent cannot
   satisfy all original Q inequalities. This broader claim needs xi>theta;
   (2), for the fixed rank-one-built children, also covers xi=theta.
5. Re-selecting arbitrary real function values cannot repair either
   obstruction while the displayed positions, gradients, and optimum at
   zero are retained. The projection values are forced separately on every
   embedded seed copy, as proved at the end of section 5.

For the particular modified-word interval requested by TASK, let

    p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3,
    a=s-p0*t, b=2+q0*t, 0<=t<=1/50,
    xi=(b-1)/(a+1).                                     (4)

Then a>0 and xi>=theta, with strict inequality for t>0. The SAME algebraic
seed t=1/100 was used for every discovery comparison. Explicitly it is

    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    H=(284+311s)/300, xi=(290+8s)/(284+311s),
    A_0=(143+105s)/50.                                  (5)

The words, physical child transcripts, and normalization are unchanged
between the zero-map and full-map 63-step tests. These claims exclude this
specified recursive projection construction. They do not exclude different
seed transcripts, lifts, translations, different optimum alignment, or operations, and
do not resolve the original all-schedule, all-horizon exact-profile goal.

## 2. All real transverse freedom and every original constraint

A child input consists of a finite nonnegative word p of length ell and
vectors x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, including all duplicates and the endpoint.
Define y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, w=x_ell=g_ell!=0, and require

    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, e_i>=0.              (6)

Set K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell) and

    F_K(x)=max_(v in K)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_star=0.                (7)

Completing the square proves that the maximizer is P_K(x), the Euclidean
projection. Its unique minimizer condition is
<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K. Thus (6) makes g_i=P_K(x_i).
Adding this condition at two points gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(z)||^2<=<x-z,P_K(x)-P_K(z)>, hence P_K is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the two maxima at the opposite maximizers gives
0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=||d||^2. Consequently F_K is differentiable,
convex and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K, and it interpolates (7).

The original residual from fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                 (8)

Expansion of the inner products proves this identity. In particular,
Q_i,star=0 and Q_star,i=-e_i. These include both optimum incidences.

The minimizer set of F_K is K^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in K}.
For necessity, test epsilon*v in (7), divide by epsilon, and let epsilon
decrease to zero. Sufficiency follows directly from (7), including v=0.
The update equations imply x_0=w+sum_(i<ell)p_i g_i. For every minimizer z,
<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Zero is therefore a closest
minimizer: the TRUE distance is R=||x_0||>0.

For two children L,R, set R_R=||x_R,0||, e=w_L/||w_L||, d=x_R,0/R_R,
alpha=r||w_L||/R_R for the bridge 1+r, where r>0. Let E=e^perp in the
left child span and Dspace=d^perp in the right child span. Write
u_perp=u-<u,e>e and v_perp=v-<v,d>d.

Every allowed pair of real isometries J_L,J_R satisfies J_R d=-J_L e.
It therefore has cross form

    B_C(u,v)=<J_L u,J_R v>
       =-<u,e><v,d>+<u_perp,C v_perp>,                    (9)

where C:Dspace->E is linear and ||C||<=1. To prove necessity, project
J_R v_perp orthogonally onto J_L E; its norm is at most ||v_perp||.
All axial/transverse cross terms vanish because the transverse images are
orthogonal to the common axis. No other restriction on C follows.

Conversely, for any real C with ||C||<=1, put S_C=(I-C^T C)^(1/2), the
positive semidefinite real square root. In R direct-sum E direct-sum Dspace,
define

    J_L u=(<u,e>,u_perp,0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>,C v_perp,S_C v_perp).                   (10)

The equality C^T C+S_C^2=I proves both isometries, their alignment, and (9).
Zero eigenvalues are retained; nothing is clipped or discarded. In chosen
orthonormal transverse bases, the exact PSD condition is

    [[I,C],[C^T,I]] >=0.                                (11)

Indeed its quadratic form at (z,v) is
||z+C v||^2+<v,(I-C^T C)v>. Setting z=-Cv proves necessity, and the same
identity proves sufficiency. Thus (11) is equivalent to realizability,
including singular cases. For the fixed 31-step children their full spans
have dimension 9 and C is an arbitrary 8 by 8 real matrix. The ranks 2,3,5,9
of the zero-map seed and successive joins follow from the direct-sum
realization (10): the two spans intersect exactly in their aligned axis.
The gradients span each child space, because the seed U,V do and every
join retains both embedded spanning families.

Use J_L(x_L,i,g_L,i,y_L,i) as all left data and
alpha J_R(x_R,j,g_R,j,y_R,j) as all right data. Let m=ell_L+1, so that
right child sample j has parent index m+j. The bridge equation is exact:

    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=alpha J_R x_R,0.               (12)

Left values remain f_L,i, and right values are alpha^2 f_R,j. All original
constraints are precisely the following list:

    Q_(i,k)=Q^L_(i,k)                                      (left/left),
    Q_(m+j,m+l)=alpha^2 Q^R_(j,l)                         (right/right),
    Q_(m+j,i)=alpha B_C(y_L,i,g_R,j)-e_L,i,               (13L)
    Q_(i,m+j)=alpha B_C(g_L,i,y_R,j)-alpha^2 e_R,j,       (13R)
    Q_(i,star)=Q_(m+j,star)=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-e_L,i, Q_(star,m+j)=-alpha^2 e_R,j.

Every i,k ranges over the entire left sample list and every j,l over the
entire right sample list. Diagonal rows are zero. There are no other rows.
In particular the left endpoint/bridge sample i=ell_L has y_L,i=0, so
Q_(m+j,ell_L)=0, while its reverse rows are

    Q_(ell_L,m+j)
      = r||w_L||^2/R_R^2 [-<x_R,0,y_R,j>-r e_R,j].       (14)

These rows are invariant under C. All terminal-residual rows are zero
because y_R,ell_R=0. If a left residual happens to equal a_pos*w_L with
a_pos>0 and its gradient is w_L, the other invariant rows are

    Q_(m+j,i)=a_pos||w_L||^2[-r c_R,j-1],
    c_R,j=<g_R,j,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                          (15)

Equations (13L)-(13R) are the TWO full sets of original cross normals;
neither is replaced by a sign condition or by PSD. If and only if all their
rows are nonpositive, (7) for the parent convex hull constructs one common
function, checks every Q, and gives true initial distance R_L. The endpoint
is W=alpha J_R w_R. For capacities A=1+sum p_L and B=1+sum p_R, and
P=A+B+r, put kappa_L=A^2||w_L||^2/R_L^2 and
kappa_R=B^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2. Direct substitution gives

    kappa_parent=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 B^2).       (16)

The choice rP=AB yields their product. For identical capacities the
positive solution is r=theta A and P=lambda A, giving (1). This ratio law
does not imply legality. No ratio is used as an actual payoff when (13)
fails.

## 3. The actual seed, positive interval, and complete legal children

Let U,V be orthogonal vectors with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=xi. Define

    (x_0,g_0)=(H(1+xi)U+HV,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+HV,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(HV,V), (x_3,g_3)=(V,V).                 (17)

Its word is (a,b,a), and direct subtraction checks all three updates.
Its residuals are

    y_0=(a+Hxi)U+HV, y_1=Hxi U+HV, y_2=aV, y_3=0.

At samples 0 and 1 the normal against V is respectively -a and 0; the
normal against 0 is respectively -(a+Hxi) and -Hxi. The normals against U
are zero. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 both equal -a xi and
the normal against V is zero. At sample 3 all normals are zero. These
list all distinct vertices of conv(0,U,V), including duplicate gradients.
Thus every Q is nonpositive, with e_i>0 for i=0,1,2. The seed is an actual
instance of (6)-(7), and

    R^2=H^2 D, ||w_0||^2=xi,
    k_0=<x_0,w_0>/R^2=xi/(HD),
    zeta_0=||w_0||^2/R^2=xi/(H^2D),
    kappa_0=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa_0-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                    (18)

Since xi^2+2xi-1=(xi-theta)(xi+lambda), the defect is positive precisely
when xi>theta. To check (4), the rational inequalities 7/5<s<10/7 follow
by squaring. They imply 0<p0<1/5 and q0>2/5, so a>0 throughout the stated
interval. Also H=lambda-p0 t>0 and
xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H, whose coefficient is positive. This proves the
interval and positive-defect assertions without floating arithmetic.

Here is a complete legality check for the fixed zero-map children, needed
to ensure that the final obstruction is not caused by illegal inputs.
For e_i>0 retain the signed lists

    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i,
    c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i.

All gradient samples are retained in a_i,c_i. Residuals with e_i=0 are
exactly zero and satisfy all their rows automatically. For two identical
children with C=0, (13) is equivalent to

    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,
    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                             (19)

The seed lists are nonnegative. Their attained maxima are

    max a_i=1, max B_i=1, max c_i=c_*=(1+xi)/(HD),
    max D_i=A_0.

For the last assertion, D_1=A_0, D_2=H, and
D_0=H(1+xi)+H^2xi/(a+Hxi)<=H(2+xi). Also B_0=Hxi/(a+Hxi)<=1,
B_1=B_2=1. Hence the first zero-map join is legal by (19).

For a zero-map join the exact transported lists are

    a_left=-k/(r zeta) a,   a_right=a,
    B_left=-rk B,          B_right=B,
    c_left=c,              c_right=-rk c,
    D_left=D,              D_right=-k/(r zeta) D,
    k_new=-r k^2,          zeta_new=r^2 zeta^2.           (20)

These formulas follow from (9) with C=0 and the definitions; the right
energies and endpoint squared norms carry alpha^2 and the left initial
norm is unchanged. Set omega=1/(2+xi), q=A_0 k_0=xi(2+xi)/D,
u=lambda omega, and v=theta q. For xi>=theta, u<=1, while 0<q<1 because
D-xi(2+xi)=1+xi. After the first join the attained extreme intervals are

    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*],
    D_i in[-u A_0,A_0].                                (21)

Induction in (20) gives, for j>=1,
A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and k_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j).
The new copying factors in (20) are positive and at most one. Each old
extremum is retained by the unmultiplied copy of its corresponding list.
Therefore (21) remains the exact list of extrema at every later formal
zero-map join. For j>=1, (19) becomes

    r_j v c_* = lambda^(j-2) T(xi) <=1,
    u A_0 <= r_j.                                      (22)

The polynomial identity

    D^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=1+2xi+3xi^2+xi^3>0

shows 0<T(xi)<1. Thus j=1 and j=2 satisfy both inequalities (22), since
r_1=A_0 and r_2=lambda A_0. This proves legality of h[1],h[2],h[3], with
all original normals. Formula (16) gives kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j) at these
legal levels. Their functions, gradients, values and TRUE distance are
those in section 2, not just formal Gram matrices.

## 4. Why the old failed row moves, but a different row cannot move

For the actual fixed child h[3], index 0 is the original seed gradient U,
and the initial point is the original seed x_0 under the leftmost isometry.
Consequently the squared norm of its component orthogonal to x_0 is

    ||(g_0)_perp||^2=1-(1+xi)^2/D=xi/D>0.              (23)

Child residual y_25 comes from seed residual y_1 inside the first left
half of h[1], followed by two right-copy embeddings. Let beta=alpha_1
alpha_2>0 be their combined scale. The h[1] endpoint w_1 and its residual
y_1 satisfy

    ||y_1||^2=H^2 xi(1+xi),
    <y_1,w_1>=-r_0 xi^2/D,
    ||w_1||^2=r_0^2 xi^2/(H^2D).

These follow by substituting (17) into (9) at C=0. The right-copy
isometries carry BOTH vectors with the same beta. Thus the left residual
component orthogonal to w_3 has squared norm

    ||(y_25)_perp||^2
      =beta^2 H^2 xi [1+xi-xi/D]>0.                   (24)

Equations (23)-(24) prove that

    Q_(32,25)(C)=Q_(32,25)(0)
        +alpha_3 <(y_25)_perp,C(g_0)_perp>             (25)

has a nonzero linear coefficient. For example, the norm-one map
v -> -<(g_0)_perp,v>(y_25)_perp/
      (||(g_0)_perp|| ||(y_25)_perp||)
strictly decreases this row. This assertion concerns that row only; it
does not claim simultaneous feasibility of the other rows.

Now use the DIFFERENT residual y_30. At every join the final two seed
gradients remain equal to the new endpoint w. Since the last step is a,

    g_30=g_31=w_3, x_30=H w_3, y_30=a w_3,
    e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.                                (26)

This is an actual sampled residual, not a boundary surrogate. It has zero
transverse component. At the FIRST zero-map join the right copy of seed
gradient 0 has index 4. Formula (20) gives c_4=-r_0 k_0 c_*=-v c_*.
Its two later LEFT embeddings preserve this gradient/initial pairing and
R^2, so it remains c_4=-v c_* in h[3]. At the final join, child gradient 4
has parent index 32+4=36. Substitution of (26) into (15) gives exactly (2).

To establish its sign on the entire stated interval, logarithmic
differentiation and multiplication by the positive denominator gives

    [T'(xi)/T(xi)] xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D
       =2+xi+xi^2+xi^3>0.

Also T(theta)=2-s, by theta^2+2theta=1. Therefore
lambda T(xi)-1 >= lambda(2-s)-1 = s-1=theta>0.
This is a one-row exact affine separation. Its PSD multiplier can be
zero: after enforcing the alignment, the left side of this single
original required inequality is a positive constant on the ENTIRE
realizable cross-Gram set, indeed on the affine space of all C. No
floating solver verdict is used in the proof.

For comparison, the old row has Q_(32,25)(0)/e_25=lambda T(xi)-1 by (21):
B_25=-v and c_0=c_*. This equality does not make it invariant; (25) gives
its nonzero derivative. The invariant bridge-gradient obstruction for the
separate planar core is unnecessary here. No planar formula has been
applied to this orthogonal core.

## 5. A two-row separator when earlier cross maps are also free

We now rederive a stronger obstruction without assuming C=0 at ANY earlier
join. Every first join still has the exact bridge identity

    H(1+xi) g_4 + H g_6 = -r_0 w_0.                    (27)

Here g_4 is the scaled right copy of U, g_6 the scaled right copy of V,
and w_0 the left seed endpoint. Indeed the left side is the isometric
image of alpha_0 x_seed,0, so (12) proves (27) for every allowed transverse
map. Take inner product with the retained initial point x_0 and divide by
R^2. Left-copy embeddings preserve this equality and all its scalars.
Thus in the leftmost 7-step subtree of ANY 31-step right child,

    [(1+xi)c_4+c_6]/(2+xi)
        =-r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.                     (28)

Both gradients remain at indices 4 and 6 of that child. No agreement
between choices in different subtrees is needed: their initial point and
initial norm equal the common seed's up to an isometry, and (28) has the
same right side. The left 31-step child also retains its last seed pair,
so it satisfies (26) with its own nonzero w_3 and the SAME positive a.

Using (15) on the final join and averaging the rows j=4,6 with the
positive weights (1+xi)/(2+xi),1/(2+xi), the result is

    [(1+xi)Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
      =r_3 theta k_0-1 =lambda A_0 k_0-1
      =lambda xi(2+xi)/D-1.

The final factorization is

    lambda xi(2+xi)-D=(xi-theta)(s xi+lambda).           (29)

For xi>theta this proves (3) strictly, contradicting the required two
nonpositive original Q rows. The entire fixed-seed repeated operation
is therefore infeasible at length 63 for positive defect, regardless of
all transverse choices at all depths. This conclusion is conditional
only on the precisely specified isometric copies, bridge, projection
values, and absence of lifts/other changes. It is not a no-go theorem for
other children or for the unrestricted worst-case problem.

At xi=theta the right side of (3) is zero, so this argument does NOT
exclude arbitrary earlier couplings at length 63 in that limiting case.
For completeness, the same retained average at a later top bridge r_j
would require lambda^(j-2)q<=1. At j=4 this fails even when xi=theta,
since lambda^2 q=lambda>1. This is a textual statement about the recursive
rule, not a numerical evaluation above the assigned horizon cap.

Function values are rigid on these data. To prove this without assuming
the projection model as a restriction, give a seed arbitrary values
f_i=f_i^proj+delta_i, with f_star=0 and g_star=x_star=0, and require all
original Q inequalities. At sample 3, x_3=g_3 and y_3=0. Its two optimum
inequalities read delta_3<=0 and -delta_3<=0, so delta_3=0. Since g_2=g_3,
the two original rows (2,3),(3,2) both had zero projection residual and
now force delta_2=delta_3=0. The rows (2,1) and (1,3) also had zero
projection residual: the first is <V-U,Hxi U+HV>=0, and the second uses
y_3=0. They force delta_2<=delta_1<=delta_3, hence delta_1=0. Finally
g_0=g_1 makes both rows (0,1),(1,0) zero before the value perturbation,
and therefore delta_0=delta_1=0. Every step uses original Q rows.

An isometry followed by a positive homothety preserves these zero
residuals and the property x_3=g_3. Every queried sample in the recursive
construction belongs to a four-sample seed copy, since joins add only the
bridge step between existing endpoints, without adding another sample
type. Apply the preceding eight-row argument to each copy together with
the COMMON optimum sample 0. All its delta_i vanish. Thus every function
value is forced, even when arbitrary cross maps were used in building
the child spaces. Equations (2)-(3) exclude every convex differentiable
1-smooth interpolant of these positions and gradients having a minimizer
at 0; merely changing its values or its representation does not escape.
This does not address a changed optimum sample or altered vector data.

## 6. What signed state must be retained, and why this operation stops

An exact reusable state for arbitrary transverse joins may keep the full
Gram of (x_0,w,g_0,...,g_ell,y_0,...,y_ell), the word, and the energies
e_i. These data determine the projected transverse Gram blocks, (13),
and (11), including every nullspace. Four scalar extrema alone do not
parameterize an arbitrary transverse feasibility problem.

There is a small necessary signed part of that state which already
separates the present family. If a child has a residual y_i=a_pos*w and
g_i=w, a_pos>0, (15) forces

    c_R,j>=-1/r for EVERY right gradient j.             (30)

Thus the left endpoint-facing residual must be paired with the entire
signed list of right initial/gradient correlations. It cannot be deleted
as an irrelevant transverse direction. Any retained convex combination
of these c_R,j must obey the same bound. The positive combination (28)
forces r theta k_0<=1, which contradicts the actual fourth bridge for a
positive-defect seed. This invariant survives arbitrary transverse
rotations and contractions at all earlier levels.

A broader finite-depth obstruction uses only a few seed features. Consider
ANY nonnegative seed word p of capacity A_0=1+sum p_i with an actual
projection core, initial norm R>0, endpoint w, and
k_0=<x_0,w>/R^2>0. Suppose one of its samples has g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w with gamma,delta>0. Use the same unlifted isometric copying
operation with balanced bridges r_j=theta lambda^j A_0, allowing every
transverse map at every join. At the first join the GD identity gives

    sum p_i g_right,i + g_right,terminal = -r_0 w.

The nonnegative weights sum to A_0. Their weighted average of the signed
initial/gradient correlations is therefore -r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.
Embedding this whole first join always into the left child preserves
those correlations. Separately, embedding the displayed endpoint-facing
sample always into the right child preserves g_i=gamma*w and
y_i=delta*w relative to the current endpoint. At a later top join (15),
with the factor gamma retained, requires every right correlation to be
at least -gamma/r_j. Averaging them forces

    r_j theta k_0 <= gamma.                             (32)

Because r_j grows as lambda^j and k_0>0, this inequality eventually fails.
Thus this larger class of FIXED seeds with positive initial/endpoint
pairing and one such residual cannot be iterated indefinitely solely by
choosing transverse correlations. The statement uses projection values;
the independent value-rigidity argument in section 5 covers arbitrary
value selection for the specific orthogonal seeds of the main claim.
It does not cover seeds without the displayed positive pairing or
endpoint-facing sample, depth-dependent changes of seed data, or lifts.

For a general feasible join, the signed boundary pairings transport as

    <g_left,W>=alpha B_C(g_L,w_R),
    <y_left,W>=alpha B_C(y_L,w_R),
    <g_right,W>=alpha^2<g_R,w_R>,
    <y_right,W>=alpha^2<y_R,w_R>,
    <x_parent,0,g_left>=<x_L,0,g_L>,
    <x_parent,0,y_left>=<x_L,0,y_L>,
    <x_parent,0,g_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,g_R),
    <x_parent,0,y_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,y_R).            (31)

The cross entries of the full retained Gram are (9), with factor alpha
when exactly one vector comes from the right child. Internal right
entries have factor alpha^2. Equations (9)-(16),(31) give an exact full
coupling rule at any growing horizon WHEN feasible. They assert no
iteration theorem without proving feasibility at every step. Here the
concrete obstruction (3) decides it: arbitrary transverse freedom alone
does not make this seed family iterable.

## 7. Discovery ledger, calibration, and evidence preservation

The three recorded optimization evaluations all used t=1/100 and the
same explicit seed (5). The smaller calibration was h[2] of length 15,
whose legality is proved in section 3. Both h[4] cases used the SAME
actual h[3] children and the SAME h[4] word.

1. Length 15, C fixed to zero: numerical normalized residual objective
   8.7710378984e-11, maximum original Q about 8.33e-17.
2. Length 63, C fixed to zero: objective 0.4164822178621603;
   Q_(32,25) approximately 0.01256893369169749.
3. Length 63, full unrestricted 8 by 8 C: objective
   0.41648221754700987; largest dual weights are the duplicate original
   rows (36,30),(37,30). The contraction block is strictly PSD in this
   numerical minimax-residual solution, with smallest eigenvalue about
   0.597687. It is NOT a feasible interpolant: the positive residuals
   were neither discarded nor repaired.

The common exact predicted normalized row residual is lambda T(xi)-1,
approximately 0.416482217535. Discovery used 3 of 8 allowed optimization
evaluations and 18.623366069994517 measured build-plus-solve seconds of
60, including imports, model construction, and solver compilation. There
were 2 distinct seed/parent cases out of the allowed 4. Each attempt was
logged BEFORE evaluation and updated with actual cost afterward.

All 4,225 rows of the 63-step ordered-pair/optimum/diagonal matrix were
kept, along with full contraction PSD. The positive row scales alter only
the residual objective, not the feasibility set. Raw child coordinates,
all words, orthonormal bases, all original Q values, the entire C, all Q
dual entries, the PSD dual, the scalar dual, and complete contraction
eigenvalues are saved. No eigenvalue was clipped, no PSD repair applied,
and no nullspace deleted. Source and raw-file SHA256 hashes are recorded
in the public ledger. The executable source is
workers/max/research/max_general_transverse_discovery.py; raw files are
workers/max/research/transverse_raw/attempt_01 through attempt_03 (.npz
and .json). The ledger is research/max_general_transverse_discovery_budget.json.
The separate tiny exact audit max_transverse_exact_audit.py passed 19
checks in Q(sqrt(2)), including the seed formulas, both separator signs,
the factorization (29), and the two polynomial identities used in the
legality proof. It makes no optimization or verification call. A read-only
inspection of the saved arrays gave the identical invariant Q_(36,30)
in both length-63 runs and the two-row residual approximately
0.00256492296668. These are diagnostic consistency checks, not acceptance
by the proof verifier.

The derivations in sections 1-6 use no numerical output as a premise.
No installation, verifier call, additional solver replay, external
publication, or subagent was used. No allowance renews. This document is
an unverified proof candidate, and the project's original target remains
unresolved.

## 8. Scope test: a changed actual seed delays this particular separator

This is a separate construction on the SAME three-step word (5), not a
replacement of the fixed children in the preceding discovery comparisons.
It sharpens the scope of the result; it is NOT a legal 63-step repair.
There was no optimization evaluation on this changed seed.

Keep the exact a,b of (5), and set

    rho=599/600, Klen=a+rho, z=b-1, m=a+b,
    eta=z/Klen, Rnew^2=m^2+Klen*z, A=1+2a+b.

Take orthogonal U,V with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=eta, and define

    (x_0,g_0)=(mU+Klen V,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+Klen V,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(Klen V,V), (x_3,g_3)=(rho V,rho V).      (33)

The residuals are y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V, y_1=zU+Klen V,
y_2=(Klen-1)V, y_3=0. The three GD updates have steps a,b,a.
Here Klen>1 and 0<rho<1. At samples 0 and 1 the normals against V are
-a and 0, and the normals against 0 are -(m-1),-z. Normals against rho V
are no larger than those against V because all the V/residual pairings
are nonnegative. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 equal
-(Klen-1)eta, and the normal against rho V equals
-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta. At sample 3 all residuals vanish. Normals against
each sample's own gradient vanish. This checks every distinct vertex of
conv(0,U,V,rho V). Section 2 supplies the actual common function, values,
and TRUE distance Rnew, since x_0=rho V+(a+b)U+aV is a nonnegative
combination of the actual gradients.

This seed still has positive defect, with

    kappa_new=A^2 rho^2 eta/Rnew^2 >1+1/2000.            (34)

It also has an explicit legal first transverse join and an optimal signed
initial/gradient minimum at that join. Take r_0=theta A and use transverse
bases U on the left and
(Klen sqrt(eta)/Rnew, -m/Rnew) on the right in the orthonormal coordinates
(U,V/sqrt(eta)). The latter vector is perpendicular to x_0/Rnew and has
norm one. Let the scalar cross map be c in [0,1]. The scaled physical
cross matrix on the pair (U,V) is exactly

    r_0 rho eta/Rnew^2 * [[Klen*c, -m*c],[-m,-z]].       (35)

This follows directly from (9) with w=rho V and
alpha=r_0 rho sqrt(eta)/Rnew. It is realized by (10), with scalar
S_C=sqrt(1-c^2), so no PSD correction is implicit.

All cross gradient/residual inner products in BOTH directions are
nonpositive. To check the only possibly positive terms, remove the
positive prefactor in (35). Pair a right U with the three nonzero left
residuals; the results are

    Klen[(m-1)c-m], Klen[zc-m], -m(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c<=1 and m-z=a+1>0. Pair a left U with
the three nonzero right residuals; the results are

    -Klen*c, -Klen*(a+1)*c, -m*c*(Klen-1),

which are nonpositive because c>=0. Every remaining pair uses the V row
or column of (35), whose entries are nonpositive, with nonnegative
residual coefficients; rho V contributes an additional positive factor.
The fourth residuals are zero. Subtracting the nonnegative energies in
(13) checks every cross Q. Thus this is an actual legal seven-step join
for EVERY c in [0,1], with true distance Rnew and ratio kappa_new^2.

The retained signed correlations of its right U,V gradients are

    c_U=r_0 rho*m*z*(c-1)/Rnew^4,
    c_V=-r_0 rho*eta*(m^2 c+Klen*z)/Rnew^4.

Choose

    c_opt=(a+1)Klen/[m(m+Klen)] in (0,1).               (36)

Then c_U=c_V=-r_0 k_new/(m+Klen), where k_new=rho*z/Rnew^2.
The right rho V gradient has correlation rho*c_V and is less negative;
the left gradients have positive initial correlations. Equation
m*g_right,U+Klen*g_right,V=-r_0 w proves that their weighted average
has this value for ANY transverse coupling, so (36) maximizes the
minimum signed initial/gradient correlation over every allowed coupling
at the first join. It is an exact argument, not a numerical optimizer.

The last queried gradient in (33) is w/rho, rather than w. Thus the
endpoint-facing row threshold (30) becomes c_R,j>=-1/(rho*r). The
positive weighted combination inherited from these two first-join
gradients would, at the fourth balanced bridge r_3=theta lambda^3 A,
have normalized residual Charge_new-1, where

    Charge_new=r_3 rho*r_0 k_new/(m+Klen)<1-1/4000.     (37)

Consequently that inherited two-row certificate is strictly NEGATIVE
for this changed actual seed; it cannot exclude the 63-step parent.
Neither existence of legal intervening 15-,31-step general-coupling
children nor feasibility of its 63-step parent is asserted here.

For a mechanically checkable sign audit of (34),(37), their exact
expressions in Q(sqrt(2)) are

    kappa_new-1 =
      [-19019047380837966731+13474265781546157318*s]
          /40842989603870102500,

    1-Charge_new =
      [341554841617983701-241357907739927883*s]
          /603687956995287500.

For the first margin, write the displayed positive integers in its
numerator and denominator as P1,Q1,D1, so the numerator is -P1+Q1*s.
Then

    2(2000 Q1)^2-(2000 P1+D1)^2
       =2441206548123238434623351342332719331750000>0.

Both compared quantities are positive, so this proves (34). For the
second margin write its numerator as P2-Q2*s and its denominator as D2.
The quantity 4000 P2-D2 is positive, and

    (4000 P2-D2)^2-2(4000 Q2)^2
       =789713290761361657120990345679724250000>0.

This proves (37). The seven exact sign/formula checks are recorded in
max_transverse_changed_seed_scope_audit.json. This is trivial scalar
arithmetic, not a parameter sweep, conic evaluation, or verification call.

Finally, this example does NOT evade (32): it still has k_new>0 and the
endpoint-facing constants gamma=1/rho and delta=(Klen-1)/rho>0. Fixed
copies therefore fail at some later depth under arbitrary transverse
coupling. The example demonstrates a real scope distinction between the
specific 63-step separator and the broader eventual obstruction, and
identifies why changing only this scalar terminal ratio is insufficient
for an all-depth construction.

# Finite multiplicative phase covers for actual full-seed insertion

**Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof draft.** Worker max4, assigned analytical
round 51. No mathematical evaluator, solver, DP, replay, machine algebra,
formal verification, consult, or subagent was used. All new assertions below
belong to this single proof candidate; none is an accepted intermediate fact.

The mathematical predecessors are accepted facts `04219554e41d9750`
(especially proof Sections 3, 7, 9--12) and, only when explicitly recovering
the earlier fixed-ray comparison, `4c92d3fc4eb6153e`. No unverified finding
or external exponent theorem is a premise. The construction uses arbitrary
full primitive seeds inside BASIC dominant trees. It does not require or
prove arbitrary-template inflation.

## 1. Original model and complete seed hypothesis

For a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative schedule
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), define W_n(h) to be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite-dimensional Euclidean space, every convex differentiable
function f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star,
and every x_0!=x_star, under

    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).

Here L>0 is fixed but arbitrary. This is the original W=2R normalization;
the empty schedule is included.

For nonnegative a,d set

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain all original integer splits:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),      n>=1.

Write U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, lambda=1+sqrt(2), and rho=log_2(lambda).

Fix an integer r>=1. A seed in this draft means an ACTUAL word
h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with every h_i>=0, sum s, capacity A=1+s,
and m=r+1, together with a full real (r+1)-by-(r+1) array Lambda.
For EVERY ordered distinct pair i,j in {0,...,r}, require

    Lambda_ij>=0,     Lambda_ii=0.

Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1, and define

    T_ii=1,  T_it=h_t for t<i,  T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

The full original-coordinate certificate requirements are

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).       (1)

Transpose and positive semidefiniteness have their ordinary real-matrix
meanings. There are exactly r(r+1) off-diagonal multipliers. Every formal
gradient g_0,...,g_r remains, even when physical steps vanish. No gradient
equality or coordinate deletion is allowed at a zero step. There is no
assumption h_i>=1, product balance, zero remainder, rank, support, or
symmetry. These are precisely the general-length sufficient hypotheses
in accepted `04219554e41d9750`, proof Section 10.

For clarity about their scope, that accepted result normalizes L=1 and
uses x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
        +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Its exact identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Actual smooth convex trajectories have Q_ij<=0. Thus (1) implies the
primitive inequality on the full function class. Here Gram is the matrix
of pairwise inner products, and the matrix pairing is trace(M^T Gram).
This accepted implication is only sufficiency; no attainment or equivalence
of certificate classes is assumed.

## 2. Accepted ray and physical-substitution inputs

The following are precise statements used from `04219554e41d9750`.
For every positive integer b, the limit

    Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1)

exists and is finite and positive. For every integer j>=0 put

    L_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    U_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2,

    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j)
           +(3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2)
           +(9/10000)lambda^(-j).

The accepted enclosures and their proof give

    L_pc(b,j)<=Z_b<=U_pc(b,j),
    0<=U_pc(b,j)-L_pc(b,j)<=E(b,j).                         (2)

Indeed the latter is exactly the bound obtained by subtracting the two
endpoints in accepted Section 9: use the accepted adjacent bound for
B_(b*2^j)-B_(b*2^j-1) and B_n<=4C_n<=4(n+1)^rho. In particular both
endpoints tend to Z_b. For every fixed integer d>=0 and defined index,

    0<=Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)
       <=E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)d b^(rho-1)2^(-j).                  (3)

The same accepted proof gives

    1<rho<4/3,
    B_n>=C_n,
    C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1),
    C_(b-1)>=1.                                           (4)

For every nonnegative n, take any fully expanded maximizing tree of the
ORIGINAL B_n recurrence, expanding every primitive child using the
ORIGINAL C recurrence. Such a tree has n internal nodes, n primitive
empty leaves, and one terminal dominant empty leaf. In particular,
there is no restriction on a maximizing dominant split.

Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual seed of Section 1.
Retain all internal nodes and keep the dominant empty leaf empty with
mass 1. Recompute all physical bridges:

* At a primitive join, for actual child capacities A_left,A_right, use
  bridge 1+d_pp, where d_pp>0 solves
  d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left A_right.
* At a dominant join, for primitive left capacity A_left and actual
  dominant right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), use bridge
  1+A_left/zeta, where zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

Concatenate (left,bridge,right) at every node. Denote the resulting word
by Hsub_n, and its ACTUAL mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n. Accepted
Sections 11--12 apply for every seed satisfying (1), including arbitrary
PSD remainders and zero steps. They give

    length(Hsub_n)=mn,
    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2<=Dsub_n<=A B_n.             (5)

The upper objective certificate holds for all functions in Section 1;
its matching Huber example gives the displayed equality. In particular
this is not merely a quadratic or projection-family assertion.

The length in (5) counts n old bridge steps and nr seed steps. The lower
mass bound pays for the one untouched dominant leaf: all primitive
capacities scale by A, while the square-root dominant merge is
1-Lipschitz in the square root of the right mass. This is why the
subtraction sqrt(A)-1 is present. We never physically replace the
dominant empty leaf by an object of mass A.

## 3. Integer-ray monotonicity and exact dyadic scaling

The admissible split k=0 in B_(n+1) gives

    B_(n+1)>=G(1,B_n)>B_n,

because B_n>0 and sqrt(B_n^2+8B_n)>B_n. Thus B is increasing directly
from the ORIGINAL recurrence. If positive integers b<=c, then

    B_(b*2^j-1)<=B_(c*2^j-1)

for every j. Taking the established limits proves Z_b<=Z_c. We do not
need or assert strict increase of the ray limits.

For every integer ell>=0, changing the limiting index gives

    Z_(2^ell b)
      =lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^(j+ell)-1)
      =lambda^ell Z_b.                                    (6)

This uses the accepted integer-ray limits only. No continuity, density,
or regularity of a real-valued phase function has been assumed.

## 4. The finite product and its strict margin

Choose positive integers b0,d,K and an integer ell>=0. Let C_pc be a
positive certified upper bound for Z_d/Z_b0. Suppose

    m^K b0<=2^ell d.

Define, for 0<=i<=K,

    b_i=b0 m^i,
    P_pc=lambda^ell C_pc.

All quantities Z_b are positive. Exact telescoping, monotonicity, and
(6), in that order, prove

    product_(i=0)^(K-1) [Z_(m b_i)/Z_b_i]
      =Z_(m^K b0)/Z_b0
      <=Z_(2^ell d)/Z_b0
      =lambda^ell Z_d/Z_b0
      <=P_pc.                                             (7)

Every inequality has the direction required for an UPPER product bound.
In particular this argument never turns an upper ray enclosure into a
lower bound for an unknown ratio.

Also m^K b0>=b0, so monotonicity makes the telescoped ratio at least 1.
Thus P_pc>=1, and the strict capacity condition A^K>P_pc implies A>1.

It is convenient to allow a certified capacity floor a with 1<a<=A.
Assume a^K>P_pc and choose a positive gamma satisfying

    K gamma<=1-P_pc/a^K.                                  (8)

Necessarily gamma<1. Write

    q_i(a)=Z_(m b_i)/(a Z_b_i).

The product of the q_i(a) is at most t_pc=P_pc/a^K<1. Therefore some
index i_star in {0,...,K-1} satisfies q_i_star(a)<=t_pc^(1/K).
Otherwise their product would exceed t_pc.

For completeness, for x in [0,1] the inequality
(1-x)^K>=1-Kx follows by induction: multiplying the induction bound by
1-x adds the nonnegative term K x^2. With x=gamma, (8) gives

    (1-gamma)^K>=1-K gamma>=t_pc.

Consequently there is a fixed index i_star with

    Z_(m b_i_star)/(A Z_b_i_star)
      <=q_i_star(a)<=1-gamma.                             (9)

This is an existence assertion about a finite list, not a selection of
an unproved optimal phase. Sections 5--6 make it a physical and certified
statement.

## 5. Physical families, exact offsets, and uniform limit passage

For each i in {0,...,K-1} and integer j>=0, put

    n_(i,j)=b_i 2^j-1,
    N_(i,j)=m n_(i,j)=m b_i 2^j-m,
    H_(i,j)=Hsub_(n_(i,j)).

Thus there are K explicitly defined schedule families. At a fixed j
there are exactly K specified finite physical words, possibly with
different lengths. Each word is generated by the procedure in Section 2,
for example with the least maximizing split used to resolve ties.

The numerator is evaluated at its TRUE physical horizon. Equation (3)
applies with ray m b_i and shift d=m-1=r, since

    N_(i,j)=m b_i 2^j-1-(m-1).

It proves lambda^(-j)B_(N_(i,j))->Z_(m b_i). Also (5) yields

    0<=A Z_b_i-lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_(i,j))
      <=A E(b_i,j)
        +2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A Z_b_i)lambda^(-j/2).           (10)

To see the second term, subtract the square in (5) from A B_n,
discard the negative term -(sqrt(A)-1)^2, and use
B_(n_(i,j))<=lambda^j Z_b_i. Thus the denominator converges to
A Z_b_i>0. By (5),

    lim_j B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
        =Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i).                             (11)

There are only K indices. In (3) use the maximum ray m b_(K-1) and
the fixed shift m-1; in (10) use b_(K-1) and Z_b_(K-1). These give
common errors tending to zero, while every denominator limit is at least
A because Z_b>=L_pc(b,0)=B_(b-1)>=1. Therefore (11) is uniform over
the finite list and over all maximizing-tree ties. No interchange of
an infinite phase minimum with a limit is involved.

For any one fixed selected i, the integers N_(i,j) strictly increase
and tend to infinity with j. The words at different horizons need not
be prefixes of one another; the original prescribed-horizon guarantee
does not require that property.

## 6. Certified phase selection and an explicit common horizon

At a search depth t>=0 compute, or supply certified enclosures for,
the K quantities

    F_i(t)=U_pc(m b_i,t)/(a L_pc(b_i,t)).                   (12)

They are valid upper bounds on q_i(a): the numerator is bounded from
above and the positive denominator from below. Both endpoints in (2)
converge, so F_i(t)->q_i(a). Choose the first t for which some

    F_i(t)<1-gamma/2,                                    (13)

and freeze the smallest such i. This rule terminates: the index supplied
by (9) has limit at most 1-gamma, leaving the strict slack gamma/2.
It is unnecessary to decide which index minimizes the true ray ratio.

This is a certified convergent procedure, not a prescription to use
uncertified floating-point comparisons. For rational a and gamma, it has
the following finite-precision implementation in principle. At stage
t>=1, form outward rational enclosures of each L_pc(b_i,t) and
U_pc(m b_i,t) with absolute error at most 2^(-t), keeping every lower
denominator positive. Use the lower L endpoint and upper U endpoint
in (12), and stop only upon the strict rational inequality (13).
Each finite B value is defined by finitely many additions, products,
positive square roots, and maxima in the ORIGINAL recurrence. These
operations admit arbitrarily accurate rational outward enclosures:
rational square comparisons bracket each positive square root, and the
maximum of lower or upper endpoints bounds the corresponding maximum.
For a fixed stage only finitely many such operations are needed. The
recurrences have positive inputs, so arbitrary prescribed absolute
accuracy follows by refinement. The extra errors tend to zero with t,
and termination follows from the same strict slack. There is no claim
that this procedure is computationally economical, and it was not run.

More generally, an arbitrary real seed need not come with a computable
capacity oracle. The effective input is a certified rational floor
1<a<=A with a^K>P_pc, and a certified positive rational gamma satisfying
(8). If A^K>P_pc, such choices exist by strictness and rational density;
their certification uses the supplied seed data. This states the actual
input required for effective selection rather than presuming an oracle
for arbitrary real numbers. The exact-real formulation (12)--(13) is
also a valid mathematical selection rule.

It remains to turn the selected ratio into a bound for every sufficiently
large physical horizon. Put b_max=b_(K-1) and

    J=ceil(log(60 b_max/gamma)/log(3/2)).                  (14)

This can equivalently be selected by the finite integer test
(3/2)^J>=60 b_max/gamma, with rational arithmetic when gamma is rational.
Its finiteness follows from 3/2>1.

Here is a common error bound with no unproved rates. Since
sqrt(2)>5/4, lambda>9/4 and sqrt(lambda)>3/2. Also sqrt(2)<3/2,
and (4) gives rho-1<1 and rho/2<1. For b>=1,
j>=0, and q=(2/3)^j,

    E(b,j)+2lambda^(-j/2)
       <[12b+(3/25)(b+1)+9/10000+2]q
       <=[12+6/25+9/10000+2]b q
       <15b q.                                          (15)

Every inequality uses nonnegative terms; 2^(-j)<=q and
lambda^(-j)<=q as well. Thus for all i and all j>=J,

    E(b_i,j)+2u_j<gamma/4,   u_j=lambda^(-j/2).            (16)

By (2) and Z_b_i>=1,

    B_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j Z_b_i(1-E(b_i,j)).

Also (4) implies B_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j, so

    (1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_(n_(i,j)))<=u_j.

Using (5), monotonicity, and the LOWER ray enclosure for the larger ray,

    B_(N_(i,j))<=B_(m b_i 2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(m b_i),

we get, whenever (16) holds,

    B_(N_(i,j))W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
       <=[Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i)]
          /[(1-E(b_i,j))(1-u_j)^2].                      (17)

The factors in the denominator are positive by (16). For 0<=E,u<1,

    (1-E)(1-u)^2
       =1-E-2u+2Eu+(1-E)u^2>=1-E-2u.

Hence that denominator is greater than 1-gamma/4. For the fixed index
selected by (13), Z_(m b_i)/(A Z_b_i)<=q_i(a)<1-gamma/2, so

    B_(N_(i,j))W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
       <(1-gamma/2)/(1-gamma/4)
       <1-gamma/4,            EVERY j>=J.                (18)

The last strict inequality follows from
(1-gamma/4)^2-(1-gamma/2)=gamma^2/16>0. Thus the explicit positive
relative separation is gamma/4, and the chosen phase is fixed before
the unbounded horizon sequence is used. The family and selection depend
only on the seed and benchmark, never on an objective or its trajectory.

## 7. The exponent interface follows from a finite integer cover

Take b0=d=1 and C_pc=1. Then (7) becomes precisely

    product_(i=0)^(K-1) Z_(m^(i+1))/Z_(m^i)<=lambda^ell
       whenever m^K<=2^ell.                              (19)

Thus A^K>lambda^ell is sufficient. This finite condition for some
integers K>=1,ell>=0 is equivalent to A>m^rho. In one direction,
lambda^ell=(2^ell)^rho>=m^(K rho), so the strict power inequality implies
A>m^rho. Conversely, if A>m^rho, choose an integer K with

    K log(A/m^rho)>log(lambda)

and set ell=ceil(K log_2(m)). Then m^K<=2^ell<2m^K, and

    lambda^ell<lambda m^(K rho)<A^K.                     (20)

Here rho>0 and lambda=2^rho justify raising inequalities to rho.
If desired first choose a rational a with m^rho<a<A and perform the
same argument with a; Sections 4--6 then apply with certified inputs.
This proves sufficiency of A>(r+1)^rho without a claim about an
exponent obstruction or capacity ceiling for arbitrary seeds.

For an accepted upper comparison Z_d/Z_b0<=C_pc, the stronger finite
interface available from (7) is

    A>(lambda^ell C_pc)^(1/K),
    m^K b0<=2^ell d.                                    (21)

The word "stronger" refers to using this additional certified comparison;
it is not an assertion that every choice in (21) beats (19), or that
its value is below m^rho. For instance, at K=1,ell=0, b0=3 and d=3m,
the accepted constants in `4c92d3fc4eb6153e` recover its sufficient
capacity hypotheses for r=5 and r=6. Its more specialized horizon and
margin estimates remain valid in their stated scope.

## 8. Exact rational examples, with no evaluator

All checks here are written as explicit integer equalities or positive
integer differences. They are mathematical parts of the joint proof,
not reports of computations executed by a program.

First record small power identities:

    lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2),
    lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
    lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2).

The last conjugate is lambda^(-10)>0, so

    lambda^10<lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726<6750.            (22)

Also sqrt(2)<17/12 and sqrt(2)<99/70, because respectively
289>288 and 9801>9800. Hence

    lambda<29/12,  lambda<169/70,  lambda^4<34.            (23)

### 8.1 Length five: capacity 49/5 and seventeen phases

Let m=6, a=49/5, K=17, ell=44, b0=d=1, C_pc=1. The length cover is

    6^17<=2^44
      iff 3^17<=2^27,
    3^17=129140163<134217728=2^27.                        (24)

The capacity power bound follows from small factored checks. First

    49^4-9220*5^4=5764801-5762500=2301>0,

so a^4>9220. Next

    922^2=850084,  675^2=455625,
    200*850084-373*455625
      =170016800-169948125=68675>0,
    49*373^2-170*200^2
      =6817321-6800000=17321>0.                          (25)

Consequently

    lambda^44/a^17
       <[34*6750^4]/[(49/5)*9220^4]
       =170*675^4/(49*922^4)
       <6800000/6817321<1.                              (26)

This proves the needed exact finite power inequality; no decimal
approximation to rho is used. More quantitatively,

    17321*10000=173210000>115894457=17*6817321,

so (8) holds with gamma=1/10000, even at the capacity floor a=49/5.
Every actual full seed with r=5 and A>=49/5 therefore has a phase in

    b_i=6^i,             0<=i<=16,
    N_(i,j)=6^(i+1)2^j-6,

which the certified procedure selects. With

    J5=ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)),

its physical words satisfy, for EVERY j>=J5,

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/40000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.     (27)

The capacity 49/5 is below 99/10 by exactly 1/10.

### 8.2 Length six: capacity 12 and eleven phases

Let m=7, a=12, K=11, ell=31, b0=d=1, C_pc=1. The exact length cover is

    7^11=1977326743<2147483648=2^31.                     (28)

Use (22)--(23) and the equality 6750=(125/32)12^3. Then

    lambda^31/12^11
       <6750^3*(169/70)/12^11
       =125^3*169/(32^3*70*12^2)
       =330078125/330301440<1,                          (29)

since the denominator minus the numerator is 223315. Furthermore

    223315*20000=4466300000>3633315840=11*330301440.

Thus (8) holds with gamma=1/20000. Every actual full seed with r=6
and A>=12 has a certified selectable phase in

    b_i=7^i,             0<=i<=10,
    N_(i,j)=7^(i+1)2^j-7.

With J6=ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)), for EVERY j>=J6 it gives

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/80000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.     (30)

The capacity 12 is below 1201/100 by exactly 1/100.

### 8.3 An accepted comparison shortens the length-five list

For an additional finite illustration, take m=6, a=197/20, K=9,
ell=21, b0=3, d=15, and C_pc=19299/2500. The accepted comparison in
`04219554e41d9750` is Z_15/Z_3<C_pc. The cover reduces to

    6^9*3<=2^21*15
      iff 3^9<=5*2^12,
    19683<20480.                                        (31)

The integer checks

    197^3-950*20^3=7645373-7600000=45373>0,
    4*19299=77196<77500=31*2500

give a^3>950 and C_pc<31/4. With (22)--(23),

    lambda^21*C_pc/a^9
      <[6750^2*(29/12)*(31/4)]/950^3
       =899*135^2/(48*50*19^3)
       =16384275/16461600
       =218457/219488<1.                               (32)

Here 135^2=18225, 19^3=6859, and
16461600-16384275=77325. Moreover

    1031*2000=2062000>1975392=9*219488.

Thus gamma=1/2000 satisfies (8). There are now only nine phases,

    b_i=3*6^i,          0<=i<=8,
    N_(i,j)=18*6^i*2^j-6.

With J5a=ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)), the selected phase gives
for EVERY j>=J5a the relative separation 1/8000. The required capacity
197/20 is below 99/10 by 1/20, but is above the smaller 49/5 capacity
in Section 8.1. These are different finite tradeoffs, not an ordering
of their unknown best true phases.

For this SAME K=9, the least unanchored dyadic exponent is 24, since

    2^23=8388608<10077696=6^9<16777216=2^24.

The product bound lambda^21*C_pc is strictly smaller than lambda^24:
C_pc<31/4<14<lambda^3, where lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>14 follows from
sqrt(2)>7/5. This is a proved improvement over the unanchored finite
bound at this fixed K. It asserts no optimality over all finite covers.

## 9. Complete conditional theorem proved by the joint argument

Fix the original function class and ALL-split benchmark from Section 1.
Suppose an actual length-r seed, r>=1, satisfies EVERY condition (1),
with h>=0 and no coordinate identifications, and put m=r+1, A=1+sum h.
Let b0,d,K be positive integers and ell>=0 an integer. Let C_pc>0 be
a proved upper bound for Z_d/Z_b0 and assume

    m^K b0<=2^ell d,    A^K>lambda^ell C_pc.

Choose any certified floor 1<a<=A and gamma>0 satisfying

    a^K>lambda^ell C_pc,
    K gamma<=1-lambda^ell C_pc/a^K.

Such real choices exist, and rational choices exist under the same strict
inequality, as explained in Section 6. Define the K physical families
H_(i,j), the exact horizons N_(i,j)=m b0 m^i 2^j-m, and J by (14).
Then the certified convergent rule (12)--(13), or its rational outward
enclosure version, selects a FIXED i in {0,...,K-1}, terminates after
finitely many stages, and for every integer j>=J gives

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-gamma/4) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.

The horizons form an unbounded increasing integer sequence. The full
all-function upper certificates and Huber equalities are supplied by the
actual basic-tree construction (5); every limiting comparison and finite
bound uses these actual horizons. The family is finite in its phase
index, and the error estimates are uniform over that finite index and
all maximizing-tree ties.

In particular A>(r+1)^rho is a sufficient interface, witnessed by some
finite K and ell. The exact separate sufficient capacities A>=49/5 for
r=5 and A>=12 for r=6, with the phase lists, J values, and relative
margins (27) and (30), follow. A>=197/20 for r=5 has the shorter nine-phase
alternative in Section 8.3. None of these hypotheses supplies an actual
seed or its full multiplier array.

## 10. Scope and audit boundaries

This is a conditional family of prescribed-horizon, full-class objective
guarantees. It is not an anytime schedule or an all-horizon comparison.
The selected phase is not identified numerically in this round, and no
claim of a phase optimum is made. Its certified finite selection is
proved above but has not been executed. No seed was discovered, and no
new fact was submitted or accepted.

The proof requires neither a virtual smooth-convex oracle nor inflation
by a nonbasic template. It makes no inference from the Ye--Liu exponent
theorem about an arbitrary finite seed's capacity. The fixed-b3 fact
`4c92d3fc4eb6153e` remains valid with its original, sharper specialized
finite horizons. All improvements here concern sufficient interfaces
using potentially different phases.

In particular K counts the compared integer rays. The expression A^K
comes from multiplying K normalized ray ratios; it does not claim that
an arbitrary seed can be inflated through itself K times or that a new
physical primitive seed of capacity A^K has been constructed.

The original unrestricted exact-profile Goal remains OPEN. This joint
draft requires a separately selected independent audit and formal gate
before any of its new mathematical claims can be used as facts.

# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or
fact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified
findings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the
definitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted
fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not
upper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.

## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b

Fix an integer m>=2. Within this document write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

The parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not
recomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the
conjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.
Counting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,

    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),
    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)

There are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at
curvature t in [0,1] is exactly

    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)

Let p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then

    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,
    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,
    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)

The exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions
is

    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)

Indeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity
makes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar
quantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have
zero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change
neither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.

For an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its
distinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open
interval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is

    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),
    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2
                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)

Consequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive
roots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the
maximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.
Coincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations
(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.

The unit-curvature values alone are

    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)

They are not used as a replacement for (4).

## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b

For a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted
cut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule
mass S, its reciprocal denominator is

    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any
closed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a
given level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.

For h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last
preceding masses are, respectively,

    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)

Their following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting
the original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are

    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)

with following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences
in (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes

    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)

Taking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and
1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can
be larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final
cut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever

    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)

The global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever
b<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use
the actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.

## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step

For the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so
the actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,
    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,
    ac>=127/128>7/8,
    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)

For example lambda<17/7 gives
c<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.
The upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].
Consequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives

    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,
    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)

Let M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its
following mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most
r^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least
S_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing
for x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most

    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)

These statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the
first and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,

    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.

Thus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:

    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)

## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0

This argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write
v_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,

    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.

If no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are
nonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives

    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)

The last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual
restricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with
d_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are
nonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give

    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,
    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)

Here the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the
factor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)

The bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion
holds directly as well. Therefore

    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]
                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)

using t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum
estimate

    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),
                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)

Both entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives
(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,

    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))
       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]
       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)

For the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3
and c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,

    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)

## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant

Let rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,

and B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly
from these recursions that

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)

Put F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives

    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.

The strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of
(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives
F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and
D=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or
D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.

Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive
recursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,
induction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,
gives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows
by integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.

In our family (1) implies

    M_m/lambda^m
       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)

since A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound

    K_n(H_m) B_n
      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)

has limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n
at every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal
value for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut
baseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.

## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range

Here is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j
of an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly
between i and j, T the mass after j, and define

    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,
    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.

Assume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle

    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)

Use the retained prefix gradients and proximal point

    p=(1,0)/M_0,
    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)

These prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of
J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint
the proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is
active. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X. In
the second block, the proposed proximal points X-tq, 1<=t<=M_1, have

    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0,
    <q-p,X-tq>
      =A kappa/M_1+A/M_0
          -t[A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1)]
      >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.

This verifies every actual old query, even if individual ordinary steps
are large. At the second checkpoint its jet is

    x_j=y+q, g_j=q, f_j=||q||^2/2.

For completeness, the envelope minimizes J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This has a
unique minimizer, and an active-plane slope g at z proves optimality at
x=z+g. At two proximal points, monotonicity of the subgradients gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The two minimizing
inequalities identify the derivative as g; joint convexity proves
convexity of the envelope. Thus the claimed function has the required
global regularity, not only valid formal data.

The second checkpoint, of size1+M_1 epsilon, sends the iterate to
X_tail=y+epsilon d. The optimum jet is (0,0,0). Every interpolant of
this prefix has origin as an actual closest minimizer to (1,0): the
retained first checkpoint jet (p,p,||p||^2/2), interpolated with any
minimizer z, forces <p,z><=0. Its squared distance from (1,0) is
therefore at least1, attained at origin. This normalization persists
for all extensions below.

Allow ANY legal continuation with a single common gradient g at every
tail query, including the terminal sample; allow affine intercepts,
positive values and extra dimensions, and retain every prefix jet.
Its endpoint is y+epsilon d-Tg. The accepted interpolation inequality
from this endpoint to the old jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) gives

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||^2
                         <=epsilon^2||d||^2/sigma
                         =A kappa epsilon^2/sigma.

This uses a retained old query, not a reset to a new problem. From (25),
EVERY such constant-tail continuation obeys the exact universal cap

    2f_end<=U_ij:=A^2 epsilon^2/[(1+epsilon)(1+2T)].     (27)

In particular it covers all angles and all optimized tail planes of the
previous explicit two-checkpoint cap policy. It does not cover angles
kappa>A/(1+epsilon), different two-block prefixes, or variable-gradient
tails. No claim that (27) is attained for every pair is needed below.

## 7. The complete cap-range mechanism is dominated by K on H_m

Return to the full H_m, m>=128. For a selected pair i<j let their levels
be u,v; write d=a c^v=h_j-1, x=1+S, and epsilon=d/x. The individual
accepted cut terms at i and j are K_i,K_j. Direct cancellation gives

    U_ij/K_i=epsilon^2/(1+epsilon)
                          +2d epsilon/(1+2T),          (28)
    U_ij/K_j=(h_i-1)^2(1+P+h_i+S)^2
                          /[(1+P)^2 x(x+d)].           (29)

Every occurrence of level v has a full H_v immediately after it, so
T>=S_v. If v>=2 then M_v>=M_2=4+2a+ac>5, and by (11),(12),

    2d/(1+2T)<=2r M_v/(2M_v-1)<=10r/9<=1/2.

Consequently, for epsilon<=1, (28) is at most1.

If epsilon>1, then x<d. The first selected cut must lie INSIDE the
copy of H_v immediately preceding j and its level satisfies u<v.
Otherwise that copy lies between the cuts, giving S>=S_v and
x>=M_v>d. Inside this copy put P_loc for its mass before i. The
subcopies of H_u immediately before and after i yield

    P>=P_loc, x>=M_u,
    1+P_loc=M_v-(h_i-1)-x.

Also h_i-1<=d/c<=r M_v/c and x<d<=r M_v. For fixed x, the factor
(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P) decreases with P, so substituting P_loc in (29)
gives

    U_ij/K_j
      <=(h_i-1)^2 M_v^2/
          [(M_v-(h_i-1)-x)^2 x(x+d)]
      <r^2/[2(1-r-r/c)^2]
      <=648/841<1.                                    (30)

The penultimate step uses h_i-1<=r M_u<=r x and x/(x+d)<1/2.
For the last, r<=9/20 and c>=12/5 imply 1-r-r/c>=29/80.

It remains to include the two small levels. If v=0, then d=a<1/2 and
epsilon<=d. Equation (28) is at most d^2/(1+d)+2d^2<=2/3. If v=1,
then d=ac<1. Unless i is the one level-zero step immediately before j,
the intervening mass includes H_1, so epsilon<=1/2 and T>=S_1>=1;
(28) is then at most1/6+1/3=1/2. For that one adjacent pair x=1 and
h_i-1=a. Equation (29) is bounded by

    a^2(2+a)^2/(1+ac)<=1/(1+ac)<1,

because a<=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<=1. This covers EVERY ordered pair,
including pairs near the end and repeated occurrences of either level.

Combining (14),(27)--(30), every member of the complete constant-tail
mechanism in Section6 has normalized error at most K_n(H_m). Therefore

    max{Q_m(d_0), K_n(H_m),
        supremum of the Section6 constant-tail scores}
      =1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.             (31)

This is a quantitative failure of a concrete retained-history mechanism
on the ASSIGNED constant-level family. Its tail bound allows all
dimensions, intercepts and positive values once its prefix and angle
range are fixed. Thus retuning only its two cuts, its angle inside (25),
or its final constant-gradient plane cannot produce the target comparison
for all appended b: b=d_0 already defeats that possibility.

The unrestricted W_n may be larger. In particular, (31) gives no upper
certificate for it, no primitive-validity conclusion, and no decision of
the original all-schedule/all-horizon problem. The remaining exact branch
is to leave (25), change more than two prefix cells, or allow genuinely
varying gradients within the remaining ruler block while retaining all
queried jets and the closest-minimizer normalization.
