# Free terminal probability, physical clock metric, and clock-null details

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation89.
Guidance916499ce0062d6e3; first-law plan346caa664e70027e;
second-law freeze4e1931ba7785240a.
Original exact-profile target OPEN. No formal submission or new fact ID.

## 1. Result and its precise scope

The prescribed free-probability law has correct full-formal-space square
accounting and compatible zero incoming optimum moments. Its clock-bracketed
terminal distribution, physical-mass metric, and every prescribed local detail
weight are well defined and nonnegative for every SAME enclosing m>=1536.
The original optimum multipliers all have the required signs. The source and
last column capacities, both endpoint row-clock screens, and the last adjacent
pair and final-three-query necessary cuts pass.

Nevertheless its literal CANONICAL skew completion is not a certificate:

    lambda_(l,l-2)<-1/2,

uniformly in m. Here l=n-1 is the last QUERY, not the actual terminal n.
This is a failure of that specified completion only. An arbitrary compatible
skew correction annihilating both e and t is NOT excluded for this first law.
The other interior column capacities have explicit formulas below, but their
signs are not claimed to pass. One failed original multiplier already rules
out the frozen canonical law; no complete array is claimed.

The second and last law changes only the terminal clock distribution, to the
exact source/last barycentric distribution with the same required mean. The
same free-pi square accounting and moments still hold. But EVERY compatible
skew, including the canonical one, then satisfies TWO uniform obstructions:

    sum_(i=1)^l lambda_(i,0)<-3*pi/2,
    sum_(i=1)^l (t_i-1)*lambda_(0,i)<-3/5.

These are literal sums of original queried-prefix multipliers. The terminal
incoming mass2alpha_0 is accounted for separately, and nu_0=0 exactly.
In fact the second obstruction shows, for the fixed source common column and
fixed physical clock metric here, that ANY nonnegative completion must have

    alpha_0 < 4*pi^3.

The second law has4*pi/5<alpha_0<pi, so it cannot satisfy that requirement.
This is a scale-sensitive source allocation obstruction, not a bound on the
unrestricted function-class value or an infeasibility theorem for all duals.

All new claims are proved together below as ONE UNVERIFIED draft. Earlier
unverified findings are awareness only. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the
original interpolation orientation and terminal-square parameterization.
Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies only the original benchmark comparison and
its explicitly conditional799/800 implication. The representation fact
 d8b59c7d92425121 is not used as a quantitative upper bound.

## 2. Original problem, symbols, and uniform scalar guards

For a finite prescribed nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum
of2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over arbitrary finite Euclidean
dimensions, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an attained minimum,
a selected minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ORIGINAL updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The unchanged project target is the
asymptotic exact minimax comparison to the original all-split Zhang--Jiang
benchmark, not a comparison within this coefficient family. The conditional
route in accepted931b9653ce0f7201 still requires a full-function upper bound
W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D) for every m>=1536.

Fix such an m throughout. All child words use these same parameters:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    b=1+a, ggap=1+beta,
    H_0=(), H_(u+1)=(H_u,1+a*c^u,H_u),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1, jtail=l-2,
    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, q=17/(16D), pi=sqrt(q).

The letter pi here is the new terminal optimum probability; it is NEVER
identified with1/(1+S). V will denote a clock moment, not a function value.
e is the all-ones column in R^n, and e_i is its ith coordinate column.
The chronological matrix and clocks are

    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_iu=h_u for u<i, Ltri_iu=0 for u>i,
    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(u<i)h_u, T=t_l=S-a,
    Delta=T-1=S-b.

All clocks are strictly increasing. The last three physical steps are
(h_jtail,h_r,h_l)=(b,ggap,b), so T-t_r=ggap.

We will use the following intentionally loose exact enclosures:

    12/5<c<5/2, 99/100<beta<1,
    41/100<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,
    7/5<b<17/12, 19/10<ggap<2,
    S>n>2^20, S<c^m, 0<pi<1/1000,
    17/(48S)<q<17/(32S), D/S<201/100.                 (2.1)

For example sqrt(2)<99/70 and eps<=1/6144 give

    a>(70/169)*(6143/6145)>41/100.

The latter rational inequality follows by positive cross multiplication.
The upper bound uses beta/(1+eps)<1 and sqrt(2)<17/12. The bounds on c and
beta follow directly from sqrt(2)>7/5 and eps<=1/6144. Every step is greater
than1, giving S>n. Put vword=a/(c-2). The inequalities c>1+sqrt(2) and
beta<1 give0<vword<1. Summing the word gives the exact finite identity

    1+S_u=(1-vword)*2^u+vword*c^u.

It proves S<c^m. The q bounds follow from2S<D<3S; S>2^20 proves the stated
pi bound, and S>100 proves D/S<201/100. These are symbolic inequalities;
no recurrence or numerical evaluation has been executed.

For a suffix length Ls in{0,...,n}, define its product

    Rtail_Ls=product_(u=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_u-1), Rtail_0=1,
    s=Rtail_n, k_i=h_i product_(i<u<n)(1-h_u).

If pop(Ls) is the binary digit sum, then

    Rtail_Ls=beta^Ls*c^(-pop(Ls)),
    |k_i|=Rtail_(l-i)+Rtail_(l-i+1)<=2,
    k_i=(-1)^i |k_i|,
    k_0=(b/a)s, k_l=b, k_r=-a*ggap, k_jtail=b*a*beta.   (2.2)

Indeed h_(n-v)-1=a*c^v2(v), where v2(v) is the exponent of2 dividing v.
Counting the multiples of each power of2 gives
sum_(v=1)^Ls v2(v)=Ls-pop(Ls), proving the product formula. Multiplication
by h_i=1+(h_i-1) proves the two-suffix formula for |k_i|. The parity is
n-1-i, which has the parity of i since n is odd.

For m>=16, induction gives n>16m^2. Also
beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>2 by the binomial expansion. Therefore
beta^n<2^(-4m), and

    s*S^2 < beta^n*c^m < (5/32)^m < 1/64.

Together with(2.1), this proves0<s<pi<1. Define

    E=pi+s, F=pi-s, eta=F/E.

Then0<eta<1, pi<E<2pi, and eta*E^2=pi^2-s^2. In particular every
denominator E,a,pi,1-pi,Delta used below is strictly positive.

The finite products telescope to

    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.                   (2.3)

For detail: k_i=h_i prod_(u>i)(1-h_u), and summing the corresponding
successive-product differences over any suffix gives
sum_(u>=i)k_u=1-prod_(u>=i)(1-h_u). Thus
k_i+h_i sum_(u>i)k_u=h_i, proving the middle identity; the full-sum
identity uses prod_(u<n)(1-h_u)=-s. Multiply the middle identity by e
for the clock identity. None of these identities substitutes a probability
for the actual suffix scalar s.

## 3. Law I: literal terminal pool on actual adjacent clocks

Set

    V=(1-pi)/pi, tbar=(S-V)/(1-pi).

A direct use of S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2 gives

    (1-pi)(T-tbar)=15/(32pi)-b+(a+1/2)pi.

Using(2.1), the right side lies strictly between1/(3pi) and
(1-pi)/(2pi). For the lower bound, subtract1/(3pi): the remaining
13/(96pi)-b+(a+1/2)pi is positive. For the upper bound, subtract the
right side from(1-pi)/(2pi): it is1/(32pi)+b-1/2-(a+1/2)pi>0.
Consequently the weaker convenient bounds are

    1/(3pi)<T-tbar<1/(2pi), tbar>S/2.                (3.1)

The last inequality follows from T=S-a and
S/2>a+1/(2pi), using S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2 and pi<1/1000.

The first and last H_4 blocks have total mass S_4<c^4<40. Hence tbar
is later than every clock in the first H_4 block and earlier than every
clock in the last H_4 block: S/2>40 and T-tbar>1/(3pi)>40 suffice.
In particular the pool below avoids indices0,1 and jtail,r,l by a wide
strict margin. This uses actual original clocks, not a continuous-time
ruler approximation.

Let j be the smallest original index such that t_(j+1)>=tbar. Set

    alpha_j=(1-pi)(t_(j+1)-tbar)/(t_(j+1)-t_j),
    alpha_(j+1)=(1-pi)(tbar-t_j)/(t_(j+1)-t_j),
    alpha_i=0 at every other original query.

The denominator is exactly h_j>0. Both coefficients are nonnegative,
e^T alpha=1-pi, and alpha^T t=S-V. If tbar=t_k exactly, then j=k-1
and this formula puts the entire mass1-pi at k, with alpha_(k-1)=0.
There is no ambiguous tie choice or feasibility oracle. Put

    v=k-alpha.

Then e^T v=E and t^T v=V. Under Law I, in particular,

    alpha_0=alpha_1=alpha_jtail=alpha_r=alpha_l=0,
    v_0=bs/a, v_jtail=ba beta, v_r=-a ggap, v_l=b.       (3.2)

## 4. Common column, physical clock metric, and every local detail

The common column is exactly the assigned source column:

    z=(E/a)(b e_0-e_1), e^T z=E,
    Z=t^T z=-E/a,
    Xreq=D-pi^(-2)-eta Z^2
        =D/17-(pi^2-s^2)/a^2.                         (4.1)

Here t_0=1,t_1=1+b, so the stated Z follows by subtraction. Since a>2/5,

    Xreq>D/17-425/(64D)>0

as D>11. Also Xreq<D/17<S/5. The charge q=pi^2 is spent once; there is
no paid vector zeta and no omitted initial derivative direction.

Write Hvec_i=h_i and define the finite physical-mass moments

    Qmass=sum h_i^2, Rmass=sum h_i^3,
    t_mass=sum h_i t_i/S,
    Vmass=sum h_i(t_i-t_mass)^2,
    rho_clock=Xreq/Vmass,
    Kclock=rho_clock[diag(Hvec)-Hvec Hvec^T/S].         (4.2)

Every moment is given explicitly by the finite level-count formula

    sum_(i<n) h_i^d = sum_(u=0)^(m-1) 2^(m-1-u)(1+a c^u)^d,
    d=1,2,3.

This is a closed finite expression, not an evaluated recurrence. The word
is palindromic. With P_i=t_i-1 its prefix mass, palindrome reversal sends
P_i to S-P_i-h_i while retaining h_i. Thus

    sum h_i P_i=(S^2-Qmass)/2,
    sum h_i^2 P_i=(S Qmass-Rmass)/2.

Expanding (P_i+h_i)^3-P_i^3 and summing gives
sum h_i P_i^2=S^3/3-S Qmass/2+Rmass/6. Hence

    t_mass=1+(S^2-Qmass)/(2S),
    Vmass=S^3/12+Rmass/6-Qmass^2/(4S).                (4.3)

This verifies the proposed palindrome identity, including the correction
terms coming from the actual unequal physical steps.

Here is a useful stronger lower bound, with all finite-depth losses retained.
The level formula gives

    Qmass=2S-n+a^2(c^(2m)-2^m)/(c^2-2).

Since (2/c)^m<(5/6)^m<1/100, the mass formula yields
S>(99/100)a c^m/(c-2). The bound(5/6)^m<1/100 follows, for example,
from(6/5)^4>2 and m>=28. Therefore

    Qmass/S^2 < 2/S+(100/99)^2*(c-2)^2/(c^2-2)
               < 1/500+1/16 < 1/15.

The middle inequality uses S>1000 and the fact that
(c-2)^2/(c^2-2) increases on2<c<5/2, where it is less than1/17;
(100/99)^2/17<1/16 by positive cross multiplication. Formula(4.3) now gives

    Vmass>(1/12-1/900)S^3 > (2/25)S^3 > S^3/625,
    0<rho_clock<125/S^2.                             (4.4)

The deliberately looser final bound will simplify the canonical-skew estimate.
Cauchy--Schwarz in weights h_i shows Kclock is PSD, and Kclock e=0.
Moreover Kclock t has exact entries

    (Kclock t)_i=rho_clock h_i(t_i-t_mass),
    t^T Kclock t=Xreq.                               (4.5)

Define, at EVERY original index0<=i<=n-3,

    d_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2).

The coefficient sum is zero. Its clock product is
h_(i+1)t_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))(t_i+h_i)
+h_i(t_i+h_i+h_(i+1))=0. Thus d_i^T e=d_i^T t=0 exactly.

All detail weights, before any sign tests, are

    tau_i=8(q+v_i^2+v_(i+1)^2+v_(i+2)^2)/(h_i+h_(i+1))^2,
        1<=i<=n-3,
    tau_0=(3q-(Kclock)_00)/h_1^2,
    K=Kclock+sum_(i=0)^(n-3) tau_i d_i d_i^T.          (4.6)

For i>=1 every denominator is positive and every numerator is strictly
positive. For i=0, (Kclock)_00<125b/S^2<250/S^2<1/S<3q by(2.1).
Thus tau_0>0 as well. The only detail with a nonzero coordinate0 is d_0,
whose coordinate0 is h_1. Consequently

    K>=0, Ke=0, Kt=Kclock t, K00=3q, t^T Kt=Xreq.     (4.7)

The clock-null details spend no additional clock energy. This statement is
about a literal specified PSD matrix, not an unknown matrix variable.

## 5. Full original polynomial and both zero-incoming moments

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0, retaining ALL independent formal
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n and all queried indices. Use the orientation in
accepted1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_iu=f_i-f_u+<g_i,x_u-x_i>+||g_i-g_u||^2/2.

Let y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. After the original GD substitution,

    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),
    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                                (5.1)

The last identity follows from x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i y_i and the finite
product expansion; it does not execute a trajectory or identify formal
gradients. The triangular change of coordinates is invertible, and the
terminal g_n remains independent.

Define

    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    dvec=F(v-z), rvec=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-2alpha,
    A^T=-A, Ae=dvec, At=rvec, N=Ltri^T(P+A).           (5.2)

Section6 specifies A literally. EVERY original multiplier is

    lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i for i<n,
    lambda_(u,n)=0 for u in{star,0,...,l},
    lambda_(i,u)=-N_iu for distinct queried i,u,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i,
    lambda_(u,u)=0.

For every queried pair the explicit entry formula is

    N_iu=P_iu+A_iu+h_i sum_(v>i)(P_vu+A_vu).           (5.3)

At a queried index, prefix outgoing minus incoming mass is2alpha_i;
adding the incoming terminal mass2alpha_i cancels that divergence. The
terminal divergence is2, and the optimum divergence is-2. These assertions
hold as algebraic identities even if some finite-pair coefficients are signed.

The interpolation identity Q_iu=delta_i-delta_u+<g_i,y_u-y_i> gives

    sum_(prefix i!=u)lambda_iu Q_iu=2alpha^T delta-g^T N y.

Adding the full outgoing-terminal identity from accepted1252 and then
substituting(5.1) yields

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_iu Q_iu
        =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2 + y^T K y
          +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-pi^2)||x_0||^2.   (5.4)

For a direct expansion, Pe=Ev+Fz and Pe-Ae=2s v+2Fz.
Thus-g^T N y=y^T P y-<x_0,(2s v+2Fz)^T y>.
The terminal subtraction is-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2. Its2s cross term cancels,
and eta E^2=pi^2-s^2 gives precisely(5.4). At q=pi^2 this is PSD on
the entire original formal space. It is not a restriction to clock-null
vectors, a difference-gradient subspace, or actual trajectories.

Both incoming moments must be checked before demanding nu=0. In general
for the displayed Ae but before specifying At,

    mu=2pi Ltri^T v,
    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,
    e^T nu=2pi V-2(1-pi),
    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2(S-V), X=t^T Kt.           (5.5)

The first scalar identity uses skewness and t^T Ae=F(V-Z); the second
uses t^T At=0. Setting V=(1-pi)/pi and X=Xreq as in(4.1) makes both
moments EXACTLY zero, since (V+1)^2=pi^(-2) and D=1+2S. In particular
these are not the old special-clock identities V=2 or theta0=2-6p.

Every optimum outgoing sign has a separate exact proof. From(2.3),

    (Ltri^T v)_i
      =h_i(pi+sum_(u<i)alpha_u)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.      (5.6)

Consequently mu_i>0 for all i. The terminal multipliers are nonnegative,
and the construction of A below makes EVERY nu_i=0, not just its moments.

## 6. Literal canonical skew and a uniform entry bound

Let

    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec,
    A0=(dvec e^T-e dvec^T)/n,
    fvec=rvec-tmean*dvec+(dvec^T t)e/n,
    Acan=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.              (6.1)

This is the frozen completion for each of the two laws in this report.
The two moment equations imply

    e^T rvec=-dvec^T t, t^T rvec=0,
    e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0.

Multiplying(6.1) by e and t therefore gives Acan e=dvec and Acan t=rvec.
It is skew by inspection. In particular nu=0 component by component.
There is no linear feasibility problem left implicit in(6.1).

For completeness Ve has a uniform positive lower bound. Let M=2^(m-1)-1
be the length of each child around the central step Hc=1+a c^(m-1).
The actual first-child clocks and second-child clocks differ by at least Hc.
Since S<c^m and a/c>4/25, Hc>4S/25. The pairwise variance identity gives

    Ve=(1/n)sum_(i<u)(t_i-t_u)^2
       >=(M^2/n)Hc^2 > n S^2/625,                  (6.2)

where M/n>1/4 since n>2. No continuum approximation is involved.

The following estimate applies to BOTH laws and indeed to any nonnegative
alpha of total mass1-pi with the required clock moment, using the same Kt:

    |Acan_iu|<1/100 for all original i,u.              (6.3)

Here are the bounds establishing it. Formula(2.2) gives |v_i|<=3;
|z_i|<8pi, |Z|<5pi, |dvec_i|<4pi and |dvec^T t|<2.
By(4.4)--(4.5), |(Kt)_i|<125. Thus |rvec_i|<5/pi.
As tmean<S and pi S<17/(32pi), (6.1) gives |fvec_i|<9/pi.
Also |cvec_i|<S. Applying(6.2) to(6.1) gives

    |Acan_iu| < 8pi/n +11250/(n*pi*S)
              <34000*pi/n <34/n <1/100.

For the middle inequality use pi^2 S>17/48, so1/(pi S)<3pi.
The final inequalities use pi<1/1000 and n>2^20. These generous bounds
already suffice; the actual original indices have not been coalesced.

## 7. Law I: source identities, endpoint screens, and late necessary cuts

The first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Therefore, for ANY compatible
skew, not just(6.1),

    N00=b(Pe-Ae)_0-a P00
       =b^2*q/a-a K00-a alpha_0^2,
    mu_0=2pi(b pi+a alpha_0),
    (Nt)_0=-2a(Kt)_0+2a alpha_0/pi.                  (7.1)

For the first identity, complete the square in v_0 using
v_0=bs/a-alpha_0 and z_0=bE/a. The constant is
b^2[s^2+eta E^2]/a=b^2 pi^2/a. For the last identity use
Nt=2Ltri^T(Kt+Vv+eta Zz-alpha); its two common-column terms cancel
because eta z_0=bF/a, and1+V=1/pi.

Thus Law I has EXACTLY the requested calibrations:

    N00=b^2*q/a-a K00=(1/a+2-2a)q,
    mu_0=2bq,
    sum_(u>0)(t_u-1)N0u=-2a(Kt)_0-2bq.              (7.2)

All details leave (Kt)_0=(Kclock t)_0<0. Write
r0=N00-mu_0=(1/a-4a)q>0. The source column capacity is N00>0 because
alpha_0=nu_0=0. To check the source row-clock screen, put

    B0=-sum_(u>0)(t_u-1)N0u=2a(Kt)_0+2bq.

Nonnegative finite multipliers require b*r0<=B0<=Delta*r0.
These inequalities really pass. From(4.3)--(4.4),

    |(Kt)_0| <= Xreq*b*S/(2Vmass),
    |(Kt)_0|/(bq)<100(D/S)^2/289<7/5.

Use a<5/12 for2a*(7/5)<7/6. The increasing function2-1/a+4a is
greater than6/5 when a>41/100 (at41/100 it is1231/1025>6/5).
Consequently

    B0-b*r0=2a(Kt)_0+bq(2-1/a+4a)>bq/30>0.

For the other endpoint, B0<2bq, while
r0>(12/5-5/3)q=(11/15)q and Delta>10. This proves B0<Delta*r0.
In particular the source weighted sum in(7.2) is strictly negative.

At the last query, Law I has z_l=alpha_l=0 and v_l=b. Put

    Dlast=Nll=Pll=Kll+b^2,
    rlast=Dlast-mu_l,
    Bl=(Nt)_l-T mu_l.

Then

    mu_l=2pi b, (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+2Vb,
    Bl=2(Kt)_l+2b(V-pi T),
    V-pi T=15/(32pi)-1+a pi>1/(3pi).                  (7.3)

The last column capacity Dlast-2alpha_l=Dlast is positive. Also
(Kt)_l>0. Let tau=tau_jtail. Only d_jtail reaches coordinate l, so

    Kll=(Kclock)_ll+tau*b^2,
    tau=8(q+v_jtail^2+v_r^2+b^2)/(b+ggap)^2<4.

The inequality follows from v_jtail<1, |v_r|<1, b<3/2, q<1/4 and
b+ggap>3. As (Kclock)_ll<1, it follows that Dlast<13 and rlast<13;
also rlast>b^2-2pi b>1. Nonnegative last-row coefficients require

    ggap*rlast <= Bl <= Delta*rlast.

The lower inequality follows from Bl>2b/(3pi)>26>ggap*rlast.
For the upper inequality, equivalently
(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*Dlast<0, use
2(Kt)_l<1, 2Vb<3/pi, Delta>S/2 and Dlast>1. Then the left side is
less than1+3/pi-S/2<0, using(2.1) and S=17/(32pi^2)-1/2.
Both complete endpoint row screens therefore pass.

The final adjacent-pair invariant follows from the last rows of Ltri^T:

    N_rl+N_lr=2P_rl+ggap*Pll=:C2.

If all finite coefficients were nonnegative, C2<=0 would be necessary.
Here its exact components are

    C2_clock=rho_clock*ggap*b[1-(b+2)/S],
    C2_detail=-tau*b[2b+ggap*(2-b)],
    C2_v=ggap*b*(1-a), C2_z=0.

The clock term is positive but less than1/100. The v term is less than2.
Since tau>8b^2/(b+ggap)^2>32/25 and b>7/5,
|C2_detail|>2*(32/25)*(49/25)>5. Hence C2<-1: this cut passes.

The exact final-three-query identity, rederived in the original orientation,
is

    C3=2(P_jtail,l+P_rl)+(3-a beta)Pll-mu_l-nu_l-2alpha_l
       =b*lambda_(r,l)
          +sum_(i<jtail)[lambda_(l,i)+lambda_(i,l)].  (7.4)

Indeed, in addition to the adjacent identity,
N_jtail,l+N_l,jtail=2P_jtail,l+b N_rl-b beta Pll.
Insert these two identities into the sum of the original last row and last
column, using their sums mu_l and nu_l+2alpha_l, respectively, to get(7.4).
Thus C3>=0 is necessary regardless of the completion.

For Law I all four parts are explicit:

    C3_clock=rho_clock*b[(3-a beta)
                       -((3-a beta)b+2(b+ggap))/S]>0,
    C3_detail=tau*b^2(1-a beta)>0,
    C3_v_and_mu=b[3+a-a beta(1-a)-2pi]>0,
    C3_z=0.

The first bracket is positive by S>2^20, a<1/2, b<3/2, ggap<2.
The other two signs follow from0<a beta<1/2 and pi<1/1000.
Hence this final-three-query cut passes as well. The old negative cut for
the fixed V=2, late-common-column architecture has not been reused.

For clarity, EVERY queried column capacity for either law has the literal
formula

    cap_i=Nii-nu_i-2alpha_i
         =Pii+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_ui+Acan_ui)-2alpha_i.    (7.5)

It equals sum_(u!=i, u<n)lambda_(u,i). These are all actual columns;
there is no aggregated or omitted interior column. Section7 has established
the signs of cap_0 and cap_l for Law I. It does not assert the signs of
all other cap_i. The independent finite-pair failure in Section8 precludes
claiming a certificate even if additional capacities were to pass.

## 8. Decisive first-law failure and the remaining skew freedom

At jtail=l-2, only d_jtail contributes to the off-diagonal K_jtail,l.
By(3.2),

    P_jtail,l=-rho_clock*b^2/S+tau*b*ggap+b^2*a*beta.

The last term exceeds3/4, because
(7/5)^2*(2/5)*(99/100)>3/4 by positive cross multiplication.
The negative term has magnitude less than250/S^3<1/100.
The detail term is strictly positive. Consequently P_jtail,l>2/3.
The last row of Ltri^T is the last coordinate row, so under(6.1)

    N_l,jtail=P_l,jtail+Acan_l,jtail>2/3-1/100>1/2,
    lambda_(l,jtail)=-N_l,jtail<-1/2.                 (8.1)

This is an original finite interpolation coefficient, not a formal negative
vector or a restricted PSD test. Its failure is uniform for every assigned m.
It decisively refutes the frozen first law while all the checked scalar,
optimum, endpoint, and late-cut conditions above remain true.

It would be incorrect to infer failure of all compatible skews from(8.1).
All completions are Acan+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. An explicit remaining
freedom can be written using the source and last-query anchors. For each
0<i<l set

    u_i=e_i-[(T-t_i)/Delta]e_0-[(t_i-1)/Delta]e_l.

Then u_i^T e=u_i^T t=0. For any two interior indices i<j and scalar kappa,

    C=kappa(u_i u_j^T-u_j u_i^T)

is an explicit compatible correction, with original array effect

    (DeltaN)_pq=kappa[(Ltri^T u_i)_p(u_j)_q
                         -(Ltri^T u_j)_p(u_i)_q].    (8.2)

It leaves all row/column sums, row clocks, P, the full PSD charge, and both
cuts in Section7 unchanged. Taking i=jtail,j=r changes A_l,jtail by
kappa*(t_r-1)/Delta, which is nonzero for nonzero kappa. Thus even the
particular bad entry can be moved by a literal allowed correction. No claim
is made that doing so repairs every other sign. The compatibility space is
not empty, but global sign feasibility inside it remains OPEN for Law I.
No search, unspecified LP/SDP, or existence oracle is substituted for it.

## 9. Law II: frozen replacement of the terminal clock distribution

After(8.1), the second and final allowed law replaces ONE pattern: the
terminal distribution. All other rules remain as written, including the
source common column, physical clock metric, local d_i library, the tau_i
rule (recomputed from the new v), and the canonical skew(6.1).

Define

    Bsrc=((1-pi)T-(S-V))/Delta,
    alpha=Bsrc*e_0+(1-pi-Bsrc)*e_l.                   (9.1)

Because1<tbar<T, these are positive coefficients of total mass1-pi,
and alpha^T t=S-V exactly. This is the actual endpoint barycentric law,
not a claim that it is equivalent to the interior bracket law.

Its source mass is explicit in pi:

    Bsrc/pi=[15/32-b*pi+(a+1/2)pi^2]
                  /[17/32-(b+1/2)pi^2].             (9.2)

The denominator is pi^2*Delta>0. Comparing numerator and denominator gives

    4pi/5<Bsrc<pi,  alpha_l>1-2pi>0.                (9.3)

For the lower bound, numerator minus4/5 times denominator is
7/160-b*pi+[a+1/2+(4/5)(b+1/2)]pi^2>0,
using b<3/2 and pi<1/1000. For the upper bound, denominator minus numerator
is1/16+b*pi-2b*pi^2>0. Every coefficient in(9.1) is therefore well defined.

Set v=k-alpha and recompute tau_i for i>=1 from(4.6); tau_0, Kclock,
Kt, and Xreq are unchanged. Positivity of every tau_i, K>=0, Ke=0,
K00=3q, t^T Kt=Xreq, the two zero incoming moments, and the full original
polynomial(5.4) are all justified by exactly the SAME displayed algebra,
which requires only alpha>=0, e^T alpha=1-pi, alpha^T t=S-V.
This is a joint derivation, not reliance on an unverified lemma as a fact.
Equations(5.3), (6.1), and(9.1) specify every second-law original multiplier.
All optimum and terminal signs still pass, and nu is identically zero.

## 10. Two all-completion obstructions for Law II

The first-row identities(7.1) hold for EVERY compatible skew. With K00=3q,

    N00=(b^2/a-3a)q-a Bsrc^2
       =(1/a+2-2a)q-a Bsrc^2 <(9/2)q.

Since nu_0=0, the total incoming queried-prefix mass is

    cap_0=sum_(i=1)^l lambda_(i,0)=N00-2Bsrc
         <(9/2)pi^2-(8/5)pi<-3pi/2.                (10.1)

The upper coefficient9/2 follows from a>2/5 and-2a<0; the final inequality
uses pi<1/1000. The terminal edge lambda_(n,0)=2Bsrc has NOT been dropped:
it is precisely the2Bsrc subtracted in the column capacity. Thus a sum of
original finite queried multipliers is strictly negative for all completions.

There is also a stronger-scale source row-clock obstruction. Define

    Wsrc=sum_(i=1)^l (t_i-1)N0i.

Using(7.1) and the source row sum mu_0 gives

    Wsrc=-2a(Kt)_0+2a Bsrc(1/pi-pi)-2b pi^2.          (10.2)

Here (Kt)_0=rho_clock*b(1-t_mass)<0. By(9.3), a>2/5 and b<3/2,

    Wsrc>(16/25)(1-pi^2)-3pi^2>3/5.

Every weight t_i-1 is positive for i>0. Since lambda_(0,i)=-N0i,
(10.2) is exactly

    sum_(i=1)^l (t_i-1)lambda_(0,i)=-Wsrc<-3/5.       (10.3)

Neither(10.1) nor(10.3) depends on a canonical entry. Adding any explicit
or arbitrary skew C with Ce=Ct=0 preserves them. Therefore Law II is
excluded for ALL compatible completions, not merely the literal one.
Its other column capacities and late cuts cannot reverse either obstruction;
no assertion that they all pass is needed or made.

The scope can be stated more generally WITHOUT introducing a third law.
Keep the same q=pi^2, source z, Kt=Kclock t and zero incoming vector, but
allow ANY nonnegative terminal distribution with the required mass and mean,
and ANY additional PSD details annihilating both e and t. Any nonnegative
original array must satisfy Wsrc<=0. Formula(10.2), with Bsrc replaced by
alpha_0, forces

    alpha_0 <= pi*[a(Kt)_0+b pi^2]/[a(1-pi^2)]
              < b*pi^3/[a(1-pi^2)] < 4pi^3.          (10.4)

The first inequality is necessary even if its numerator is negative, in
which case no nonnegative alpha_0 can satisfy it. For the prescribed metric
its numerator is positive: Section7 showed
|(Kt)_0|<(7/5)bq and a<5/12, hence a(Kt)_0+bq>(5/12)bq>0.
The last inequality uses a>41/100, b<17/12, and pi<1/1000.
No K00 assumption is required for the row-clock implication(10.4).
Thus source terminal probability is limited at order pi^3 in this architecture,
whereas the endpoint distribution requests order pi. The separate capacity
inequality(10.1) additionally uses the specific K00=3q.

This necessary condition does NOT apply to terminal mass at arbitrary other
indices without rederiving the corresponding original row identity. It does
not exclude the first-law bracket pool, whose alpha_0 is exactly zero. It
also does not prohibit a changed common column, a changed non-null clock
metric, nonzero incoming load with correctly paid moments, or a different
full certificate architecture.

## 11. Author audit, shared-state scope, and handoff

The direct-proving screen and counterexample procedure have been applied to
the two frozen whole laws. The explicit counterexample to first-law sign
feasibility is its specified original coefficient(8.1), with all parameter
and moment assumptions retained. The second law has the two original cut
counterexamples(10.1),(10.3). Neither is a legal primal adversary or an
unrestricted lower bound on W.

Audit points checked symbolically in this draft:

1. Free pi, actual suffix scalar s, actual same-m clock T=S-a, and actual
   terminal n remain distinct. No old T=1/p-b or V=2 relation was imported.
2. The terminal square and the common square reproduce the ENTIRE original
   polynomial, with q spent as pi^2 and all independent gradients retained.
3. Both incoming moments follow from the original sums, and the literal skew
   makes nu identically zero. No source load or paid imbalance is hidden.
4. The original bracket selection and an exact-clock tie are defined, and the
   pool is separated from both endpoint blocks by proved clock inequalities.
5. Every denominator and local detail weight is positive for all assigned m;
   the palindrome physical-mass variance includes both finite-step corrections.
6. All optimum signs are proved entrywise. Source and last capacities and
   both endpoint screens are checked for Law I. Interior capacities are fully
   specified but not claimed nonnegative. The first-law failure is a literal
   finite-pair sign, not an untested inference from a moment relaxation.
7. The adjacent and final-three cuts are rederived with the free-pi data.
   They pass for Law I; their old special-clock evaluations are inapplicable.
8. Canonical failure is separated from all-completion failure. Formula(8.2)
   explicitly exhibits the first law's surviving neutral-skew freedom.
9. The second law changes only the terminal distribution; its induced v and
   detail weights are recomputed. Both source obstructions retain the incoming
   terminal mass and the exact zero incoming optimum vector.
10. The source mass upper bound(10.4) depends on the source column and fixed
    Kt, not on an alleged universal optimum-probability identity.

Only one continuation invocation was allocated, and its two-law allowance is
consumed. This report stops at the stated cap; it does not automatically renew
work, control a worker/service, finalize the project, revoke a fact, publish
outward, or declare the original goal or a partial Goal complete.

Resource record: TWO explicit laws; ZERO mathematical programs, numerical or
arithmetic replays, executed recurrences or trajectories, solvers, optimizers,
benchmark DP, formal calls, fact_submit calls, new facts, installations,
consults, or subagents. Administrative text reads/writes/hashes only. One
Matlas query and one web query were used for background orientation; no
external theorem was imported. The finite identities reduce to the existing
original-array setting, so no new paper was used as a proof premise.

The concrete remaining first-law question is whether an EXPLICIT neutral skew
C can make EVERY original multiplier nonnegative with the already fixed
bracketed alpha,z,K and correct free-pi moments. The source+last replacement
cannot do this. Passing the available scalar cuts, or merely moving the bad
canonical tail entry, is not a solution of that question. The unrestricted
original exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

# Changed initial projection attachments: two complete H4 certificate obstructions

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation83, ended baseline82.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; guidance b5e78dfeb7a03562.
One invocation, completed ceiling83/attempted84, controller cap1.
No formal call or fact_submit is authorized. New identities and obstructions
below are jointly derived draft mathematics, not facts available downstream.

## 1. Result and scope

Both specified new attachment families fail as sufficient conic/PSD identities
for the FULL H4 endpoint at d=1. Neither failure is an actual-history
counterexample to that endpoint inequality.

Mechanism1 transfers actual initial-mixture mass from g3,g11 to rL,rR.
Its changed initial-x terms are canceled by the explicitly included ORIGINAL
auxiliary normal rows. It retains the sibling-normal allocation and permits
every nonnegative right local-cycle weight. Unlike the old frozen family,
it genuinely changes the old common-physical-gradient test. But a separate
terminal-only perturbation defeats the entire new parameter family.

Mechanism2 directly addresses that terminal defect: it additionally transfers
arbitrary remaining initial physical mass to the actual terminal projection
q=g15. All fifteen physical attachments are allowed to change. The changed
x terms are canceled using ORIGINAL terminal-normal rows. The whole family
still fails, on the explicit full-space assignment in Section8. This witness
also specializes to mechanism1 and is independent of every attachment and
sibling-allocation parameter. It is a negative value of the complete
quadratic, not a negative coefficient considered in isolation.

The original primitive-row array C_rows is fixed in both families; no claim
about arbitrary redistributions of that array is made. The final original
row weights are all specified and nonnegative. An entrywise formula specifies
every coefficient of the complete formal matrix, including the zero x row
after exact cancellation and the unequal E,J auxiliary denominators.

Both mandatory actual histories are retained in Sections9-10 with their
actual right H3, all physical reserves, and all radial/normal terms. Their
full endpoint gaps remain strictly positive. No child is reset.

Accepted fact1e0bf3f41457d8e4 proves the actual H3 inequality for every
compact convex body containing0 and every d>=1, and its finite-word
representation and full-class seven-step conclusions have precisely their
stated scope. We use its H2 identities and actual H3 law. We neither reprove
the representation theorem nor claim that the accepted H3 law implies H4.

H4 at d=1 remains OPEN. H4 for all d>=1 remains OPEN. All-depth propagation,
any uniform (1+C0/m)/D upper bound, and the original minimax target remain
OPEN. No all-depth mechanism or third attachment family is opened.

## 2. Fixed word, all samples, and accepted inputs

Fix ONE integer m>=1536 and keep it in every child. Put

 epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
 c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
 H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j), S_j=sum H_j.

Local abbreviations, identical to the source, are

 A=1+a, B=1+beta, t=S2=2A+B, C=1+t,
 k=beta*c=d_2, K=beta*c^2=d_3, ell=C+k,
 s=S3=2C+k-1=t+ell, C3=1+s=2C+k,
 b=1+K, h=s+b=C3+K, S=S4=2s+b.

Here S is the LOCAL H4 mass; the enclosing full word still has mass S_m
and n=2^m-1. No replacement of either mass is intended. The actual H4 is

 (A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A,1+k,A,B,A).        (2.1)

For one nonempty compact convex body D0 containing0 in a positive finite
Euclidean dimension, let P be projection onto D0 and

 F(X)=max_(g in D0)(<X,g>-||g||^2/2),
 U_d(X)=max_(g in D0)(2<X,g>-d||g||^2), d>0.

The actual samples obey x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i, g_i=P(x_i), 0<=i<15;
g15=P(x15) is the terminal gradient and supplies no physical step. Put

 x=x0, u=x3, z=x4, v=x7, w=x8, uR=x11, zR=x12, y=x15.

Thus z=u-(1+k)g3, w=v-b*g7, zR=uR-(1+k)g11. At d=1 use

 J=1+2t, D=1+2s, M=1+2S=D+2h,
 eta=M-2s=D+2b, E=eta+2t,
 M=E+2ell, D=J+2ell, E-J=2h.                 (2.2)

The ACTUAL auxiliary maximizers on this SAME body are

 p0=P(x/M), rL=P(z/E), rR=P(zR/J),
 q=P(y)=g15, qL=P(v/eta), rO=P(w/D).

The sample inputs belonging to labels p0,rL,rR,qL,rO are respectively
x/M,z/E,zR/J,v/eta,w/D. They are not extra GD steps.

For ANY two physical or auxiliary labels use

 Q_(i,j)=<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j><=0,                (2.3)

where X_j is that label's sample input and G_j its projection. This is
the exact projection specialization of smooth-convex interpolation in
accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4, PartB(2.3), derived there from its predecessor
1252b8823bb5a290. In particular Q_(i,p0)=<x/M-p0,G_i-p0>.

For a physical word of mass T from X to Y its primitive energy is

 V=||X||^2-||Y+T*P(Y)||^2.

Accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 PartB(2.4),(3.3)-(3.6),(4.2)-(4.5)
supplies the exact primitive, H2, join, and maximization identities used
below. The off-root identities are algebraic and do not assume primitive
positivity at H3 or H4. PartB(6.3) supplies the full actual H3 U law.

## 3. The complete base identity and every base row

We restate the full source(4.8), including its row and square coefficients,
and check it from the accepted identities. Set C1=1+A and

 kap1=(3C1+beta)/(C1-beta),
 R1=(C1+beta)[C1^2-beta(2C1+beta)]/(C1-beta),
 deltaA=2-A^2,
 e0=deltaA, e1=R1(2/B-1),
 e2=kap1*deltaA+R1(2/A-1).

All three e_i are positive by accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 PartB(3.4)-(3.7).
On each H2 block of four gradients the complete base multiplier list is

 Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2(1+A), Lambda_12=2R1/B,
 Lambda_21=(kap1-1)A+2R1/B,
 Lambda_13=(kap1-1)beta, Lambda_31=kap1-1,
 Lambda_23=2kap1+(kap1-1)A+2R1/A,
 Lambda_32=2kap1(1+A)+2R1/A.                 (3.1)

All other H2 entries are zero. For offsets o=0,4,8,12 define

 C_o=-sum_(i!=j in {0,1,2,3})Lambda_ij Q_(o+i,o+j),
 P_o=sum_(j=0)^2 e_j||g_(o+j)-g_(o+j+1)||^2,
 V_o=C_o+P_o.

Use C00=C_0,C01=C_4,C10=C_8,C11=C_12, and analogous P,V labels.
Put

 kap=(3C+k)/(C-k), kapO=(3C3+K)/(C3-K),
 d_i=(C+k)[k(2C+k)-C^2]/(C-k),
 d_o=(C3+K)[K(2C3+K)-C3^2]/(C3-K),
 Bterm=kapO*d_i+d_o.                         (3.2)

Here d_i,d_o are the source's defect coefficients, not sequence elements
d_j. They are strictly positive. To check the signs set u0=sqrt(2)-1:

 k-u0*C=epsilon[u0+4u0^2/(1+epsilon)-lambda*epsilon]>0.

Indeed u0>2/5,lambda<5/2,epsilon<1/8 give a positive bracket.
Also K/C3=c*(k/C)/(2+k/C)>u0, since c>lambda and the map
r -> c*r/(2+r) is strictly increasing with lambda*u0/(2+u0)=u0.
The bounds C>11/2,k<5/2,C3>13,K<25/4 ensure both denominators in(3.2)
are positive. These bounds follow directly from epsilon<=1/6144,
beta>9/10,c<5/2,a>1/3,a<u0<1/2. In particular Bterm>0.

The three joins are

 E_i=sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+k Q_(3,7)
                                      +sum_(j=4)^6 h_j Q_(j,7),
 E_r=sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,11)+Q_(15,11)+k Q_(11,15)
                                      +sum_(j=12)^14 h_j Q_(j,15),
 E_o=sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,7)+Q_(15,7)+K Q_(7,15)
                                      +sum_(j=8)^14 h_j Q_(j,15).

Define the COMPLETE nonnegative row expression and all physical squares

 C_rows=C00+kap*C01+kapO*C10+kapO*kap*C11
       -(kap-1)E_i-kapO*(kap-1)E_r-(kapO-1)E_o,
 P_sq=P00+kap*P01+kapO*P10+kapO*kap*P11.       (3.3)

For absolute specificity this is also an entry rule for the base array.
Start with zero on every ordered pair. For each offset o add rho_o*Lambda_ij
to the coefficient of -Q_(o+i,o+j), where
rho_0=1,rho_4=kap,rho_8=kapO,rho_12=kapO*kap. For the three tuples

 (p,n,I,e,lambdaJoin)=
 (3,7,{4,5,6},k,kap-1),
 (11,15,{12,13,14},k,kapO*(kap-1)),
 (7,15,{8,...,14},K,kapO-1),

add lambdaJoin*h_j on (j,p) and(j,n) for each j in I, lambdaJoin on(n,p),
and lambdaJoin*e on(p,n). Add coincident entries. This completely defines
every base ordered-row coefficient, including all overlaps, with no negative
entries and no implicit feasibility variables.

Applying accepted PartB(3.3) to the two inner joins and then the outer join,
with actual capacities C,C and then C3,C3, gives exactly

 V4=C_rows+P_sq+Fdef,
 Fdef=-d_i||g3||^2+(d_i-d_o)||g7||^2
                -kapO*d_i||g11||^2+Bterm||q||^2.            (3.4)

The four diagonal defect coefficients sum to0. This application keeps the
signed off-root term from the accepted identity; it does not invoke an
exact-root composition law on a perturbed bridge.

Let

 Hvec=sum_(i=0)^14 h_i*g_i=x-y,
 Vmix=Hvec+(1+S)q,
 Wstar=M*p0-Vmix,
 Nstar=2<x-M*p0,Wstar>,
 Qbase=||Wstar||^2+P_sq+Fdef.                 (3.5)

The canonical mixture Vmix/M is convex since its nonnegative weights sum
to M. The exact maximization identity accepted PartB(4.2),(4.5), applied
to the WHOLE H4 at d=1, is

 M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]=V4+||Wstar||^2+Nstar
               =C_rows+Nstar+Qbase.          (3.6)

This is precisely source(4.8). Its derivation accounts for the complete
matched source identity: the left and outer auxiliary terms, the signed
radial differences and the entire actual right H3 combine to this full
word identity. No term is assumed nonnegative merely because it disappeared
by exact algebraic cancellation. In particular (3.6) is not an inequality
obtained by throwing away the source Xi or the right child.

## 4. Exact sibling and local normals

Write TL=x-z=sum_(i<4)h_i*g_i and TR=x-zR=sum_(i<12)h_i*g_i. Also put

 L01=A*g4+B*g5+A*g6, L10=A*g8+B*g9+A*g10,
 Udiff=u-uR=(1+k)g3+L01+b*g7+L10,
 Zdiff=z-zR=L01+b*g7+L10+(1+k)g11.            (4.1)

The actual nonnegative combinations, with their ordered orientations, are

 Ng=-Q_(11,3)-Q_(3,11)
   =<Udiff,g3-g11>-||g3-g11||^2,

 Na=-E Q_(rR,rL)-J Q_(rL,rR)
   =<Zdiff,rL-rR>-E||rL||^2-J||rR||^2
                                      +(E+J)<rL,rR>,

 Nc=-Q_(rR,11)-J Q_(11,rR)
   =k||g11||^2+(J-k)<g11,rR>-J||rR||^2.       (4.2)

The weights E and J in Na are different: Na is the mass-balanced sum
<z-E*rL,rL-rR>+<zR-J*rR,rR-rL>. Its x terms cancel. The unweighted
sum instead contains(1/E-1/J)<x,rL-rR>; it is not used here.

Set the literal sibling coefficients

                   theta=d_i/k, omega=d_i,                (4.3)

and allow tau>=0 multiplying Nc. All three are nonnegative and theta,omega
are strictly positive. The obstructions below hold independently of tau;
in fact they also hold if theta,omega are any nonnegative numbers.

## 5. Mechanism1: changed physical-to-auxiliary initial attachments

This family was fixed in plan1bbce25036dd1237 before its coefficient
derivation. Choose 0<=wL,wR<=1+k and put

 DeltaA=wL(rL-g3)+wR(rR-g11),
 VmixA=Vmix+DeltaA, WA=Wstar-DeltaA,
 NA=2<x-M*p0,WA>.

The numerator VmixA gives weights h3-wL,h11-wR at g3,g11, the unchanged
other physical weights, weights wL,wR at rL,rR, and1+S at q. They are
nonnegative and sum to M, so VmixA/M is a feasible convex mixture and
NA is a nonnegative sum of original initial-maximizer normal rows.

Define the two additional ACTUAL normal quantities

 AL=<z-E*rL,rL-g3>=-E Q_(3,rL)>=0,
 AR=<zR-J*rR,rR-g11>=-J Q_(11,rR)>=0.          (5.1)

The changed initial normal satisfies Nstar=NA+2<x-M*p0,DeltaA>.
Extract2wL*AL+2wR*AR from this last term. Using z=x-TL,zR=x-TR,
the entire x coefficient cancels, leaving the EXACT quadratic

 Q1=Qbase
    +2wL<TL+E*rL-M*p0,rL-g3>
    +2wR<TR+J*rR-M*p0,rR-g11>
    -omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.               (5.2)

Thus the full endpoint equality is

 M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]
   =C_rows+NA+2wL*AL+2wR*AR
                      +omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Q1.         (5.3)

Every term outside Q1 is nonnegative on an actual history. A PSD Q1 on
all independent formal gradients would therefore be sufficient for H4.
There is no assumption that the changed square is still ||Wstar||^2:
equivalently, writing aL=TL+E*rL-Vmix and aR=TR+J*rR-Vmix gives

 Q1=||WA||^2+P_sq+Fdef-||DeltaA||^2
              +2wL<aL,rL-g3>+2wR<aR,rR-g11>
              -omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.                   (5.4)

The -||DeltaA||^2 is part of the full polynomial. Equations(5.2),(5.4)
agree by expanding WA=Wstar-DeltaA; neither conceals an auxiliary step.

Two diagnostic necessary conditions explicitly replace the old frozen
calculation. On the mandatory formal assignment g0=...=g15=p0=G!=0,
rL=rR=0, we have TL=ell*G,TR=(h+ell)*G and

 Q1=[2wL(M-ell)+2wR(M-h-ell)-tau*k]||G||^2.    (5.5)

A candidate that pays this required direction must satisfy

             2wL(M-ell)+2wR(M-h-ell)>=tau*k.               (5.6)

The new terms in(5.5) are retained; the old value -tau*k cannot be reused
as the new obstruction. This constraint allows positive tau: for example
wL=1+k,wR=0,tau=(1+k)(M-ell)/k makes(5.5) strictly positive.

With p0 and every physical gradient set to0, the complete auxiliary block is

 E(2wL+theta)||rL||^2+J(2wR+theta+tau)||rR||^2
                                  -theta(E+J)<rL,rR>.     (5.7)

Its diagonal entries are positive and its determinant condition is

 (2wL+theta)(2wR+theta+tau)
                         >=theta^2*(E+J)^2/(4EJ).          (5.8)

This retains the actual denominator mismatch E-J=2h. Neither(5.6) nor
(5.8) is asserted sufficient; the obstruction below holds even if both
are imposed as extra restrictions on the family.

For every parameter choice take the FORMAL scalar-direction assignment

 p0=rL=rR=g0=...=g14=G!=0, q=(1+v0)G.

Both attachment differences in(5.2) vanish. Ng,Na,Nc also vanish. The
only physical square that changes is the last H2 adjacent square, and
Wstar=-(1+S)v0*G. Consequently the WHOLE Q1 is

 Q1=[2Bterm*v0+Kterm*v0^2]||G||^2,
 Kterm=(1+S)^2+kapO*kap*e2+Bterm>0.           (5.9)

Taking v0=-Bterm/Kterm gives

                    Q1=-Bterm^2||G||^2/Kterm<0.            (5.10)

This excludes all wL,wR,tau in mechanism1, including any paying the old
test and the auxiliary block. The failure specifically calls for a change
that can affect the actual terminal-gradient contact. It does not prove
an actual H4 trajectory violates the endpoint law.

## 6. Mechanism2: all physical-to-terminal initial transfers

After the decisive(5.10), plan2331dae962e6ea64 fixed the second and last
family. Retain mechanism1 and choose fifteen nonnegative parameters t_i,
0<=i<15, subject to

       t_i+wL*1_(i=3)+wR*1_(i=11)<=h_i.                   (6.1)

The symbols t_i are attachment masses; scalar t=S2 remains unchanged.
Define

 Tsum=sum_(i<15)t_i, DeltaT=sum_(i<15)t_i(q-g_i),
 Delta=DeltaA+DeltaT, Vmix2=Vmix+Delta,
 W2=Wstar-Delta, N2=2<x-M*p0,W2>.

The new mixture weights are

 mu_i=h_i-t_i-wL*1_(i=3)-wR*1_(i=11), 0<=i<15,
 mu_q=1+S+Tsum, mu_L=wL, mu_R=wR.             (6.2)

They are nonnegative and sum exactly to M. In particular terminal mass
has increased by precisely Tsum, rather than being silently left at1+S.
For each physical i<15 include the ORIGINAL terminal normal

                  NT_i=<y-q,q-g_i>=-Q_(i,15)>=0.          (6.3)

Since y=x-Hvec,

 2<x-M*p0,DeltaT>-2sum t_i*NT_i
                      =2<Hvec+q-M*p0,DeltaT>.             (6.4)

This displays the entire changed x coefficient and its cancellation.
The full second-family equality is

 M[U_M(x)-U1(y)]
  =C_rows+N2+2wL*AL+2wR*AR+2sum t_i*NT_i
                         +omega*Ng+theta*Na+tau*Nc+Q2,

             Q2=Q1+2<Hvec+q-M*p0,DeltaT>.                 (6.5)

No maximizer has been replaced by an arbitrary feasible gradient. The
positive normal rows in(6.5) use the actual p0,rL,rR,q on the one body.
The candidate PSD test treats their formal vector coordinates independently,
as a sufficient identity must do before invoking those row inequalities.

For comparison with a literal new-mixture square, the whole expression is

 Q2=||W2||^2+P_sq+Fdef-||Delta||^2
    +2wL<TL+E*rL-Vmix,rL-g3>
    +2wR<TR+J*rR-Vmix,rR-g11>
    -2S<q,DeltaT>-omega*Ng-theta*Na-tau*Nc.                (6.6)

This keeps all cross terms of the two transfers through -||Delta||^2.
It is algebraically identical to(6.5); no unpriced square is discarded.

Here is the COMPLETE final ordered-row list in a single convention:
every entry below is a coefficient of -Q_(i,j). Start with the explicit
base array from Section3, then add

 (i,p0): 2M*mu_i, 0<=i<15;
 (15,p0): 2M*mu_q;
 (rL,p0): 2M*wL; (rR,p0): 2M*wR;
 (3,rL): 2wL*E;
 (11,rR): J*(2wR+tau); (rR,11): tau;
 (11,3): omega; (3,11): omega;
 (rR,rL): theta*E; (rL,rR): theta*J;
 (i,15): 2t_i, 0<=i<15.                     (6.7)

Coincident base and added pairs are summed; all other entries are zero.
All coefficients are nonnegative by(6.1)-(6.2). Formula(6.7) also gives
the first family on setting every t_i=0. It specifies the actual factor M
in the initial rows and the unequal factors E,J in the auxiliary rows.
No unspecified row redistribution or hidden signed multiplier is present.

## 7. Every formal quadratic coefficient

This section gives one entry formula for the entire matrix, rather than
only a selected principal restriction. Let the nineteen independent vector
coordinates have labels I={p,0,1,...,15,L,R}, where p means p0,L means rL,
R means rR, and15 means q. Let e^a be the nineteen-dimensional unit column
of label a; these columns are coefficient bookkeeping, not physical vectors.
For columns a,b define sym(a,b)=(a*b^T+b*a^T)/2. Set

 Hc=sum_(i<15)h_i e^i,
 wc=M*e^p-Hc-(1+S)e^15,
 tcL=sum_(i<4)h_i e^i, tcR=sum_(i<12)h_i e^i,
 acL=tcL+E*e^L-M*e^p, dcL=e^L-e^3,
 acR=tcR+J*e^R-M*e^p, dcR=e^R-e^11,
 bc=Hc+e^15-M*e^p,
 dcT=sum_(i<15)t_i(e^15-e^i),
 uc=sum_(i=3)^10 h_i e^i, dcG=e^3-e^11,
 zc=sum_(i=4)^11 h_i e^i, dcA=e^L-e^R.

Define the scalar diagonal column f by f_3=-d_i,f_7=d_i-d_o,
f_11=-kapO*d_i,f_15=Bterm and f_a=0 on every other label. Let

 Pmat=sum_(o in {0,4,8,12})rho_o sum_(j=0)^2
                 e_j(e^(o+j)-e^(o+j+1))(e^(o+j)-e^(o+j+1))^T,
 Gmat=sym(uc,dcG)-dcG*dcG^T,
 Amat=sym(zc,dcA)-E*e^L(e^L)^T-J*e^R(e^R)^T
                                    +(E+J)sym(e^L,e^R),
 Cmat=k*e^11(e^11)^T+(J-k)sym(e^11,e^R)-J*e^R(e^R)^T.

Here rho_o is the explicit four-entry list in Section3; e_j in Pmat are
the scalar H2 coefficients e0,e1,e2. Then the FULL second-family matrix is

 Qmat=wc*wc^T+Pmat+diag(f)
      +2wL*sym(acL,dcL)+2wR*sym(acR,dcR)+2sym(bc,dcT)
      -omega*Gmat-theta*Amat-tau*Cmat.                     (7.1)

For clarity, its coefficient at every ordered pair of labels alpha,beta is

 Qmat_(alpha,beta)=wc_alpha*wc_beta+Pmat_(alpha,beta)
   +f_alpha*1_(alpha=beta)
   +wL[(acL)_alpha*(dcL)_beta+(dcL)_alpha*(acL)_beta]
   +wR[(acR)_alpha*(dcR)_beta+(dcR)_alpha*(acR)_beta]
   +bc_alpha*(dcT)_beta+(dcT)_alpha*bc_beta
   -omega*[((uc)_alpha*(dcG)_beta+(dcG)_alpha*(uc)_beta)/2
                                      -(dcG)_alpha*(dcG)_beta]
   -theta*[((zc)_alpha*(dcA)_beta+(dcA)_alpha*(zc)_beta)/2
            -E*1_(alpha=L)*1_(beta=L)-J*1_(alpha=R)*1_(beta=R)
            +(E+J)(1_(alpha=L)*1_(beta=R)
                       +1_(alpha=R)*1_(beta=L))/2]
   -tau*[k*1_(alpha=11)*1_(beta=11)
          +(J-k)(1_(alpha=11)*1_(beta=R)
                       +1_(alpha=R)*1_(beta=11))/2
          -J*1_(alpha=R)*1_(beta=R)].                     (7.2)

In this formula

 Pmat_(alpha,beta)=sum_o rho_o sum_(j=0)^2 e_j
  [1_(alpha=o+j)-1_(alpha=o+j+1)]
  [1_(beta=o+j)-1_(beta=o+j+1)].

All h_i have been given literally in(2.1); all parameters and coefficient
columns have been assigned above. Thus(7.2) specifies each entry directly,
with no matrix to solve for. Diagonal norm coefficients are Qmat_(a,a);
the coefficient of <G_a,G_b> for a!=b is2Qmat_(a,b). First-family entries
are obtained by dcT=0. There is no restriction or quotient on coordinates.
If the original initial point x is appended as a twentieth formal coordinate,
its entire row and column in the remainder are ZERO: the x terms were
canceled explicitly in(5.1)-(5.2),(6.3)-(6.4), not set to0 by a test choice.

One additional contact check helps locate the second family's attempted
repair. At all nineteen coordinates equal to a common nonzero G, Q2=0.
A PSD quadratic must have zero first derivative there: expanding Q(e+tv)
and choosing either sign of sufficiently small t proves this necessity.
Differentiating the displayed complete polynomial gives

 wL=theta*h/(2ell),
 2(h+ell)wR=tau*(J+k)-theta*h,
 t_i=0 for i not in {3,7,11},
 S*t3=d_i+(omega-theta)h/2,
 S*t7=d_o-d_i,
 S*t11=kapO*d_i+(theta-omega)h/2,
                    S*Tsum=Bterm.                       (7.3)

Before substitution of the first two equalities, the g3,g11 equations are
-2d_i+2ell*wL-omega*h+2S*t3=0 and
-2kapO*d_i+2(h+ell)wR+omega*h-tau*(J+k)+2S*t11=0.
The rL,rR equations use E-J=2h, so the denominator mismatch remains in
these contact conditions as well. No claim that(7.3) satisfies the box
constraints or guarantees PSD is made. The next obstruction is stronger
and does not require any contact or auxiliary-block feasibility assumption.

## 8. Full-space obstruction for every parameter of mechanism2

Take any nonzero Euclidean vector G. Assign the physical formal gradients
block by block as follows:

 g0=g1=g2=g3=G,
 g4=g5=g6=g7=0,
 g8=g9=g10=g11=G,
 g12=g13=g14=g15=q=0,
                         rL=rR=G.                       (8.1)

Let Iplus={0,1,2,3,8,9,10,11} and

                 Tplus=sum_(i in Iplus)t_i.

For every allowed attachment tuple choose the remaining formal coordinate

                    p0=(2ell-Tplus)G/M.                  (8.2)

This specifies a vector in the full nineteen-coordinate space, not a
projected test on selected coefficients. Evaluate EVERY term of(6.5):

 * Each H2 block is constant on its four gradients, hence P_sq=0.
 * g3=g11=rL=rR=G, so both auxiliary attachment differences vanish.
 * g3-g11=0 gives Ng=0. rL-rR=0 gives Na=0.
 * g11=rR gives Nc=0, also directly from k+(J-k)-J=0.
 * The actual displacement FORMULAS give Hvec=2ell*G. No gradient
   coordinate or step has been omitted in computing this vector.
 * Fdef=-(1+kapO)d_i||G||^2, since g7=q=0.
 * DeltaT=-Tplus*G, Wstar=-Tplus*G=DeltaT.
 * Hvec+q-M*p0=Tplus*G=-Wstar.

Consequently the complete remainder, with no other terms left uncomputed, is

                  Q2=-[(1+kapO)d_i+Tplus^2]||G||^2<0.     (8.3)

The strict inequality uses only d_i>0 and kapO>0. It holds for every
wL,wR,t_i,tau satisfying the nonnegative-row requirements, regardless of
(5.6),(5.8),(7.3). It is even independent of theta and omega. At t_i=0
it also gives Q1=-(1+kapO)d_i||G||^2, strengthening the first-family
obstruction. This strengthening was found while examining mechanism2;
it is not a third mechanism.

Equivalently the changed mixture square is W2=0 on(8.1)-(8.2). Direct
terminal attachment consumes an additional Tplus^2 instead of paying the
two remaining negative off-root terms. This explains why solving the
common-gradient contact equations alone cannot repair the whole matrix.

The assignment is FORMAL, not a projection history. In particular it is
not asserted that its chosen auxiliary gradients equal projections of
the corresponding formal inputs. Such constraints are precisely the
nonnegative original rows already allocated in the attempted identity.
A sufficient PSD remainder must be nonnegative before those row signs
are invoked, on all independent formal gradients. Thus(8.3) disproves
PSD sufficiency for these two families and nothing more. We do not
evaluate the full endpoint gap on this non-actual assignment or infer
an actual H4 adversary from it.

## 9. Mandatory actual all-core history, retaining the right H3

Take D0=[-1,1], x=1/100 and Qc=a^2*beta. The bounds a>1/3,beta>9/10,
a<u0 imply1/10<Qc<1/5. Every prefix core multiplier has magnitude at
most(5/2)^2*7=175/4<100: only the two inner bridges k and the outer K
can expand, and all other factors have magnitude a or beta less than1.
Induction on the literal word therefore keeps every physical query and
the terminal in(-1,1). This is a symbolic guard, not an executed trajectory.
All auxiliary denominators are at least1, so all auxiliary projections
are interior too. The exact physical landmarks are

 u=-Qc*x, z=k*Qc*x, v=-k*Qc^2*x, w=-K*v,
 uR=-Qc*w, zR=k*Qc*w, y=-k*Qc^2*w
                                  =-K*(k*Qc^2)^2*x.       (9.1)

The entire actual right H3 is in this formula. Gradients equal their
inputs, and p0=x/M,rL=z/E,rO=w/D,rR=zR/J,q=y,qL=v/eta.
Every original physical and auxiliary normal row vanishes, including
ALL added initial, auxiliary, sibling, local and terminal rows. Thus(5.3)
and(6.5) both reduce exactly to

                     Q1=Q2=x^2-M*y^2>0.                 (9.2)

To see the strict sign, k*Qc^2<1/10,K<7,M<71 imply
M*(y/x)^2<3479/10000<1. No estimate is applied to a reset right child.

Here is that right child in its FULL retained accepted H3 expansion. Define
R(X,N,Z)=N||P(X/N)-Z||^2+2<X-NP(X/N),P(X/N)-Z>. Put

 RPr=R(w,D,(w-zR+(J+ell)rR)/D),
 RRr=R(zR,J,(zR-y+C*q)/J),
 B_r=C^2*uR^2-(zR+ell*zR/J)^2,
 V10=(1-C^2*Qc^2)w^2,
 V11=(1-C^2*Qc^2)zR^2.

Accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 PartB(6.3), on the actual right H3, states

 G_R=U_D(w)-U1(y)=w^2/D-y^2
                     =V10/D+V11/J+B_r/D+RPr+RRr.         (9.3)

Here D*RPr=ell^2*zR^2/J^2 and J*RRr=t^2*y^2. The two LEFT H2 energies
are similarly(1-C^2*Qc^2)x^2 and(1-C^2*Qc^2)z^2. They are retained,
not declared zero. The right terminal radial term is exactly zero because
d=1 and q=P(y); the other radial/normal terms are included in the full
identity(3.6) by the accepted maximization equality. No isolated Xi is
used as an endpoint estimate.

## 10. Mandatory inherited cap/cap history, with actual right clamps

Take ONE body D0=[-r,1], r=k/C, and x=C. The first H2 has constant
gradient1 and points C-T>=1 for prefix mass T in[0,t], ending at u=1.
Then z=-k. The second H2 has constant gradient-r and points-k+T*r<=-r,
ending at v=-r. Thus V00=V01=P00=P01=0 and w=K*r.

No face pattern is assigned to the right child. Define the scalar clamp
P_r(X)=max(-r,min(X,1)) and T_l(X)=X-l*P_r(X). Its complete ACTUAL
remaining history, on the SAME body and SAME inherited parameters, is

 x9=T_A(w), x10=T_B(x9), uR=T_A(x10),
 g11=P_r(uR), zR=uR-(1+k)g11,
 x13=T_A(zR), x14=T_B(x13), y=T_A(x14), q=P_r(y).           (10.1)

These are unevaluated exact expressions for original steps, not a numerical
or arithmetic trajectory replay. The full retained right-child quantities are

 V10=w^2-(uR+t*g11)^2, V11=zR^2-(y+t*q)^2,
 Theta=ell[C*J-k(C+J+k)]/(J+k),
 B_r=Theta(g11-rR)^2
    +2ell(1+C/(J+k))(uR-g11)(g11-rR)
    +2ell*C/(J+k)(zR-J*rR)(rR-g11),
 RPr=R(w,D,(w-zR+(J+ell)rR)/D),
 RRr=R(zR,J,(zR-y+C*q)/J),
 G_R=U_D(w)-U1(y)=V10/D+V11/J+B_r/D+RPr+RRr>=0.          (10.2)

Equation(10.2), including the sign and every actual remainder, is exactly
the accepted H3 law and identity at d=1. It is not an unverified stronger
transfer assertion or a substituted cap child. The radial term at the right
terminal is zero precisely because q=P_r(y), not because it was discarded.

The auxiliary values are

 p0=C/M, rL=-k/E, rO=w/D, qL=-r/eta, rR=zR/J.              (10.3)

For the first four expressions interiority follows from E>C,M>C,
1/3<r<1/2 and0<w<7/2<D. For rR, every H2 step l lies in(0,2), and
projection optimality against0 gives

 |X-lP_r(X)|^2<=|X|^2-l(2-l)|P_r(X)|^2<=|X|^2.

Thus |uR|<=w<7/2. If uR<=-r, zR=uR+(1+k)r is between-5/2 and k*r;
if-r<=uR<=1, zR=-k*uR is between-k and k*r;
if uR>=1, zR=uR-(1+k) is between-k and1/2. Therefore

 -5/2<zR<5/4, J=2C-1>10,
                     rR=zR/J in(-1/4,1/8) subset(-r,1).

Hence the initial and auxiliary normal residuals in our two changed
identities vanish: N2=NA=AL=AR=Na=0. The remaining actual added terms are

 Ng=(uR-g11)(g11-1)>=0,
 Nc=(uR-g11)(g11-zR/J)>=0,
 NT_i=(y-q)(q-g_i)>=0.                       (10.4)

The last terms retain the ACTUAL terminal q from(10.1). Neither Ng,Nc
nor NT_i is assumed zero. The full second-family identity becomes

 C^2-M*U1(y)=C_rows+omega*Ng+tau*Nc+2sum t_i*NT_i+Q2.      (10.5)

The first family's specialization sets t_i=0. On the other hand retaining
the whole actual right H3 gives exactly

              C^2-M*U1(y)=C^2-(M/D)w^2+M*G_R>0.          (10.6)

For its sign, h=s+1+K<D since s>11>K, so M/D<3. With K<25/4,k<5/2,
C>11/2,

 (M/D)w^2/C^2=K^2*k^2*M/(C^4*D)
                                <46875/58564<1.

Combining this with the full(10.2) proves the stated positive endpoint gap.
We do NOT infer that Q2 itself is positive on this cap history after
subtracting arbitrary added nonnegative rows. This actual-history check
and the formal PSD obstructions are logically different assertions.

## 11. Remaining interface and bounded closeout

The new algebra identifies a stricter unpaid direction than the previous
canonical-mixture obstruction. Initial physical-to-auxiliary transfer can
change the old common-gradient test and the unequal-denominator auxiliary
block. Terminal transfer can change the common-gradient contact derivative.
Nevertheless the full direction(8.1)-(8.2) annihilates all of those auxiliary
and sibling corrections and all four H2 reserves, leaving the strictly
negative original inner-join defects. The direct terminal transfer adds
a negative square on that direction.

Any later construction continuing from these identities must change at least
one of the features annihilated by(8.1), for example by a fully specified
redistribution of original join rows or by other attachments/normal rows
that do not vanish there. This is a necessary design warning for these
templates, not an assertion that such a change suffices or that arbitrary
initial attachments are impossible. No such third family was attempted.

No sufficient H4 identity has been obtained. Accordingly there is no
quantitatively priced recursion, no absolute C0 or new threshold, and no
all-d/all-depth conclusion. The original m>=1536 and W=2R remain unchanged.
The only accepted positive ingredient used at higher child level remains
fact1e0bf3f41457d8e4 at its actual H3/all-d scope.

Recall: TASK, applicable master_guidance, own local notes, shared findings
and dead ends, and the complete source ec8a052084a6ce2e were read. The source

164e6c19e75797df61f02ba8bd218c56bbab60ef91a2598a44b16efb743244dd,
