# Global optimum-load moments and an endpoint-metric transport obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, allocated invocation84.
Original exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No fact submission or new fact.

This is one joint argument for independent verification, not a chain of
accepted intermediate results. TASK.md and master_guidance53db4a56c9e0379b
authorize one invocation, at most two substantive mechanisms, and zero
mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays,
optimizers, solvers, formal calls, installations, consults or subagents.
Those counts remain zero. Only textual mathematics and administrative
reading, writing, retrieval and hashing were used.

## 1. Outcomes and limits

Mechanism I strengthens the previous two literal-skew exclusions. For the
ENTIRE family

    w_i=kappa*u_i, alpha_i=(1-p)u_i/U,
    d=tau*(rho-e_0), kappa>0, tau>=0,

no balanced skew re-selection can produce a full certificate at
q<=qtar=17/(16D). The total incoming-optimum load forces tau>p; its upper
clock moment and the exact global PSD charge then require u_0>9p^2/80.
The actual same-enclosing-m word has u_0<p^2/64. This excludes EVERY
skew A with Ae=d, not just the two skews in the prior report.

Mechanism II changes that invariant cost. It has explicit positive weights
proportional to e_0+e_z+p^2*rho, with the scale fixed by a displayed clock
variance; its imbalance minimizes global energy for its clock moment.
A literal skew map makes EVERY incoming-optimum load zero. All outgoing
terminal and outgoing-optimum multipliers are positive, and the entire
original-vector polynomial is PSD at qtar with strict slack. Its finite
first-row SUM is negative, as required. Nevertheless its finite first-row
CLOCK moment is impossible for nonpositive off-diagonal N entries. This
failure survives every additional skew C with Ce=Ct=0. Thus this is an
explicit changed-metric construction and a precise whole-law obstruction,
not a proposed feasible certificate.

Neither result excludes other metrics, other terminal probabilities, other
imbalance laws, or the full dual cone. In particular, the second obstruction
fixes the incoming load to zero: changing it can change the row clock.
No negative signed coefficient or formal negative vector is an interpolable
primal function. No full upper bound on W and no benchmark improvement is
proved here. Accepted931's missing full-function hypothesis remains open.

## 2. Original problem, accepted scopes, notation

For L>0 and a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the
supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite
Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable L-smooth f with an
attained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original question asks
whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h).
Its benchmark is U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, retaining every integer split:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the residual orientation, the full
independent-vector certificate interpretation and the exact reduction
deleting incoming edges only at the ACTUAL unqueried terminal. It deletes
no queried-prefix row. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the word below,
its mass formula, and B_n/D<47/50. Only CONDITIONAL on the missing
full-class upper W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D), for every m>=1536, does it imply
B_n W_n(H_m)<799/800 and a negative resolution of the original problem.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. Use the SAME enclosing parameters at
every child:

    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=ell(1+eps), a=beta/c, b=1+a,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, z=n-1,
    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, Aword=1+S, D=1+2S,
    p=1/Aword, qtar=17/(16D).

Here p is the optimum probability, not a convergence exponent. The actual
terminal is index n, different from the final queried index z. Every
queried index 0,...,z remains. The hierarchy gives
h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing positive j.
The final three steps are (b,1+beta,b). Elementary bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2, 4/3<b<3/2.       (2.1)

For example 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12, eps<=1/6144, and beta>7/8 imply these
bounds. With vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in (0,1), the exact mass formula is

    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j.

It follows either from accepted931 or by substitution in
1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, with initial value1. Therefore

    2^m<Aword<c^m, c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<1/128.              (2.2)

## 3. Exact tail quantities and uniform analytic bounds

Let sbit(L) denote the binary digit sum of nonnegative L. Write

    r_L=product_(j=n-L)^(n-1)(h_j-1), 0<=L<=n, r_0=1.

Counting backward, h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j). Binary expansion gives
sum_(j=1)^L v2(j)=sum_(r>=1)floor(L/2^r)=L-sbit(L). Hence

    r_L=beta^L*c^(-sbit(L)), s=r_n=beta^n/c^m.          (3.1)

Define the original terminal coefficients and their positive magnitudes:

    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),
    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.

For L=z-i, the sum of two consecutive positive suffix products gives
u_i=r_L+r_(L+1), and k_i=(-1)^i u_i. In particular

    u_z=b, u_(z-1)=a(1+beta), u_(z-2)=b*a*beta,
    u_0=(b/a)s, U>7/3.                                (3.2)

For the last bound, the three displayed terminal weights exceed
4/3,5/8,7/18, respectively, whose sum169/72 exceeds7/3.
The finite binary product identity is

    Z=sum_(L=0)^n r_L=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),
    U=2Z-1-s.                                        (3.3)

We will need a stronger small-source estimate than the prior report:

    u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.                      (3.4)

Here is a fully symbolic all-m proof. For integers m>=16,
2^m-1>16m^2: at16,65535>4096; induction follows from
2(16m^2)+1>16(m+1)^2 for m>=3. Also
beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.
Consequently beta^n<2^(-4m). Since b/a=1+1/a<4 and p>c^(-m),

    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.

The final inequality already holds for m>=8, since5/32<1/2.
Equation(3.2) and p<1/128 then imply rho_0<1/10. Also
s/p=beta^n*Aword/c^m<beta^n<1, proving s<p.

Both mechanisms use the actual terminal probabilities

    alpha_star=p, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i,
    delta=(1-p)/U, v=k-alpha,
    gamma=2p/(p+s), eta=(p-s)/(p+s)=gamma-1.

Thus 1<gamma<2, eta>0, every alpha_i>0, and their sum with p is1.
Since delta<3/7, v_0=(1-delta)u_0>0 and v_0<u_0.
Let e be the n-dimensional all-ones column and e_i its coordinate columns.
Define the chronological clock and its terminal-law mean by

    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, tbar=sum_i rho_i t_i,
    Tlast=t_z=1/p-b, DeltaClock=Tlast-1=1/p-b-1,
    Uclock=t^T v.

The letter T below denotes a matrix; Tlast denotes a scalar. Telescoping
gives t^T k=S, so

    Uclock=S-(1-p)tbar=(1-p)(1/p-tbar)>0.              (3.5)

In particular 1<tbar<Tlast<1/p. We will use

    32<Uclock<512m^3, p*Uclock^2<1/1024.              (3.6)

For the lower bound put M=sum_(L=0)^n L r_L. Differentiating the finite
binary product, or summing each digit contribution, gives

    M/Z=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) 2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).

Choose j with m/2<2^j<=m; j<m. Bernoulli's inequality gives
beta^(2^j)>=1-2^j/(4m)>=3/4, and c+beta^(2^j)<7/2. Thus M/Z>3m/28.
Further ns<n/c^m<1. The positive sum sum_(L<n)L(r_L+r_(L+1)) exceeds
M-ns>M-1, while U<2Z. Hence

    sum_i rho_i(z-i)>(M/Z-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.

The sum of the last L+1 physical steps is at least b+L, and (3.5) is
(1-p) times its rho-weighted mean. Since 1-p>1/2 and3m/56>80, this
proves Uclock>32.

For the upper bound let J=L+1>=1 and choose the least d with2^d>J.
Then d<=m, 2^d<=2J, and those last J steps lie in the final H_d.
Their sum is at most S_d<c^d<(2^d)^2<=4J^2. Because
u_i<2 beta^L and U>2, their rho-weighted mean is smaller than

    4 sum_(L>=0)(L+1)^2 beta^L
      =4(1+beta)/(1-beta)^3<512m^3.

The geometric-series identity follows by twice differentiating its
convergent power series, or by multiplying by(1-beta)^3. No sequence
was evaluated by a program. Finally,2^m>2^30 m^6 for every m>=128:
at128 it compares2^128 with2^72; the induction step uses
(1+1/m)^6<=sum_(j>=0)(6/m)^j<=64/61<2.
Together with p<2^(-m), this even gives p Uclock^2<2^(-12), which
implies the weaker bound in(3.6).

## 4. Complete original-coordinate factor and signed array, jointly derived

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep the vectors
x_0,g_0,...,g_n arbitrary and independent, and substitute only the
actual update x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j. For original samples use

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Actual interpolable jets have Q_ij<=0. For i<n set y_i=x_i-g_i and
Delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2; star quantities vanish. Let T be lower triangular
with T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise. Then

    y=e*x_0-Tg, g=T^(-1)(e*x_0-y), t=Te.             (4.1)

This is an invertible change on the entire original space. Explicitly,
with pi_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j),

    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i
          +sum_(j<i)h_j product_(j<r<i)(1-h_r)y_j.

The update x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i y_i now gives

    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y, e^T k=1+s, T^T k=h.

For the last identity telescope each suffix:
k_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i. Thus e^T v=p+s. Set

    Cprob_i=p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j,
    R_i=h_i Cprob_i-alpha_i=(T^T v)_i>0.

Strict positivity follows from
R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i. Also

    R_0=bp+a alpha_0<2p,
    H=p+s-v_0=p+alpha_0-s/a, 0<H<2p.                (4.2)

These use(3.4); the lower bound H>p-u_0 is positive. The actual terminal
center is V=-s*x_0+v^T y. In particular the free original terminal
gradient has not been identified with a queried gradient.

For any positive detail weights w_i, Ww=sum_i w_i, define

    K=diag(w)-w w^T/Ww, tbar_w=sum_i w_i t_i/Ww.

Let d be any real column with e^T d=0, and A any skew matrix with Ae=d.
The general form is A=(d w^T-w d^T)/Ww+B with B skew and Be=0.
Define

    P=K+gamma v v^T, N=T^T(P+A).

Every finite scalar entry is explicitly

    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(r>i)(K_rj+A_rj)+gamma R_i v_j. (4.3)

The FULL signed array on the original indices is

    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,z},
    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i=2p R_i+(T^T d)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i
            =(Kt)_i+gamma Uclock v_i-(At)_i-2alpha_i. (4.4)

Diagonal multipliers are zero. Terminal signs pass for our alpha, but
none of the other signs is presumed. The outgoing prefix sum at i is
N_ii and the incoming prefix sum is N_ii-2alpha_i. Adding the actual
terminal edge2alpha_i gives zero total divergence at i. Terminal
divergence is2; optimum divergence is-2. Hence sampled values cancel in

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij.

For prefix indices Q_ij=Delta_i-Delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>. Their remaining
quadratic term is -g^T N y. Inserting(4.1), using e^T K=0,
e^T A=-d^T and e^T P=2p v^T, gives
-<x_0,2p v^T y-d^T y>+y^T P y. Together with -2f_n, the actual outgoing
terminal rows give ||g_n-V||^2-||V||^2-2sum alpha_i Delta_i. The value terms
cancel as stated. Completing the common square therefore gives

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2
       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.                (4.5)

Let ybar_w=sum_i w_i y_i/Ww and J=(1/4)sum_i d_i^2/w_i. Since sum d=0,
the FULL factor is

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2
       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(d_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2
       +eta||v^T y-(p+s)x_0||^2
       +(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2.                         (4.6)

The initial charge J is paid ONCE globally. All detail directions, the
common square and its real derivative, and the actual terminal square
remain. Full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J. For necessity set scalar
x_0=1, y_i=c0-d_i/(2w_i), where
c0=1+[sum_i v_i d_i/(2w_i)]/(p+s), and set g_n=p. The terminal, detail
and common squares in(4.6) vanish; reconstruct every queried g_i with(4.1). This gives
M(q)=q-p^2-J. It is a formal-space test, not an interpolable instance.

For the actual common-gradient direction g_i=G at all queried indices,
y_i=x_0-t_i G. The full prefix quadratic after deleting only its terminal
square is

    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-(d^T t)<x_0,G>
                 +(t^T Kt+eta Uclock^2)||G||^2.      (4.7)

Thus the real initial/common derivative is retained, and skew transport
cannot remove it.

## 5. Global load moments, exact skew completion and first-row moments

Write M0=e^T nu and M1=t^T nu. From(4.4), Ke=0, e^T v=p+s,
e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0 give EXACTLY

    M0=2p Uclock+d^T t-2(1-p),
    M1=t^T Kt+gamma Uclock^2-2t^T alpha.              (5.1)

Any nonnegative incoming load must obey

    M0>=0, M1>=0, M0<=M1<=Tlast*M0.                  (5.2)

These are necessary screens for the full array, not sufficient signs.
They are invariant under every balanced skew re-selection keeping d.
For the isolated load problem they are also sufficient: the endpoint
column

    nu_target=((Tlast*M0-M1)/DeltaClock)e_0
                   +((M1-M0)/DeltaClock)e_z          (5.3)

is nonnegative and has precisely those two moments. This does not assert
that the induced finite interpolation multipliers have the needed signs.

Here is a literal skew completion for ANY specified nonnegative load with
the compatible moments. Put F=Kt+gamma Uclock v, r=F-2alpha-nu_target,
tmean_e=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean_e e, V_e=cvec^T cvec>0, and

    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,
    fvec=r-tmean_e d+(d^T t)e/n,
    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/V_e.              (5.4)

Compatibility gives e^T r=-d^T t and t^T r=0. Consequently
e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Direct multiplication now proves Ae=d
and At=r, with both displayed terms skew. Thus(4.4) yields precisely
nu_target. Every entry is a displayed formula in the original indices;
there is no unknown feasible matrix or LP. Any other skew with the same
actions on e,t differs by C with Ce=Ct=0. Such C preserves the factor
and the incoming AND outgoing optimum loads, but may change finite rows.

The first row has two further necessary invariants. Define

    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,
    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,
    G0=P0-DeltaClock*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-Tlast)N_0j.

The first row of T^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Hence
N_00=-a K_00-b d_0+gamma R_0 v_0 and

    S0=a K_00+gamma R_0 H+d_0.                       (5.5)

To obtain the clock row, use
N_0j=-a K_0j+gamma R_0 v_j-b d_j-a A_0j.
Multiplication by(t_j-1) and substitution of At=F-2alpha-nu give

    P0=-2a(Kt)_0+gamma Uclock(R_0-a v_0)-2p R_0
                 -b(d^T t)+2a alpha_0+a nu_0+a d_0. (5.6)

In particular, C e=C t=0 cannot alter S0 or P0. If every finite first-row
multiplier is nonnegative, N_0j<=0, then the complete two-moment screen is

    S0<=0, DeltaClock*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                 (5.7)

Indeed b=t_1-1<=t_j-1<=DeltaClock. Its lower inequality is G0>=0.
When S0=0 it forces all off-diagonal entries to vanish, not just their sum.

## 6. Mechanism I: whole absolute-tail law, every balanced skew excluded

For arbitrary kappa>0 and tau>=0 fix

    w_i=kappa u_i, alpha_i=(1-p)rho_i,
    d=tau(rho-e_0),
    A=tau(rho e_0^T-e_0 rho^T)+B,
    B^T=-B, Be=0.                                  (6.1)

Here B is universally quantified for an obstruction, not offered as an
unknown sign-feasible construction. Every skew with Ae=d is included.
The exact single charge and clock variance are

    J=tau^2(1-rho_0)/(4kappa u_0),
    Vtail=sum_i u_i(t_i-tbar)^2,
    t^T Kt=kappa Vtail, d^T t=tau(tbar-1).           (6.2)

The charge follows by separately summing i=0 and i>0 in(4.6).
Equation(3.5) and pS=1-p now reduce(5.1) to

    M0=tau(tbar-1)-2p(1-p)tbar,
    M1=kappa Vtail+gamma Uclock^2-2(1-p)tbar.        (6.3)

Nonnegative nu requires

    tau>=2p(1-p)tbar/(tbar-1)>p.                    (6.4)

The strict inequality uses tbar>1 and p<1/2. The upper clock constraint
M1<=Tlast*M0, together with pTlast=1-bp, is

    kappa Vtail<=tau Tlast(tbar-1)
                     +2bp(1-p)tbar-gamma Uclock^2
                 <tau/p^2+3.                      (6.5)

Here Tlast,tbar<1/p,2b<3, and the dropped last term is nonpositive.
The other moment requirements in(5.2) still apply, but are not needed
for this contradiction.

For any center tbar, retaining only the adjacent final two terms gives

    Vtail>= [u_z u_(z-1)/(u_z+u_(z-1))]
                             (t_z-t_(z-1))^2>1.    (6.6)

This two-term minimum follows by completing its scalar square. Indeed
u_z>4/3,u_(z-1)>5/8 and t_z-t_(z-1)=1+beta>15/8 give the lower bound
(20/47)*(225/64)=4500/3008>1.

Suppose full PSD holds at q<=qtar. Since
qtar/p=17/[16(2-p)]<1, equation(4.6) implies

    kappa>=tau^2(1-rho_0)/(4p u_0).

Combine this with(6.5), divide by the positive tau, and use tau>p:

    (1-rho_0)Vtail/(4u_0)
      <tau(1-rho_0)Vtail/(4p u_0)
      <1/p^2+3/tau<1/p^2+3/p<2/p^2.

Consequently u_0>(1-rho_0)Vtail p^2/8>9p^2/80, contradicting(3.4).
This covers all m>=1536, all kappa>0,tau>=0, and EVERY balanced B.

For completeness, the outgoing optimum signs in this entire family pass.
Let F_i^u=u_i+h_i sum_(j>i)u_j>0. Then

    mu_0=2pR_0+a tau(1-rho_0)>0,
    mu_i=2pR_i+tau F_i^u/U>0 for i>0.

All terminal signs also pass. The contradiction is precisely between
incoming-optimum nonnegativity and the full global PSD charge. It does
not rely on either literal transport rejected previously, a finite-row
deletion, or a primal construction. Allowing tau<0 cannot save the family:
M0 in(6.3) would already be strictly negative.

## 7. Exact two-moment imbalance price

Before selecting the changed metric, record the exact charge for arbitrary
positive weights. Prescribe d_0=-r0, sum_i d_i=0 and d^T t=theta. Put

    Wplus=sum_(i>0)w_i, tplus=sum_(i>0)w_i t_i/Wplus,
    Vplus=sum_(i>0)w_i(t_i-tplus)^2>0.

The inequality Vplus>0 holds here because there are at least two distinct
noninitial clocks with positive weights. The unique minimum-energy choice
on i>0 is

    d_i=w_i[r0/Wplus
                  +(theta-r0(tplus-1))(t_i-tplus)/Vplus]. (7.1)

It has sum r0 and clock sum theta+r0 on i>0. If any other feasible
column differs by e_i^res on i>0, that difference has zero sum and zero
clock moment. Its weighted inner product with(7.1) vanishes, so the
energy increases by sum_(i>0)(e_i^res)^2/w_i. The minimum global charge is

    Jmin=r0^2/(4K_00)
                +(theta-r0(tplus-1))^2/(4Vplus).     (7.2)

The first term includes BOTH source and compensating mass:
1/K_00=1/w_0+1/Wplus. Thus paying the source alone misses a second
potentially large clock cost.

If d_0 is also selected to minimize energy at fixed theta, weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz gives the exact law

    d=(theta/(t^T Kt))Kt,
    J=theta^2/(4t^T Kt).                            (7.3)

Indeed Kt has entries w_i(t_i-tbar_w), and their squared inverse-weight
sum is t^T Kt. This attains the global lower bound and, for its resulting
d_0, also attains(7.2). This is the choice used next.

## 8. Mechanism II: explicit source/end metric and zero incoming load

Keep exactly the probabilities of Section3. Abbreviate Cclock=Uclock
within this section only, and choose

    theta=2(1-p)-2p Cclock=2p(1-p)tbar,
    Xreq=2(1-p)tbar-gamma Cclock^2
                         =theta/p-gamma Cclock^2,
    omega=p^2,
    what_i=1_(i=0)+1_(i=z)+omega rho_i,
    tbase=sum_i what_i t_i/(2+omega),
    Vbase=sum_i what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,
    ellw=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=ellw*what_i,
    K=diag(w)-w w^T/sum w,
    d=(theta/Xreq)Kt,
    nu_target=0.                                   (8.1)

These are actual scalar coefficient formulas from the original word.
In the suggested four-component parametrization they are
kappa_tail=ellw*omega/U, kappa_mass=0,
kappa_start=kappa_end=ellw. No coefficient is a matrix-feasibility oracle.

Bounds(3.6) give 1<theta<2 and

    0<gamma p Cclock^2/theta<1/512,
    (511/512)theta/p<Xreq<2/p.                      (8.2)

For theta>1 use p<1/128 and
pCclock<(p Cclock^2)/32<1/32768. Both endpoint weights of what are
at least1 at distinct clocks, so Vbase>0. Hence ellw>0 and ALL original
detail weights are positive. The identities t^T Kt=Xreq, sum d=0,
d^T t=theta and(7.3) are exact. In(5.1) both load moments are zero.
The identically zero nu_target is therefore a nonnegative load with the
required moments. Use the literal skew(5.4), with r=Kt+gamma Cclock v-2alpha,
and choose its remaining skew correction to be zero. This specifies
every entry of A and, through(4.3)--(4.4), every original multiplier.

It is useful to admit ANY extra skew C with Ce=Ct=0 when auditing this
fixed law: the coming obstruction covers all of them. Such C changes
neither the selected load nor the charge, and is not a proposed repair.

### 8.1 Full PSD and all optimum signs pass

The full charge is J=theta^2/(4Xreq), with no source-tail inverse loss.
Equations(8.2) give

    p^2+J<p^2+(256/511)p
            <(33/64)p<(17/32)p<qtar.               (8.3)

For the middle bound use p<1/128 and
1/128+256/511<33/64, equivalent to32768<33215.
The last bound follows from qtar/p=17/[16(2-p)]>17/32.
Consequently(4.6) is full PSD at the assigned target, with strict slack.
This construction really changes the failed invariant cost in Mechanism I.

Every terminal outgoing multiplier is positive; incoming terminal ones
vanish as required. Every incoming-optimum multiplier is exactly zero.
Every outgoing-optimum multiplier is positive as well. To see this, the
sign of d_i is the sign of t_i-tbar_w. These signs change only once, from
negative to positive; d_0<0<d_z. Every proper prefix sum of d is negative:
before the crossing all terms are nonpositive, and after the crossing the
remaining suffix is positive. Thus every suffix sum after i is positive
for i<z. If d_i<0, the earlier prefix is nonpositive, so
sum_(j>i)d_j>=-d_i and

    (T^T d)_i=d_i+h_i sum_(j>i)d_j
                           >=(h_i-1)(-d_i)>0.

If d_i>=0 the same expression is positive, including i=z because d_z>0.
Therefore mu_i=2pR_i+(T^T d)_i>0 at every original query.
The remaining finite signs must still be checked; PSD and these optimum
signs do not establish a certificate.

### 8.2 The finite first-row sum passes

Put Delta=DeltaClock, Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbar_w-1)>0. Then
d_0=-theta Y/Xreq. Since

    K_00/Y=[1+omega(1-rho_0)]
              /[Delta(1+omega rbar)],
    rbar=sum_i rho_i(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1],

we have K_00/Y<=(1+omega)/Delta. Now
Delta>3/(4p), omega<1/16384<1/8 and a<1/2 give
a(1+omega)/Delta<3p/4, while theta/Xreq>p. Hence

    S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+gamma R_0H
                         <-pY/4+8p^2.              (8.4)

For an explicit lower bound on Y, define xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1] and

    gbase=Vbase/Delta^2,
    fbase=(1+omega rho_0)(1+omega rbar)/(2+omega).

Retaining the two unit endpoint masses gives gbase>=1/2; centering at1/2
gives gbase<=1/2+omega/4<3/4. Also fbase>=1/(2+omega)>1/3.
Since Y=(Xreq/Delta)(fbase/gbase) and Xreq/Delta>511/512>3/4,
we get Y>1/3>1/4. Thus S0<-p/16+8p^2<0 because p<1/128.
The first-row total load is affordable and has the correct sign.

### 8.3 The finite first-row clock fails, for every Ce=Ct=0 correction

Write K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. The same scaled-weight
expressions give the uniform estimates

    |eY|<=4Xreq*omega/Delta,
    |eK|<=2Xreq*omega/Delta^2.                       (8.5)

To check them directly, 1/2-omega/4<=fbase<=1/2+omega, while
1/2<=gbase<=1/2+omega/4. Also

    K_00/ellw=
      [1+omega+omega^2 rho_0(1-rho_0)]/(2+omega)
                            lies in[1/2,1/2+omega/2].

Subtract gbase and use ellw=Xreq/(Delta^2 gbase)<=2Xreq/Delta^2.
These even give tighter estimates than(8.5).

Use(5.5)--(5.6), d^Tt=theta, d_0=-theta Y/Xreq and nu_0=0.
The endpoint-clock defect G0=P0-Delta*S0 is exactly

    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,
    Gendpoint=a(Xreq-Tlast*theta)/Delta
       +gamma R_0[Cclock-Tlast(p+s)+Delta v_0]
       -a gamma Cclock v_0+2a alpha_0,
    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)
                         +theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.    (8.6)

For the first line expand(5.6) and then substitute the two definitions
of eY,eK. Since Xreq<2/p, Delta>1/(2p), theta<2, a<1/2 and Delta>a,
(8.5) gives

    Epert<=|Epert|<28 omega=28p^2.                  (8.7)

Specifically the first term is at most10a Xreq omega/Delta<20omega,
and the second at most4theta omega<8omega.

The exact relation Xreq=theta/p-gamma Cclock^2 and
Tlast=1/p-b gives

    Xreq-Tlast*theta=b theta-gamma Cclock^2.

Using a>1/3,gamma>1,Delta<1/p, and dropping the negative terms
-gamma R_0 Tlast(p+s) and -a gamma Cclock v_0 in(8.6), we obtain

    Gendpoint<3p-(p/3)Cclock^2+4p Cclock+5u_0.

Indeed a b theta/Delta<3p because Delta>1/(2p);
gamma R_0<4p; gamma R_0 Delta v_0<4u_0; and2a alpha_0<u_0.
Combine with(3.4),(8.7) and p<1/128 to conclude

    G0<p[-Cclock^2/3+4Cclock+3+29p]
       <p[-Cclock^2/3+4Cclock+4]<0.                 (8.8)

The last sign follows from Cclock>32: Cclock^2/3>(32/3)Cclock,
so the bracket is less than -(20/3)Cclock+4<0.

But every nonpositive first-row off-diagonal entry would give
G0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-Tlast)N_0j>=0. Equation(8.8) is a contradiction.
At least one original finite lambda_(0,j)=-N_0j is therefore negative.
Every correction Ce=Ct=0 preserves this exact defect and cannot repair it.
This covers the full stated law for every SAME enclosing m>=1536, even
though its full PSD, all optimum signs and first-row sum already pass.

## 9. Scope audit, retrieval and handoff

All physical leaves, queried gradients, actual terminal gradient and
initial/common charge are present in the same original-coordinate identity.
There is no quotient, averaged terminal, dropped prefix interpolation row,
local budget recycling, or inferred primal interpolation from a formal test.
All signs needed for a certificate have explicit formulas. The positive
terminal and optimum signs are proved where claimed. Finite signs fail;
the report does not call their signed polynomial a feasible upper bound.

Mechanism I fixes w proportional to u, alpha proportional to u, p=1/(1+S),
and d=tau(rho-e_0), but includes EVERY skew with that imbalance. Changing
the imbalance's clock distribution, the metric or the probabilities lies
outside its exclusion. Mechanism II changes the metric and globally
minimizes the imbalance charge for its clock moment, but fixes nu=0 and
the displayed endpoint-dominated metric. Changing the incoming load or
substantially redistributing interior metric weight lies outside that
exclusion. The free skew corrections preserving the specified data have
been fully included in both obstruction scopes.

The two-moment minimum-price formula(7.2) exposes the distinction between
repairing the initial charge and repairing the clock charge. Mechanism II
also shows that satisfying both global load moments, the full PSD factor,
all optimum signs and the first-row SUM still need not satisfy the
first-row CLOCK. Those necessities should precede another local transport
proposal. No third mechanism is attempted or implied here.

Query-memory recovered TASK, current guidance, the complete previous own
report and the relevant accepted facts. The immediate-conclusions,
direct-proving, construct-counterexamples and identify-key-failures
procedures guided the joint analytical screens. Fact search found the
accepted terminal reduction but no accepted version of the new identities.
Matlas's repair search returned unrelated robust-halfspace and SGD lemmas;
none is used. Web retrieval recovered Altschuler--Parrilo,
Acceleration by Stepsize Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth
Convex Optimization, arXiv:2309.16530, https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530.
It is previously audited background only. No theorem from it is imported;
both mechanisms reduce to the explicit interpolation and finite linear
algebra regime already in the accepted interface, so no additional
literature layer is needed for these scoped algebraic obstructions.

The complete report has received an author textual audit only. It is not
verifier-accepted and must not be used as a fact-graph premise. The two
mechanisms exhaust this cap1 invocation. Return to the main agent after
saving the report and shared handoff; do not renew, overwrite a live TASK,
submit a fact, finalize, revoke, shut down, push, or mark a partial Goal
complete. Original every-n/every-h exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

# Late common column and the paid source-column capacity

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation87.
Original exact-profile minimax target OPEN. No fact_submit, no new fact_id.
Guidance136c8e6e0b9c4945; plan d0d3e2892870802d.

## 1. Outcome and exact scope

The prescribed late common column genuinely repairs the last-column
shortage. Its full original-space PSD condition, all optimum signs,
last adjacent-pair cut, and both endpoint row-moment screens pass for
every SAME enclosing m>=1536. The source column nevertheless fails:

    c_0 := N_00 - nu_0 - 2alpha_0 < -1/128.

Here nu_0=1/64 is the specified incoming optimum multiplier. This is
the sum of ALL finite incoming multipliers at the first query, not a
sign test of one arbitrarily selected skew completion. The entire law
fails for every compatible skew correction.

The second mechanism is a whole-class obstruction for the genuinely
enlarged PSD-detail architecture. Even after changing the detail
matrix, its clock energy, the paid column, and the common column,
full original-space PSD implies the source capacity bound

    N_00 <= b^2 q/a.

Consequently nonnegative finite incoming coefficients require

    nu_0 + 2alpha_0 <= b^2 q/a.                    (1.1)

For q<=17/(16D), the right side is <6p<1/128, whereas the prescribed
nu_0 is1/64. No PSD-detail repair retaining that incoming load can
succeed in this architecture. No unspecified PSD matrix is assumed
feasible: the second mechanism derives a necessary inequality for
all members and gives literal chronological detail families it covers.

This is a restriction on coefficients and their paid initial charge.
It is not full-dual infeasibility, not a legal primal counterexample,
not a lower bound for W, and not a resolution of the original question.
The surviving freedom includes nu_0 of order p or zero, with all other
loads and individual signs then rederived. No third law is attempted.

Every new result below belongs to this ONE joint unverified draft.
No earlier unverified finding is used as an accepted intermediate lemma.
Only accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used at their
stated scopes. The latter's799/800 implication remains conditional.

## 2. Original target, coordinates, and uniform scalar bounds

For a fixed nonnegative length-n word h and L>0, W_n(h) is the supremum
of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional
convex differentiable L-smooth functions with a minimizer and x_0!=x_star,
using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R. The original problem is
whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and
U_n^ZJ the exact all-split Zhang--Jiang benchmark, not a decimal profile.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical hierarchy and the scalar
comparison B_n/D<47/50, where U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Its separation conclusion
requires the still-unproved full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for all m>=1536.
Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation orientation
and exact unqueried-terminal reduction. No queried sample is deleted.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536. All children retain these SAME parameters:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,
    S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).

All short notation is LOCAL to this report. In particular rho below is
a probability vector, not the benchmark exponent; T is a scalar clock,
Ltri a matrix, and a superscript T means transpose. Public names ending87
are aliases for this report's definitions, not project-glossary overrides.
Let e be the all-ones column on the n queried indices and e_i its coordinate
columns. Define

    Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, Ltri_ij=0 for j>i,
    t=Ltri e, t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,
    T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1, hgap=1+beta, t_r=T-hgap.

The last three steps are (b,hgap,b). Elementary bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12,
    1-2a>a^2, 7/5<b<17/12, 15/8<hgap<2,
    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40), 3/(4p)<Delta<1/p,
    Delta-b>1/(2p), p<dclock:=1/Delta<4p/3,
    0<rclock:=hgap/Delta<4p.                       (2.1)

For the lower a bound use sqrt(2)<10/7 and
7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5. Its upper bound follows from beta/(1+eps)<1.
The quadratic inequality follows from1+a<sqrt(2).
The hierarchy mass, also obtained by substitution into
1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j, is

    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,
    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).

This proves the p bounds; the remaining clock inequalities follow by
substituting Delta=1/p-b-1. These are symbolic all-m inequalities,
not evaluated recurrences or asymptotic approximations.

For integer suffix length Ls in[0,n], let sbit(Ls) be its binary digit
sum and put

    rtail_Ls=product_(j=n-Ls)^(n-1)(h_j-1)
             =beta^Ls*c^(-sbit(Ls)), s=rtail_n,
    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j), u_i=|k_i|,
    U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.

Indeed h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j), where v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing j,
and sum_(j=1)^Ls v2(j)=Ls-sbit(Ls). With Ls=l-i,

    u_i=rtail_Ls+rtail_(Ls+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,
    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.

The last three u terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, so U>7/3.
Also 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 by induction, and
beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2. Hence beta^n<2^(-4m), and

    u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64.

It follows that

    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, s<p, rho_i>0.                 (2.2)

Finite product telescoping gives

    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t^T k=S.               (2.3)

For the terminal pool define

    Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho], Vlast=b(1-p).

Here Vtail>32, as follows without an external estimate. Set
Ztail=sum_(Ls=0)^n rtail_Ls and Mtail=sum_(Ls=0)^n Ls*rtail_Ls.
Expansion over binary digits gives

    Ztail=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),
    U=2Ztail-1-s,
    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).

Choose j with m/2<2^j<=m; Bernoulli's inequality gives its summand
>3m/28. An index shift gives
sum_i rho_i(l-i)=(2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns)/U>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2,
using ns<1 and positivity of the numerator. Each suffix mass
1/p-t_i is at least b+l-i. Since
Vtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i), this proves Vtail>32 for m>=1536.

Keep the EXACT assigned mixture

    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),
    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT rho], alpha_star=p,
    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.

Since4/3<Vlast<17/12 and Vtail>32,

    0<xiT<1/45, e^T alpha=1-p, V:=t^T v=2,
    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,
    0<alpha_0<u_0, 0<v_0<u_0,
    e^T v=E, 1-p/32<eta<1.                       (2.4)

For example alpha_l>44(1-p)/45>19/20 and
v_l=b-alpha_l<17/12-19/20<1/2. At0,
alpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1. The eta bound uses2s/E<p/32.
The vector R=Ltri^T v satisfies

    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0,
    R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.                             (2.5)

The first identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (2.3).

## 3. Full original-space identity and literal coefficients

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain the original formal
vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n independently. From accepted1252 use

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Put y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 on every queried index.
The actual GD substitutions give

    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),
    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                            (3.1)

The unit-diagonal triangular Ltri is invertible, so no original
queried-gradient direction is removed. The terminal gradient g_n
is still a separate free vector.

For positive weights w, define Ww=sum_i w_i and
K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww. For e^T z=E and e^T zeta=0 put

    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T, d=zeta+F(v-z),
    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                 (3.2)

The COMPLETE signed original coefficient array is

    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},
    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.

All diagonal lambda entries vanish. For every i,j<n,

    N_ij=P_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(P_uj+A_uj).       (3.3)

Thus there is a literal coefficient for EVERY original pair.
The prefix outgoing total at i is N_ii and its prefix incoming total
is N_ii-2alpha_i. The terminal contributes2alpha_i, so the finite-node
value divergences vanish; terminal divergence is2 and optimum is-2.

For prefix indices including the optimum,
Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.
The prefix quadratic is -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y.
After including -2f_n, the terminal contribution is

    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2
                                       -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.

The values cancel. Using Pe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2 gives

    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2
       +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.          (3.4)

Let ybar_w=sum w_i y_i/Ww. Completing the weighted squares gives

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2
       +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+zeta_i*x_0/(2w_i)||^2
       +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,
    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                   (3.5)

Necessity is literal: set scalar x_0=1,
y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i), c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E),
and g_n=-s+v^T y. Every displayed square vanishes; (3.1) recovers
all gradients. Thus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J.
The charge is paid ONCE. The common queried-gradient direction g_i=G
leaves prefix remainder
(q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2,
where X=t^T Kt, theta=t^T zeta, Z=t^T z. It has not been discarded.

## 4. Mechanism1: prescribed nonzero-clock common column

Set

    z=b e_l+(E-b)e_r, Z=E*t_r+b*hgap,
    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload e_0,
    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM),
    omega=p^2, what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega rho,
    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,
    Xreq=theta0/p-4-eta Z^2+ellload,
    tbase=sum what_i t_i/sum what_i,
    Vbase=sum what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,
    w=(Xreq/Vbase)what, zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.        (4.1)

This changes the common column itself. It has e^T z=E, z_0=0,
z_l=b, and genuinely nonzero clock Z. Put Zstar=1+b*hgap.
By (2.1)--(2.4),

    |Z-Zstar|<4p, 3<Z<4,
    |eta Z^2-Zstar^2|<33p,
    0<xiM<1/4, 0<sigma<1/3.                      (4.2)

Indeed Z-Zstar=-(b+hgap)p+s*t_r, and0<t_r<1/p.
The eta error is at most16p/32; the two squared-clock error is <32p.
Positivity of xiM follows from1-a-a^2/8>0 and its upper bound from
a(1-a)<=1/4. All what_i are strictly positive.
The two unit endpoint weights imply Vbase>=Delta^2/2. Further,

    2/p-26<Xreq<2/p,
    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p>0,
    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<32p,
    2<theta<3, theta/Xreq>p.                     (4.3)

For the second lower bound subtract at most20 from theta0/p,
which is greater than20480. For the ratio subtract2 from
[2-(10+eta Z^2-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]. Finally

theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+p(4+eta Z^2)>0.

Thus e^T zeta=0, t^T Kt=Xreq, t^T zeta=theta and

    J=theta^2/(4Xreq), p^2+J<(17/32)p<qtar.        (4.4)

To justify the strict bound, theta0>1, ellload=1/64 and (4.3) give
J<(theta0*p/4)(1+1/64)^2*1024/1023
 <(4225/8184)p<(67/128)p.
Then use p<1/128 and qtar=17p/[16(2-p)].

For any skew in (3.2), multiplication gives

    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,
    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,
    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),
    t^T nu=Xreq+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.           (4.5)

Here V=2, alpha^T t=1/p-3, so both moments equal ellload,
matching nu_target. A literal skew completion is

    rvec=Kt+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target,
    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,
    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,
    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,
    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.             (4.6)

The two moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.
Consequently e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0, and direct multiplication
proves Ae=d and At=rvec. Each term is skew. All other compatible
completions are A+C with C^T=-C, Ce=Ct=0. The displayed law takes C=0;
the obstructions below allow all such C.

Every terminal and incoming optimum sign passes. Every outgoing
optimum sign also passes. The zeta_i have the signs of t_i-tbase,
change sign once, and sum to zero. Every proper suffix sum is positive.
If zeta_i<0, the suffix after i is at least -zeta_i, so
(Ltri^T zeta)_i>= (h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0. If zeta_i>=0 its expression
zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j is also positive, including i=l.
Equation(2.5) then gives mu_i>0 for all queried i.

## 5. Every diagonal/column equation; the decisive first-column failure

For EVERY queried index define its actual diagonal and finite incoming mass

    Ddiag_i=P_ii+h_i sum_(j>i)(P_ji+A_ji),
    c_i=Ddiag_i-nu_i-2alpha_i
       =sum_(j!=i)(-N_ji).                       (5.1)

These formulas include every query, and (4.6) makes them literal.
For another compatible skew C, the diagonal changes by
h_i sum_(j>i)C_ji. The first and last diagonals, however, are invariant:

    Ddiag_0=b(Pe-d)_0-aP_00,
    Ddiag_l=P_ll,
    Pe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta.                          (5.2)

The first identity uses the exact first row of Ltri^T,
b e^T-a e_0^T, and skew diagonals are zero. It is NOT a row-sum
approximation. Since z_0=0 in Mechanism1,

    Ddiag_0=b(2s v_0-zeta_0)-a(K_00+v_0^2).       (5.3)

To bound it and audit the endpoint rows, normalize clocks by
xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta and set

    B=sum what_i, M=sum what_i*xclock_i,
    M2=sum what_i*xclock_i^2, B0=what_0,
    Hvar=M2-M^2/B,
    fbase=B0*M/B, kbase=B0(1-B0/B),
    Y=-(Kt)_0=(Xreq/Delta)fbase/Hvar.

Here Hvar>=1/2, B<5/2, M>=1, fbase>2/5,
Hvar<=B/4<5/8, and fbase/Hvar<3. The variance upper bound follows
from xclock in[0,1] and xclock^2<=xclock. Hence

    Y>1/2, a K_00/Y=a*dclock*(B-B0)/M<(5/6)p,
    -zeta_0=(theta/Delta)fbase/Hvar<12p.           (5.4)

Together with theta/Xreq>p and (2.2)--(2.4), (5.3) yields

    p/4<Ddiag_0<32p,
    c_0=Ddiag_0-ellload-2alpha_0<-1/128.          (5.5)

For the lower bound use
b(-zeta_0)-aK_00>(b-5/6)pY>(17/60)p,
and a v_0^2 is smaller than p/100. For the upper bound discard
both nonpositive square terms and use2bs v_0<p^2.
The last inequality uses32p<1/128. Thus the first diagonal itself
is positive but cannot pay its incoming optimum load.

This is a uniform FAILED COLUMN of the full array. It rules out
simultaneous nonnegativity of the entire collection (5.1), whatever
the other diagonals do. It would be false to mark ALL columns as passed.
Specifically at least one original lambda_(j,0), 1<=j<=l, is negative.
No compatible skew correction changes that conclusion. Having found
this cut, there is no need or justification to search individual signs
of the enormous array. The remaining tests below distinguish which
of the proposed repairs really work.

### 5.1 Last column really is repaired

The scale is at most2Xreq/Delta^2<8p, so K_ll<=w_l<12p.
But the new common column gives

    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta b^2>21/10,
    c_l=P_ll-2alpha_l>1/10.                      (5.6)

Indeed v_l>a>2/5, eta>1-p/32 and b>7/5 imply
v_l^2+eta b^2>53/25-(49/800)p>21/10.
The incoming optimum mass nu_l is zero. Thus the prior last-column
obstruction is removed, rather than merely overlooked.

### 5.2 The last adjacent-pair factor cut passes

Let hgap=h_r. Since the last two rows of Ltri^T are e_l^T and
e_r^T+hgap e_l^T,

    N_lr+N_rl=2P_lr+hgap P_ll.                   (5.7)

The skew terms cancel exactly. If both off-diagonals are nonpositive,
the right side must be nonpositive. For the prescribed law it equals

    2K_lr+hgap K_ll
     +v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)
     +eta b[2E-eps*b].                          (5.8)

Here K_lr<0, v_r=-a*hgap-alpha_r, and v_l<1/2. Thus
v_l(2v_r+hgap v_l)<-(3/25)hgap<-9/40.
The metric term is <24p and the positive part of the common term
is <4p. Therefore

    2P_lr+hgap P_ll<-1/5.                       (5.9)

This necessary pair test passes, but does not remedy (5.5).

## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens for Mechanism1

These are row tests, distinct from the column equation (5.5). They
are included to audit the nonzero-clock change jointly, not as a
substitute for the failed column test.

Define first-row quantities

    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,
    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,
    G0=P0-Delta*S0.

Nonpositive first-row off-diagonals require

    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0.                   (6.1)

From (3.2), (4.5), and the first row b e^T-a e_0^T,

    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,
    P0=b(4s+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0
                       +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0. (6.2)

For the new z_0=0, the nonmetric part of S0 is
2pR_0-2bs v_0+a v_0^2<7p^2. Hence (5.4) gives

    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                        (6.3)

For the clock, put K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and
Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Direct expansion of (6.2) gives

    G0=(a/Delta)(b*theta0-4-eta Z^2)
       +2bFZ-2pT R_0+4bs-4a v_0+2a alpha_0
       +2bsDelta v_0-aDelta v_0^2+Epert,
    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq.  (6.4)

The incoming load cancels exactly in this identity because
Xreq-Ttheta=b*theta0-4-eta Z^2-Delta*ellload,
and the expansion includes the original +a nu_0=a ellload.
No incoming load has been suppressed.

For an auxiliary base omit only omega rho, keeping its three literal
atoms at0,1,1-rclock with weights1,1,sigma. Put
Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.
Using B=2+sigma, M=1+sigma(1-rclock), and
M2=1+sigma(1-rclock)^2 in the displayed definitions gives exactly

    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)
       [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)
                         -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].              (6.5)

Since1-2p<Dclock<=1, |1/Dclock-1|<rclock,
a*dclock<p, rclock<4p, Xreq*dclock<2 and theta<3,
the bracket differs from theta by less than35p. Consequently

    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.    (6.6)

Restoring omega rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega.
Both variances are >=1/2. Direct quotient subtraction bounds the
changes of fbase,kbase,Hvar by2omega,4omega,5omega, respectively,
and those of fbase/Hvar,kbase/Hvar by32omega. The exact metric part
of G0 is

a(Xreq/Delta)(2fbase/Hvar-kbase/Hvar)
       +theta(1-a/Delta)fbase/Hvar-btheta.

Its change is <400p^2. Each base is scaled by its own variance and
the same Xreq; hence this includes the change of scale.

In (6.4), replacing theta0 by2 and eta Z^2 by Zstar^2 costs
less than42p^2. Replacing2bFZ by2b*dclock*Zstar costs less than63p^2,
since |p-dclock|<4p^2 and |Z-Zstar|<4p. Replacing-2pT R_0
by-2b*dclock costs less than20p^2; use R_0=bE-a v_0 and pT=1-bp.
The five remaining s,v_0,alpha_0 terms total less than5p^2.
Together with (6.6) and the restored positive tail, this proves

    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<1000p^2,
    Cfirst=a(2b-4-Zstar^2)+2b(Zstar-1)
                                  +xiM*hgap*(2+ellload)
          =Cold+b*hgap*(2-a*b*hgap),
    Cold=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).             (6.7)

Substitute xiM and hgap=2-eps to obtain

    Cold=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM
                                  -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM
         >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.

Here a>2/5 and eps<=1/6144 give4/125-3/24576>1/32.
Also a*b*hgap<85/72<6/5 and b*hgap>21/8. Thus the extra term
in (6.7) exceeds21/10, so Cfirst>2. A coarse direct bound gives
|Cfirst|<20. Therefore

    p<G0<30p,
    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<30p-1/32<0.            (6.8)

Equations (6.3),(6.8) establish the entire first-row screen (6.1).

For the last row put

    Slast=mu_l-P_ll,
    Jlast=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,
    Hlast=(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.

These are respectively the row total, its clock relative to1, and its
clock relative to t_r. Nonpositive off-diagonals require
Slast<=0, Jlast<=0, Hlast>=0. Set

    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/Hvar,
    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/Hvar.

They are positive and <3, with
(Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast.
For the auxiliary three-atom base,
Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.
Restoring omega rho changes this ratio by at most32p^2, by the same
quotient estimate. Now expansion gives the exact formula

    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast
          +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b
          +eta*b*(2Z-hgap*b),
    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.            (6.9)

By (4.3), Cclock>2-ellload-64p and Cclock<3. Hence
Cclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-100p.
The negative metric term has magnitude <24p. The v_l polynomial is
increasing for a<=v_l<1/2, because its derivative is greater than2.
The terms in (6.9) before the common-column contribution therefore exceed

    2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-125p
     =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-125p
     >3/8000-125p>1/4000.

The exact incoming-load loss ellload(1-xiM) is retained.
Since Z>3, hgap*b<17/6, eta>9/10 and b>7/5, the new common
contribution in (6.9) exceeds3. Thus Hlast>3.
Finally mu_l=2p v_l+(theta/Delta)Alast<19p, whereas P_ll>21/10.
Also (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l+2eta Zb-2alpha_l<26. It follows that

    Slast<-2, Jlast<26-(21/10)Delta<0, Hlast>3.   (6.10)

Both endpoint row screens really pass. The first incoming COLUMN,
not either endpoint row screen or the last adjacent pair, kills the law.

## 7. Mechanism2: explicit chronological PSD details and their global price

We now enlarge the detail term itself, instead of changing another
weight within one weighted-variance metric. The identified constraint
is the first diagonal's ability to pay a column load under a small
initial charge. Both clock-bearing and clock-annihilating details are
allowed below; the former change the paid column and all load moments.
Their price is recomputed, not assumed to vanish.

On the ORIGINAL queried indices define the literal chronological vectors

    gdetail_i=e_i-e_(i+1), 0<=i<=n-2,
    ddetail_i=h_(i+1)e_i-(h_i+h_(i+1))e_(i+1)+h_i e_(i+2),
                                           0<=i<=n-3.

They obey

    e^T gdetail_i=0, t^T gdetail_i=-h_i,
    e^T ddetail_i=0, t^T ddetail_i=0.              (7.1)

For the latter clock identity substitute
(t_i,t_(i+1),t_(i+2))=(t_i,t_i+h_i,t_i+h_i+h_(i+1));
each term cancels. Thus zero sum alone does NOT imply zero clock energy.

Choose this ONE explicit enlarged coefficient law:

    gamma_i=Xreq/[2(n-1)h_i^2], 0<=i<=n-2,
    tau_i=p/[1+h_i+h_(i+1)]^2, 0<=i<=n-3,
    Hclock=sum_(i=0)^(n-2) gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T,
    Hnull=sum_(i=0)^(n-3) tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T,
    Qdetail=K/2+Hclock+Hnull.                      (7.2)

Every vector and weight is an original-index formula. No optimizer,
recurrence replay, matrix-existence claim, or selected support is used.
All sums are finite symbolic definitions, not executed computations.
The entries of K/2 correspond to the positive weights w/2. Consequently
Qdetail is PSD and positive definite on e-perp. From (7.1),

    Qdetail e=0,
    t^T Hclock t=sum_i gamma_i h_i^2=Xreq/2,
    Hnull t=0, t^T Qdetail t=Xreq.                (7.3)

Retain the same terminal pool and late common column, and REPLACE

    zeta by zeta_new=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t,
    P by Pnew=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    d by dnew=zeta_new+F(v-z).

Use (4.6) with Kt replaced by Qdetail t, d replaced by dnew, and
rvec=Qdetail t+2v+eta Zz-2alpha-nu_target. This again is a literal skew
completion and, with (3.3), specifies EVERY original multiplier.
Equations(7.3) give e^T zeta_new=0 and t^T zeta_new=theta, so both
incoming-load compatibility moments remain ellload. The incoming
optimum vector is exactly nu_target; it is not a free unpriced correction.

The complete polynomial is (3.4) with K,zeta replaced by
Qdetail,zeta_new. In particular its paid part equals

    (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)^T Qdetail
                   (y+(theta/(2Xreq))*t*x_0)
        +(q-p^2-theta^2/(4Xreq))||x_0||^2.         (7.4)

This equality follows by expanding the square and using (7.3).
Necessity of this same charge follows with scalar x_0=1,
y=-(theta/(2Xreq))*t+c0 e, choosing c0 to cancel the common square,
and choosing the independent g_n to cancel the terminal square.
Thus the exact global charge remains

    q>=p^2+theta^2/(4Xreq),                       (7.5)

which passes at qtar by (4.4). There is no extra hidden initial cost,
no omitted clock energy, and no uncancelled terminal square.

All optimum signs pass for this second law as well. Hnull t=0, and
Ltri^T Kt has strictly positive entries by the argument following (4.6).
For Hclock t write aedge_i=gamma_i h_i>0, with aedge_(-1)=aedge_l=0.
The i-th coordinate of Hclock t is aedge_(i-1)-aedge_i; its suffix
after i is aedge_i. Therefore

    (Ltri^T Hclock t)_i=aedge_(i-1)+(h_i-1)aedge_i>0

for every i, interpreting endpoints by the zero conventions. This
uses h_i>1. Multiplication by theta/Xreq>0, addition of the half-K
term and 2pR proves every mu_new,i>0. The incoming and terminal signs
are the already specified nonnegative ones.

The source data have changed explicitly:

    Qdetail_00=K_00/2+gamma_0+tau_0*h_1^2,
    zeta_new,0=zeta_0/2-theta/[2(n-1)b].           (7.6)

Thus this mechanism changes the first diagonal and paid source column,
not just a row-clock summary. Clock-free details also alter diagonals:
the tau_0 term in (7.6) must be included, even though Hnull t=0.
The full failure of this enlarged law follows from the next global
inequality, which covers considerably more freedom than (7.2).

### 7.1 A source-capacity inequality for the entire PSD-detail class

For this necessary-condition argument allow ANY symmetric PSD Qdetail
with Qdetail e=0; no such matrix is asserted to solve a feasibility
problem. Allow ANY zero-sum paid vector zeta and ANY common vector z
with e^T z=E. Keep v=k-alpha, e^T alpha=1-p, 0<s<p, eta=F/E,
and the original coefficient formulas

    P=Qdetail+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    d=zeta+F(v-z), A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).

The incoming vector may now vary as well. Assume only that the full
original polynomial at level q is PSD. That assumption will be used
to derive a necessary inequality, not to posit a feasible completion.
For any scalar x,u choose

    x_0=x,
    y=u e_0+[x-(z_0/E)u]e,
    g_n=-s*x+v^T y.

These are permissible original formal directions by invertibility
of Ltri. The common and terminal squares vanish EXACTLY. Because
Qdetail e=0 and e^T zeta=0, full PSD implies, for every x,u,

    Qdetail_00*u^2+zeta_0*x*u+(q-p^2)*x^2>=0.    (7.7)

In particular the following explicitly evaluated quantity is nonnegative:

    Rsource=Qdetail_00+(b/a)zeta_0
                         +(b^2/a^2)(q-p^2)>=0.   (7.8)

The first diagonal formula (5.2) holds unchanged:

    N_00=b(2s v_0+2Fz_0-zeta_0)
                    -a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).

Now expand these THREE nonnegative terms:

    b^2 q/a-N_00
       =a Rsource
         +a(v_0-bs/a)^2
         +a*eta*(z_0-bE/a)^2 >=0.                (7.9)

To check the constant exactly, eta E=F and
s^2+eta E^2=p^2. The constants on the right are therefore
(b^2/a)[q-p^2+s^2+eta E^2]=b^2 q/a;
the linear terms are b*zeta_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,
and the quadratic terms are a(Qdetail_00+v_0^2+eta z_0^2).
This verifies (7.9) term by term without an inverse or external theorem.

If the actual finite incoming multipliers at the first query are
nonnegative, their total is N_00-nu_0-2alpha_0>=0. Hence

    nu_0+2alpha_0 <= N_00 <= b^2 q/a.             (7.10)

This bounds the source load by the ENTIRE paid initial budget.
It cannot be repaired by moving clock energy between details,
using clock-annihilating details, changing their ranks or supports,
changing zeta, changing z (including its clock), or changing skew
corrections while keeping the large source load and small q.
It even allows a different terminal distribution with nonnegative
alpha_0, as long as the displayed architecture and p,s apply.

For the physical parameters, b^2/a<(17/12)^2/(2/5)<6 and qtar<p.
Thus, whenever q<=qtar,

    N_00<6p<1/128,
    N_00-(1/64)-2alpha_0<-1/128                  (7.11)

if alpha_0>=0. This proves a uniform failed ORIGINAL first column
for the explicit enlarged law (7.2), and for the full class in this
subsection retaining nu_0=1/64. It is independent of all compatible
skew corrections. Equivalently that fixed load requires q>1/384,
whereas qtar<p<2^(-40).

For clarity, the obstruction includes arbitrary finite nonnegative
weights on the literal vectors gdetail_i,ddetail_i, arbitrary positive
variance metrics, their sums, and any other zero-sum PSD detail
construction. Only necessary inequalities are claimed for this class;
there is no arbitrary unknown PSD object adopted as a certificate.

### 7.2 Moment accounting for the surviving enlargement freedoms

A general sum H=sum gamma_i gdetail_i gdetail_i^T
+sum tau_i ddetail_i ddetail_i^T, with nonnegative scalar weights,
has He=0, t^T Ht=sum gamma_i h_i^2, and its second sum annihilates t.
If its clock energy Eclock is less than Xreq, the explicit replacement
Qdetail=(1-Eclock/Xreq)K+H has total clock energy Xreq.
For zeta=(theta/Xreq)Qdetail t the exact charge is again (7.5),
and the incoming total and clock are again ellload. This describes
the larger parameter class covered by (7.10); (7.2) is its single
fully fixed original-index instance used in this report.

If all details annihilate both e and t and one leaves K,zeta fixed,
then the optimum vectors and load moments remain unchanged, but
N_00 decreases by a H_00, N_ll increases by H_ll, and the last-row
clock defect changes by -hgap H_ll. The latter follows from
H t=0, mu unchanged, and the last row of Ltri^T being e_l^T.
These diagonal and endpoint changes still have to be paid; null
clock energy is not permission to ignore them.

If one changes z, alpha, or the incoming vector beyond the fixed
law, the general compatibility equations are

    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),
    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t,
    V=t^T v, Z=t^T z, X=t^T Qdetail t,
    theta=t^T zeta.

Thus changing nu_0 is a real global-moment change. The current
necessary bound requires nu_0+2alpha_0=O(p) at the target scale.
It does not assert that such a smaller load is sufficient, nor
exclude a nonnegative incoming distribution supported elsewhere.
No attempt to choose a third coefficient law is made in this round.

## 8. Scope, failed paths, literature, and resource record

Mechanism1 fails because its O(p) first diagonal cannot pay the
fixed1/64 incoming optimum coefficient, despite genuine repair of
the last column and success of both endpoint row screens.
Mechanism2 changes the PSD detail term, pays all clock energy, and
keeps the exact initial charge; it still fails the uniform source cut.
The common cause is the global paid source capacity, not a poor
choice of skew completion or an untested transport existence claim.

The new report also diagnoses the earlier source-loaded laws: any
member of this architecture using nu_0=1/64 and q<=qtar fails (7.10),
regardless of any previously successful endpoint row checks. This
is awareness about unverified candidates, not a revocation of facts.
No accepted fact claims those candidates are legal.

The original minimax target stays OPEN. Accepted1252 permits retaining
a full certificate with no incoming UNQUERIED terminal edges; it does
not eliminate queried pairs. Accepted931 supplies only the scalar
benchmark comparison and its explicitly conditional separation.
Neither a negative multiplier nor the formal vectors used in (7.7)
are asserted to be a legal smooth-convex primal trajectory.

Skills used: query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions,
search-math-results, construct-counterexamples, identify-key-failures.
The counterexample being tested is coefficient-level sufficiency of
PSD/optimum signs/endpoint screens, not the original primal conjecture.
The scoped proof establishes its failure by the full first-column sum.

Retrieval: program_stage=mature_subproblem; active_program=full original
coefficient architecture with late common column and PSD details;
missing_mechanism=simultaneous paid column capacity;
search_mode=repair, then the task-authorized scoped obstruction.
One Matlas query returned unrelated matrix/network/control statements,

https://stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf . No theorem from it is
imported or cited as a proof premise. No extra literature layer was

(7.7)--(7.9) contain the whole elementary argument.

The no-match glossary search for the proposed suffix87 public names

Some initial aggregated recall outputs were truncated; the needed prior
