# Arbitrary common reversal: curvature-dependent child caps

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.** Project gd-exact-profile-20260926,
worker max3, invocation80 after ended79, completed ceiling80 / attempted81,
controller cap1. Assignment TASK.md and master_guidance5a7e62735542c5fe;
plan d44ca813f1ada357. No result below has passed fact_submit and no new
fact is asserted. Formal calls are explicitly forbidden in this invocation.
All ingredients belong to this one joint draft; earlier unverified reports
supply awareness only, never accepted premises.

ONE substantive mechanism is used: a two-sided child state with physical
input radius d_j*w and output radii proportional to1/kappa_sigma, augmented
by capture or escape information according to the fixed gradient clips.
The two clipping-ratio cases are components of this same state. The optional
bounded-curvature-contrast fallback is not activated.

The candidate closes the ENTIRE positive independent-curvature clipped
family at its exact all-start objective profile K/(1+2*K*S_m). It does
not close the original all-function minimax problem. No mathematical
program, recurrence evaluation, numerical or trajectory replay was used.

## 1. Complete candidate statement and scope

Fix ANY integer m>=1536 and use these SAME parameters in EVERY child:

    epsilon=1/(4*m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, S=S_m,
    D_m=1+2*S_m, n_m=2^m-1,
    ell=1+a, theta=a*beta.

The superscript distinguishes a level step from the physical entries h_i.
The auxiliary d_m is not an extra step. The final physical update is ell.

Let kappa_plus,kappa_minus be arbitrary in(0,1] and b_plus,b_minus>0. Define
ONE fixed static derivative and function by

    g(x)=min(kappa_plus*x,b_plus), x>=0,
    g(x)=max(kappa_minus*x,-b_minus), x<=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t) dt,
    u_sigma=b_sigma/kappa_sigma, sigma in{minus,plus},
    q=min(kappa_plus,kappa_minus), K=max(kappa_plus,kappa_minus),
    q_cut=1/(1+d_(m-2)).

Here u_sigma is a PHYSICAL radius threshold and b_sigma a GRADIENT cap.
Write Psi_j(s) for the actual complete H_j output from s on this function.
The candidate theorem is

    sup_(s in R, s!=0) 2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
                         =K/(1+2*K*S_m).                 (1.1)

Both clips and curvatures are fixed within the supremum. This covers every
positive clipping ratio, every positive curvature contrast, every amplitude
and both signs, with arbitrary dependence on m and every threshold equality.
Only strictly positive curvatures are asserted here.

When q>q_cut set

    J=min{j:q*h_j^lev>1}, j_base=max(1,J).                (1.2)

Then J can be ANY of0,...,m-2. In other parameter regions set J=infinity
if this set is empty. The strict condition is essential: q*h=1 never
strictly crosses the q-ray. A zero hit stays zero thereafter.

The principal state at every j>=j_base, integer r>=0, is

    Psi_j([-d_j*w*theta^r,d_j*w*theta^r])
      subset [-w*theta^(r+1)/kappa_minus,
               w*theta^(r+1)/kappa_plus].               (1.3)

For max b/min b<=2 take w=max b. Otherwise reflect so b_plus>=2*b_minus,
take w=b_minus, and include the escape implication

    sign(Psi_j(s))=sigma, kappa_sigma*|Psi_j(s)|>w
        => |s|>=|Psi_j(s)|+S_j*w/3.                     (1.4)

Reflection orders the GRADIENT caps; it does not order the curvatures.
The exact signed norm/energy conversion underlying this state is

    kappa_sigma*|x|<=z
       => 2*f(x)<=kappa_sigma*x^2<=z^2/kappa_sigma.      (1.5)

Thus the state yields1/(q*d_m^2), NOT a common-K d_m^(-2) bound.
Section12 compares this with the actual objective target, uniformly where
the state is used. Section5 rederives the entire complementary low-q region
jointly, rather than importing its earlier unverified proof.

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 and the
conditional799/800 separation only after a FULL-class bound17/(16*D_m).
Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 fixes original interpolation and terminal
conventions. Neither fact contains (1.1), and this restricted-family result
does not establish that full-W hypothesis or the original minimax claim.

## 2. Static legality, all interpolation rows, true normalization

On ray sigma, writing t=|x|,

    f(x)=kappa_sigma*t^2/2,                       t<=u_sigma,
    f(x)=b_sigma*t-b_sigma^2/(2*kappa_sigma),      t>=u_sigma. (2.1)

The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and piecewise affine with
slopes in[0,1]. Partition an arbitrary interval at the finitely many
thresholds to obtain |g(x)-g(y)|<=|x-y|. Hence f is finite, convex,
differentiable and globally1-smooth. Its unique minimizer is0, f(0)=0,
and the true initial distance is |s|. Equation (1.5) follows from (2.1):
on a tail the difference kappa_sigma*t^2-2*f(x) is
kappa_sigma*(t-u_sigma)^2.

At every physical index including the terminal define
x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), g_i=g(x_i), f_i=f(x_i).
For x<y let Delta=g(y)-g(x), so0<=Delta<=y-x. On[x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integration gives both remainders at least Delta^2/2. Thus every ordered
sample pair, including the minimizer, revisits and terminal, satisfies

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.        (2.2)

Equal positions give equality. The full Gram matrix is PSD because its
quadratic forms are squares of real linear combinations of scalar sample
vectors. The terminal is the actual output after all updates, including ell.
For original smoothness L>0 use F=L*f and steps h_i/L. The iterates are
unchanged and2*(F(x_n)-F(0))/(L*s^2)=2*f(x_n)/s^2. All scores therefore
use the original W=2R normalization and the true initial distance.

## 3. Same-m constants and exact physical cut geometry

We use

    12/5<c<17/7<5/2, beta>99/100,
    2/5<a<1/2, 2/5<theta<1/2, c*theta=beta^2<1.       (3.1)

The lower bound on c follows from sqrt(2)>7/5. For the upper bound,
epsilon<=1/6144<1/1000 and sqrt(2)<17/12 give
c<(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7, the last comparison being203203<204000.
Hence a>693/1700>2/5 and theta>68607/170000>2/5. Their upper bounds
follow from c>2 and beta<1, and theta<a.

Put eta=a/(c-2). Since c*(c-2)>1>beta, one has0<eta<1. Summing the
literal word gives

    A_(j+1)=2*A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j,
    d_j/A_j<c-2<3/7<1/2, A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    S_j>=d_j/(c-2)-1.                                (3.2)

The explicit formula starts at1 and satisfies the recurrence because
2*eta+a=c*eta. We also need, at every small child as well,

    S_j/d_j>7/5, j>=1.                               (3.3)

The base is S_1/d_1=ell/beta>7/5, since a>2/5 and beta<1.
If L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5, the literal mass identity gives

    S_(j+1)/d_(j+1)=(2*L_j+1+1/d_j)/c
                     >(19/5)/(17/7)=133/85>7/5.

This is a symbolic induction at the fixed enclosing m; no recurrence
is executed or evaluated.

For any physical level-k occurrence inside H_j, its full adjacent H_k
children imply that its prefix and suffix masses P,T satisfy

    P>=S_k, T>=S_k, P+T=S_j-h_k^lev.                  (3.4)

Its entire actual remaining suffix has the form

    H_k,h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1),...,h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.5)

To obtain it, ascend the binary tree after the right H_k, skip ancestors
already entered through a right child, and at each remaining left-child
ancestor take its bridge and entire right child. Each remaining physical
update occurs exactly once. At a final leaf this suffix is empty.

## 4. Monotone segments and one crossing at any physical position

For a segment actually staying on one ray of curvature p and threshold u,
let R,z be its initial/terminal radii and T its physical mass. Radii decrease,
so all queried gradient magnitudes are at least p*min(z,u). Consequently

    R>=z+p*T*min(z,u).                                (4.1)

If z<=u this bounds terminal twice-energy by p*R^2/(1+p*T)^2.
If z>=u use

    (z+p*T*u)^2-(1+2*p*T)*(2*u*z-u^2)
                           =(z-(1+p*T)*u)^2.

Both cases give

    2*f(terminal)<=p*R^2/(1+2*p*T).                   (4.2)

For a history with exactly one strict crossing, let p,v be source and
destination curvatures, h the crossing step and P,T its prefix/suffix
masses. At source radius y put t=min(y,u_source). The prefix gives
R>=y+p*P*t>=(1+p*P)*t, so its landing z and terminal energy satisfy

    z<=[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]*R,
    2*f(terminal)/R^2
       <=[v/(1+2*v*T)]*[(h*p-1)/(1+p*P)]^2.           (4.3)

A strict crossing requires h*p>1. The two positive factors increase in
their own curvatures: their derivatives are1/(1+2*v*T)^2 and
(h+P)/(1+p*P)^2. Both can therefore be replaced by K in this UPPER
EXPRESSION. This is not a claim that actual trajectories are monotone
in their curvature.

For a level-k cut in H_j put C=1+K*S_k and e=K*h_k^lev-1. If e<=0
no curvature at most K crosses. Otherwise

    0<e/C<=d_k/A_k<c-2<1/2.                          (4.4)

The first comparison follows by increasing K to1. Put
N=K*(S_j-S_(k+1)) and x=K*(P-S_k); then0<=x<=N and

    (1+K*P)^2*(1+2*K*T)
      =(C+x)^2*(2*C-1+2*N-2*x).                     (4.5)

Its derivative is2*(C+x)*(C-1+2*N-3*x), so its minimum is at an
endpoint. The value at N minus its value at0 is
N*(2*C*(C-1)+N*(2*C-1))>=0. Thus (4.5) is at least
C^2*(2*C-1+2*N). Also1+2*K*S_j=4*C+2*e-1+2*N, and

    e^2*(4*C+2*e-1)/(C^2*(2*C-1))
        <(5*C-1)/(4*(2*C-1))<=1.                    (4.6)

The last inequality is3*(C-1)>=0. Adding the2*N terms preserves the
comparison since e^2<C^2. Together these establish, on EVERY H_j
history with at most one strict crossing,

    2*f(Psi_j(s))/s^2<=K/(1+2*K*S_j).                 (4.7)

A zero hit has zero terminal gap. A no-crossing history uses (4.2).
Also the FIRST crossing of any H_j history has landing radius z obeying

    z/|s|<=(p*h_k^lev-1)/(1+p*P)
           <=d_k/(1+P)<=d_k/A_k<c-2.                (4.8)

All of these statements have been derived here as part of the joint draft.

## 5. Joint complementary proof for q<=q_cut

Reflect so q is on the negative ray and K on the positive ray. Put j=m-1.
Every step in each H_j child obeys q*h<=q*h_(m-2)^lev<=1. The negative
radius map r -> r-q*h*min(r,u_minus) is nonnegative, nondecreasing and
at most r. A child therefore has at most one crossing and obeys (4.7).

Without a negative-to-positive return, the whole H_m has at most one
crossing and (4.7) applies directly. Otherwise the sole such return
is the root h_j^lev. Its excess e_q=q*h_j^lev-1>0 satisfies

    e_q<=h_j^lev/h_(j-1)^lev-1<c-1.                  (5.1)

If the initial start is negative, its entire left child stays on q;
(4.3) at the root bounds its positive landing by(c-2)*|s|.
If the start is positive, its first crossing is inside the left child;
(4.8) bounds that landing by(c-2)*|s|, and the rest of that child
only decreases the negative radius. The root multiplies a negative
query radius by at most e_q when it crosses, including clipped queries.
In both cases its positive landing z satisfies

    z/|s|<(c-1)*(c-2)<30/49.                         (5.2)

The FULL right H_j starts at this actual z and may cross once again;
it obeys (4.7). Thus histories with THREE full-word crossings are included.

For j=m-1>=5, d_j>(2/5)*2^5>9 and (3.2) gives
S_j>7*d_j/3-1>2*(1+d_j). Therefore S_m=2*S_j+h_j^lev<(5/2)*S_j and

    (1+2*K*S_m)/(1+2*K*S_j)<5/2.

Using the full right child and (5.2) bounds every returning history by

    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
       <(2250/2401)*K/(1+2*K*S_m).                  (5.3)

Since2250<2401, this and the no-return bound prove the required upper
profile throughout q<=q_cut. The next sections cover ALL earlier common
reversals at once, without retaining monotonicity above J.

## 6. Exact interval maps and no-crossing flow bounds

For ray sigma with curvature p, cap b, threshold u=b/p, input radii
0<=r<=R and physical step h, the largest output radii staying on that
ray and crossing to the other ray are

    Stay(R)=[R-h*p*min(R,u)]_+,
    Cross(R)=(h*p-1)_+*min(R,u).                     (6.1)

The scalar pre-sign expression has slope1-h*p in the core and slope1
on the tail, so its extrema occur at0, the threshold or the outer endpoint.
For an input interval[-R_minus,R_plus], its output on ray sigma is the
maximum of Stay_sigma(R_sigma) and Cross_tau(R_tau), tau the other ray.
The update is continuous and fixes0; its image is the entire interval
between these attained endpoints. Chronological interval composition is
therefore exact. It does not choose incompatible maximizing child starts.
Interval inclusion also makes the endpoint map nondecreasing in its bounds.

For a segment actually staying on one ray define

    V_M(R;u)=R*exp(-M),                        R<=u,
             R-M*u,                          R>=u, M<=R/u-1,
             u*exp(R/u-1-M),                  R>=u, M>=R/u-1.

Then, for physical mass T,

    terminal radius<=V_(p*T)(R;u).                    (6.2)

This follows directly even when an effective step exceeds1. In the core,
a noncrossing step has hp<=1 and1-hp<=exp(-hp). A tail step remaining on
the tail agrees with the formula. A tail step entering the core without
crossing gives u*(1-z)<=u*exp(-z), where z=hp-(R/u-1) in[0,1].
The displayed V maps spend the first max(0,R/u-1) units of effective mass
on the linear tail and all later mass in the exponential core. This proves
their additive semigroup property. They are nondecreasing in R, so induction
applies. Their derivatives with respect to u in the three pieces are0,
-M, and exp(R/u-1-M)*(1-R/u), respectively, all nonpositive; the pieces
agree at their boundaries. Hence increasing u decreases V. This comparison
is for V, not for an actual point map with hp>1.

For ANY j>=1,p in(0,1], and threshold u>=w/(2*p), every no-crossing
H_j segment from radius at most d_j*w satisfies

    p*terminal_radius<2*w/5.                         (6.3)

Put P=p*d_j and M=p*S_j. Equations (3.2)-(3.3) give
M>(7/5)*P and M>(7/3)*P-1. Compare to threshold w/(2*p).
If P<=1/2 the normalized radius is at most
P*exp(-7*P/5)<=exp(-7/10)/2<5/17<2/5, since this comparison
function increases on[0,1/2].
If1/2<=P<=1, then M>2*P-1 and (6.2) bounds it by
exp(3*P/5-1)/2<=exp(-2/5)/2<5/14<2/5.
If P>=1, again M>2*P-1 and the bound is
exp(-P/3)/2<=exp(-1/3)/2<3/8<2/5.
The rational comparisons use exp(t)>1+t. Equality at a threshold
belongs to both adjacent formulas.

A larger-input counterpart is: if u>=2*w/p, a no-crossing H_j segment
from radius at most c*d_j*w has

    p*terminal_radius<w.                            (6.4)

Let L_j=S_j/d_j>7/5. If c*P<=2, its normalized output is at most
c*P*exp(-L_j*P), whose maximum is c/(exp(1)*L_j)<85/98<1,
using exp(1)>2. If c*P>=2, L_j>7/5>c/2 ensures core entry, and
the bound is2*exp(c*P/2-1-L_j*P)<2/exp(1)<1. This proves (6.4).

## 7. Arbitrary-J literal base, all curvature contrasts

Assume q>q_cut. If J>=1, every step in H_J has q*h<=1. The q-ray is
invariant; the other ray may have effective steps far above2. There is
at most one strict crossing in H_J, from K to q. We bound its actual
output using its whole physical suffix, not monotonicity of the K map.

Set

    C_return=c*(c-2)^2/(c-1).
    C_return<765/2401<2/5,
    c*C_return<13005/16807<1.                        (7.1)

The comparisons use c<17/7, c-2<3/7 and c-1>7/5.

Suppose the crossing is at level k<J with prefix/suffix masses P,T.
From initial radius at most d_J*w, (4.3) and increasing its source
curvature to1 bound its landing radius z by

    z<=d_J*w*d_k/(1+P).                              (7.2)

The key exact physical cut estimate is

    q*d_J*d_k/[(1+P)*(1+q*T)]<C_return.              (7.3)

To prove it, put N=S_J-S_(k+1), x=P-S_k, so0<=x<=N and T=S_k+N-x.
The denominator (A_k+x)*(1+q*(S_k+N-x)) is concave in x. Its endpoint
value at N minus the value at0 is N*(1-q)>=0. Its minimum is therefore
at0. Writing t=q*d_J, the left side is at most

    t*(d_k/A_k)/(1+q*(S_J-S_k-h_k^lev)).

Equation (3.2) gives A_J-A_k>=(d_J-d_k)/(c-2). Also q*d_k<=t/c,
so q*(S_J-S_k-h_k^lev)>=t/[c*(c-2)]-q. The base condition
q*h_(J-1)^lev<=1 gives1-q>=t/c. Combining these facts and
d_k/A_k<c-2 bounds the last display strictly by

    t*(c-2)/[t*(c-1)/(c*(c-2))]=C_return.

Now set Z=q*terminal_radius/w and B=b_destination/w. The monotone
q-ray suffix and (7.2) imply

    Z+q*T*min(Z,B)<=q*z/w.

If B>=1/2 then (7.3) forces Z<C_return: Z>=B would make the left
side at least B*(1+q*T), impossible since C_return<B; for Z<B the
left side equals Z*(1+q*T). Thus each crossing base history has signed
linear radius less than(2/5)*w. Each no-crossing history obeys (6.3).
This proves the r=0 base in (1.3) for BOTH choices of w.

For every r>=0 repeat this argument with w_r=w*theta^r. The fixed caps
are still at least w_r/2. Equations (6.3),(7.2)-(7.3) give output signed
linear radius below(2/5)*w_r<theta*w_r from input d_J*w_r.
Thus all geometric base caps hold directly. No homogeneity across a
clipping threshold, or convexity of a returning point map, is presumed.

If J=0 use the literal base H_1=(ell). For input radius
d_1*w*theta^r=beta*w*theta^r, every cross endpoint from (6.1) has
signed linear radius at most
kappa_destination*a*beta*w*theta^r<=theta*w*theta^r.
Both effective leaf steps exceed1. The only additional same-ray endpoint
is a saturated tail, whose signed linear radius is at most

    kappa_sigma*w*theta^r*(beta-ell/2)_+
                        <=theta*w*theta^r.           (7.4)

Use b_sigma>=w/2, theta^r<=1, and
theta-beta+ell/2=(1+a)/2-beta*(1-a)>(3*a-1)/2>0.
This proves the leaf base at every geometric scale, including threshold
ties. H_0 is not treated as a contracting block.

For the separated-cap case we also need, at the larger base interval,

    kappa_plus*max(0,Psi_(j_base)(s))<=w
                whenever |s|<=c*d_(j_base)*w.         (7.5)

For J>=1, a no-crossing positive terminal obeys (6.4). A crossing ending
positive obeys (7.2)-(7.3) with the input bound multiplied by c; its factor
is c*C_return<1, while its destination cap is at least2*w. The same
inversion proves (7.5). For J=0, a negative-to-positive leaf crossing
has signed linear radius at most
kappa_plus*(ell-1/kappa_minus)*w<=a*w<w.
A same-positive history obeys (6.4); zero contributes nothing.

As an explicit bounded-contrast check, for J>=1 and K/q<=2 all H_J steps have
K*h<=2. A noncrossing step decreases radius and objective. A crossing
is K-to-q and lands at radius at most its input radius; from a core its
new twice-energy is at most q times the old radius squared, and from a
source tail it lands within u_K while the old twice-energy is at least
K*u_K^2. Energy therefore also decreases. This literal H_J base is then
nonincreasing in both physical radius and objective, with at most one
crossing. The quantitative proof above also handles K/q>2.

## 8. Source-cap capture through every true ancestor suffix

Suppose a crossing occurs at k<J, its source gradient cap is at most w,
and its destination cap is at least w/2. Its destination is the q-ray
and its landing radius is at most d_k*w, because

    (h_k^lev-1/kappa_source)*b_source<=d_k*w.

The following ACTUAL H_k preserves that ray. Put r=q*d_k, M=q*S_k.
Then

    r<=1-q,
    M>=r/(c-2)-q>=(1+1/(c-2))*r-1.                  (8.1)

Write C_low=1+1/(c-2)>10/3. If r<=1/2, the monotone destination
flow bound gives normalized signed linear radius at most
r*exp(-M)<=exp(1)*r*exp(-C_low*r)<=1/C_low<3/10.
If r>=1/2, the comparison threshold w/2 gives the bound
exp(-(C_low-2)*r)/2<exp(-2/3)/2<3/10.
These estimates remain valid for k=0 with H_0 empty and M=0.
In particular this landing plus its full right H_k gives signed linear
radius below(2/5)*w<theta*w.

Every remaining ancestor of level below J preserves the q-ray and
decreases radius. At the first ancestor l>=J, if any, its input is in
the core because theta*w<min b. The bridge sends it to physical radius

    <=(h_l^lev-1/kappa_source)*theta*w
    <=d_l*theta*w.                                  (8.2)

If the H_l plateau is already available, the following full right child
captures it to signed linear radius at most theta*w. Every later
ancestor is treated in the literal increasing order (3.5).

If the crossing level k>=j_base, its immediate landing is at most d_k*w;
the already available H_k plateau gives the same cap, followed by (8.2)
at each later ancestor. The remaining exceptional case is k=0,J=0:
its landing signed linear radius is at most a*w. If this is the final
leaf, this is the actual terminal bound. Otherwise a*w<min b since
a<1/2, and its next ancestor l>=1 sends it to physical radius at most
a*d_l*w<=d_l*w; the H_l plateau captures it.

Thus, whenever all needed proper-child plateaus have been established,

    crossing from a ray with b_source<=w,
    with both fixed caps >=w/2
        => terminal signed linear radius <=a*w.      (8.3)

This applies to EVERY crossing in the comparable-cap case. In the
separated-cap case it applies to every negative-to-positive return,
since b_minus=w and b_plus>=2*w. It is NOT asserted for an arbitrary
crossing from the larger gradient cap there.

When applied inside H_j, every plateau used by (8.3) has level at most
j-1. The parent currently being proved is never used as a premise.
The argument is a finite induction on the true suffix (3.5), including
the original final leaf and actual destination curvature at each step.

## 9. Comparable gradient caps: capture and plateau induction

Suppose max b/min b<=2 and w=max b. Define physical capture radii

    C_(sigma,j)=b_sigma*(S_j+1/kappa_sigma).

For every j>=0 the exact capture identity is

    Psi_j([-C_(minus,j),C_(plus,j)])=[-u_minus,u_plus]. (9.1)

An exterior start on ray sigma at radius at least C_(sigma,j) stays
saturated throughout H_j: after any prefix mass P its radius is at least
b_sigma*(S_j-P+1/kappa_sigma)>=u_sigma. Its exact output radius is
R-S_j*b_sigma.

For (9.1), H_0 is the identity. If capture holds at j, the first child of
the parent capture interval has radii
b_sigma*(S_j+h_j^lev+1/kappa_sigma), by the exterior formula and central
capture. Its bridge same-ray endpoint is exactly C_(sigma,j). All
opposite excursions are at most

    (h_j^lev-1/kappa_tau)_+*b_tau<=d_j*b_tau
       <2*(c-2)*A_j*b_sigma
       <b_sigma*(S_j+1/kappa_sigma)=C_(sigma,j).

Here1/kappa_sigma>=1 and2*(c-2)<1. Thus the bridge image is exactly
the child capture interval, and the second child proves (9.1). This
works also when just one or neither effective bridge exceeds1.

For j>=j_base suppose (1.3) holds. From parent input radius c*d_j*w,
capture and its exterior branches bound the first-child endpoints by

    R_sigma<=max(u_sigma,c*d_j*w-S_j*b_sigma).        (9.2)

Both effective bridge steps now exceed1. Its cross endpoints are at most
d_j*w. Its same-ray tail is bounded by the maximum of a nonpositive
term and c*d_j*w-(S_j+h_j^lev)*b_sigma, which is at most d_j*w since

    S_j+h_j^lev=A_j+d_j
       >(1+1/(c-2))*d_j>=2*(c-1)*d_j,
    w/b_sigma<=2.                                  (9.3)

The bridge image is inside[-d_j*w,d_j*w], and the full second child
proves the parent plateau at r=0.

For r>=1, c*theta<1 gives
c*d_j*w*theta^r<=d_j*w*theta^(r-1). The first-child plateau bounds
each signed linear radius by w*theta^r, within both cores since
theta*w<min b. The bridge sends either point to physical radius at most
d_j*w*theta^r. The second-child plateau at r gives signed linear radius
at most w*theta^(r+1). This proves (1.3) at every j>=j_base, starting
from the literal bases in Section7. Section8 now also proves (8.3)
for every crossing in the full H_m.

## 10. Separated gradient caps: joint plateau and escape induction

Reflect so U=b_plus>=2*b_minus=2*w. Either curvature may be smaller.
We prove (1.3) and (1.4) together. Section7 supplies the base plateau.

First prove base escape. For J>=1, a no-crossing history whose terminal
signed linear radius exceeds w has all queried gradients at least w,
so its radius drops by at least S_J*w. If a crossing occurs it is K-to-q.
Let h,P,T be its step and masses, y its source radius, z its landing,
r its terminal radius, and v its source gradient magnitude. The escape
hypothesis q*r>w gives z-r>=T*w on the monotone q-ray suffix.
Also q*h<=1 and z=h*v-y<h*v, whence

    v>z/h>=r/h>w/(q*h)>=w.

The source prefix gives |s|>=y+P*v and y>=v/K, so

    |s|-z>=2*y+(P-h)*v>=(2/K+P-h)*v,
    3*(2/K+P-h)-(P+h)
      =6/K+2*P-4*h>=2*A_k-4*d_k>0.                 (10.1)

Use P>=S_k at the crossing level. The coefficient is positive, and
adding z-r>=T*w gives |s|-r>=S_J*w/3.

If J=0 the base is H_1. A no-crossing history is as above. A crossing
has landing radius r<=a*|s|. If its terminal signed linear radius exceeds
w then r>w/kappa_destination>=w, and
|s|-r>=(1/a-1)*r>w>ell*w/3. Thus base escape holds in this case too.

At each induction step we also need

    kappa_plus*max(0,Psi_j(s))<=w
                     for |s|<=c*d_j*w.              (10.2)

For j=j_base this is (7.5). For j>j_base, a negative-to-positive
return gives signed linear radius at most a*w by Section8 using only
proper-child plateaus. Without such a return a positive terminal requires
the entire child to stay positive. Its root level j-1>=J has
kappa_plus*h_(j-1)^lev>1, so a positive output there requires a saturated
root query. Every earlier positive query is then saturated as well,
because radii decrease. Its initial radius must exceed

    (S_(j-1)+h_(j-1)^lev)*U
      >=2*(1+1/(c-2))*d_(j-1)*w
      >c^2*d_(j-1)*w=c*d_j*w,                       (10.3)

a contradiction. The strict comparison uses
2*(1+1/(c-2))>20/3>25/4>c^2. This proves (10.2).

Suppose plateau and escape hold at j. At unscaled parent input radius
c*d_j*w, the first child's positive endpoint is at most w/kappa_plus.
Its negative endpoint obeys, by escape,

    R_minus<=max(w/kappa_minus,(c*d_j-S_j/3)*w).     (10.4)

Both bridge effective steps exceed1. Formula (6.1) bounds both cross
endpoints by d_j*w. Its positive tail is nonpositive. Its negative
tail is at most the maximum of a nonpositive term and
(c*d_j-S_j/3-h_j^lev)*w, which is at most d_j*w because

    (c-2)*d_j<=1+S_j/3.                             (10.5)

Indeed d_j<(c-2)*(1+S_j) and(c-2)^2<1/3. The actual bridge image
is inside[-d_j*w,d_j*w], so the full second child gives the parent
plateau at r=0. For r>=1 use the geometric argument following (9.3):
both child caps are in their respective cores; each source-to-destination
bridge gives physical radius at most d_j*w*theta^r. This proves the
parent plateau FIRST, without the parent escape conclusion.

For parent escape write y=Psi_j(s), z=y-h_j^lev*g(y), t=Psi_j(z).
Suppose its terminal signed linear radius exceeds w. If t>0, Section8
excludes every negative-to-positive return using plateaus through H_j.
The entire parent path is positive and every query gradient exceeds w;
hence s-t>=S_(j+1)*w, stronger than needed.

Let t=-r<0 with kappa_minus*r>w. Child escape and plateau imply

    |z|>=r+S_j*w/3, |z|>d_j*w.                      (10.6)

The latter follows because an input radius at most d_j*w would end with
signed linear radius at most theta*w. Three cases remain.

A. z>0. A negative y could cross from the small-cap ray to at most
d_j*w, contrary to (10.6). Hence y>0 remains positive at the bridge.
Since the effective step exceeds1, this query is saturated and
y=z+h_j^lev*U>u_plus. The first child cannot have returned from the
negative ray, by Section8; all its queries are positive saturated.
Thus s=z+(S_j+h_j^lev)*U. Equation (10.6) gives
s>=r+(2*S_j+h_j^lev)*w/3.

B. z<0,y<0. The bridge is negative saturated, so
|y|=|z|+h_j^lev*w>w/kappa_minus. Apply child escape to y and (10.6)
to obtain |s|>=r+2*S_j*w/3+h_j^lev*w, which suffices.

C. z<0,y>0. Put v=min(kappa_plus*y,U). The crossing obeys
|z|<=d_j*v; by (10.6), v>w. Section8 rules out a return in the first
child because kappa_plus*y>w, so that child is entirely positive and
s>=y+S_j*v. If y<=u_plus, then
|z|=(h_j^lev*kappa_plus-1)*y and

    s-|z|>=(2/kappa_plus+S_j-h_j^lev)*v.

If y>=u_plus, use |z|=h_j^lev*U-y and y>=U/kappa_plus to obtain
the same bound, with v=U. Its coefficient satisfies

    3*(2/kappa_plus+S_j-h_j^lev)-(S_j+h_j^lev)
       =6/kappa_plus+2*S_j-4*h_j^lev
       >=2*A_j-4*d_j>0.                            (10.7)

Therefore s-|z|>=(S_j+h_j^lev)*w/3, and (10.6) supplies the rest.

Zero intermediate states are excluded by the escape hypothesis.
Cases A-C are exhaustive; S_(j+1)=2*S_j+h_j^lev completes the parent
escape proof. Induction establishes the entire state at every depth.
Section8 therefore applies to every small-cap return in the full H_m.
No parent statement was used to prove itself.

## 11. Every amplitude and the true objective

Continue to assume q>q_cut. For0<|s|<=d_m*w choose integer r>=0 with

    d_m*w*theta^(r+1)<|s|<=d_m*w*theta^r.

At a geometric boundary choose it as the upper endpoint of its next cell.
All positive amplitudes in this interval are covered since0<theta<1.
The full plateau yields, on its actual terminal ray sigma,

    kappa_sigma*|Psi_m(s)|<=w*theta^(r+1)<|s|/d_m.

The exact conversion (1.5) gives

    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2<=1/(q*d_m^2).                  (11.1)

Now let |s|>d_m*w. In the comparable-cap case every crossing history
has terminal signed linear radius at most a*w by (8.3), so (1.5)
gives score at most a^2*w^2/(q*s^2)<1/(q*d_m^2).
A no-crossing history obeys the exact objective estimate (4.2).

In the separated-cap case every negative-to-positive return has the
same cap and bound. A history without such a return has at most one
crossing, from positive to negative, and obeys (4.7). Which ray has q
is irrelevant here: the return classification uses the smaller GRADIENT
cap, never an implicit curvature ordering. Every actual terminal is covered.

Consequently all starts in q>q_cut satisfy

    2*f(Psi_m(s))/s^2
      <=max(1/(q*d_m^2),K/(1+2*K*S_m)).              (11.2)

The child statements used to reach (11.2) were explicit signed caps,
exact capture intervals and escape bounds. The desired full objective
profile was never substituted as an unproved child induction hypothesis.

## 12. Uniform objective-scale overlap and exact attainment

For every q>=q_cut,

    (q*d_m)^2>=1+2*q*S_m.                            (12.1)

For a same-m proof put d=d_(m-2)>20, using a>2/5,c>2,m>=1536.
At q_cut=1/(1+d),

    (q_cut*d_m)^2>(9/10)*c^4,
    1+2*q_cut*S_m<1+2*c^2/a<1+5*c^2.

The first inequality uses(d/(1+d))^2>(20/21)^2>9/10; the second uses
S_m<c^m and d_m=c^2*d. For x=c^2>144/25>23/4 the polynomial
(9/10)*x^2-5*x-1 is positive: its value at23/4 is1/160 and its
derivative is positive for x>=23/4. This proves (12.1) at q_cut.
For larger q its equivalent form d_m^2>=1/q^2+2*S_m/q only weakens.

Because K>=q,

    q*d_m^2>=1/q+2*S_m>=1/K+2*S_m,
    1/(q*d_m^2)<=K/(1+2*K*S_m).                     (12.2)

Equations (11.2)-(12.2) give the required upper bound for q>q_cut;
Section5 gives it for q<=q_cut, including equality at the cutoff.
The arbitrary-J construction covers every J<=m-2 at once; no
successive one-top-level enlargement is hidden in this comparison.

For exact attainment choose the K-ray, write u=b_high/K, and start
there at radius |s|=(1+2*K*S_m)*u. For every physical prefix mass P_i,

    |x_i|=u*(1+2*K*S_m-K*P_i)>=u*(1+K*S_m)>=u.

All queries are saturated on that same ray, verifying every update.
The actual final radius is u*(1+K*S_m), and

    2*f(x_(n_m))=K*u^2*(1+2*K*S_m),
    2*f(x_(n_m))/s^2=K/(1+2*K*S_m).                 (12.3)

This establishes (1.1) WITHIN THIS JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, for every
positive independent pair of curvatures and clips at the same m.
Since K<=1, its value is at most1/D_m. This is still a restricted-family
bound; it does not imply the full-W hypothesis in accepted931b9653ce0f7201.

## 13. Adversarial checks and limits

The failed common-K physical and energy d_m^(-2) plateaus remain false.
Their old static witness is

    kappa_plus=1, kappa_minus=1/(4*S_m),
    b_plus=1, b_minus=a/(8*S_m), x_0=1.

Its first leaf gives-a. Every later query is negative saturated; at
remaining prefix mass T its radius is a-b_minus*T>7*a/8>u_minus=a/2.
Its actual terminal radius is a*(7/8+ell/(8*S_m)) and twice-objective
is a^2*(5*S_m+ell)/(32*S_m^2), by direct summation of that fixed gradient.
It lies in the low-q region since4*S_m>h_(m-2)^lev. Thus it does not
contradict the arbitrary-J state; more generally the NEW energy bound
retains1/q and never asserts the old common-K d_m^(-2) scale.

At J=0 the base is the literal leaf; at J>=1 only the q-ray BELOW J
is called invariant. Above J neither sign is assumed monotone. The
gradient clip order can be opposite the curvature order. The geometric
base is proved separately at every amplitude, without false homogeneity.
The separated-cap induction proves parent plateau before parent escape,
and its return bound uses only proper children. Its base positive endpoint
is established independently in (7.5). All thresholds and zero hits remain.

Symbolic counterexample checks did not refute THIS state. That is not a
verification verdict. The principal audit targets are the arbitrary-J
cut inequality (7.3), small-child mass bound (3.3), the source-cap early
return bound (8.1), and the noncircular ordering in Section10. All of
these remain part of the joint unverified draft, not separate facts.

The original problem still concerns every finite dimension and every
smooth convex function, and an affirmative answer must cover every
nonnegative schedule. None of these quantifiers is discharged here.
Even the general scalar class with several curvature/clipping transitions
is outside (1.1). No full-W upper bound, minimax separation, finalized
answer or partial Goal completion is claimed.

## 14. Retrieval and resource account

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples. Current guidance, shared obstacles and
verification traces were recalled. The full preceding report1027af8bac85c55c
and relevant sections of7476ebfe7be45713 were read; accepted
931b9653ce0f7201 and1252b8823bb5a290 statement scopes were checked.
No accepted independent-curvature profile surfaced in fact search.
Earlier joint drafts were not proof premises.

One Matlas repair query returned inapplicable momentum, stochastic ADMM
and extragradient statements. Web fallback supplied silver-schedule
background, including arXiv:2309.16530, without a usable child bound.
No external theorem is imported. This branch stays inside the previously
audited static two-ray clipped family and exact interval maps; it opens
no new body of literature machinery that was rejected unread.

An initial oversized retrieval batch was truncated; the needed prior
report, latest guidance and accepted statement scopes were recovered
through focused reads. Targeted glossary rg returned no matches with
exit1; local symbols are explicitly defined here. The first report
write failed with 'invalid hunk' due to patch formatting and wrote no
file; the corrected administrative write created the report. A later local
simplification patch initially failed with 'Failed to find expected lines'
because its context began mid-line; the full-line correction succeeded.
Both errors were administrative and changed no mathematical store.
Empty or truncated outputs were not treated as proof or full reads.

Local executed operations were administrative text reads/writes and
integrity checks only. Mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/
trajectory/recurrence replay, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal
calls, fact_submit, new accepted facts, installations, consults and
subagents are ZERO. No task overwrite, worker/service control, outward
publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or automatic renewal.

**Entire positive independent-curvature clipped family: JOINT UNVERIFIED.
Original minimax target: OPEN. New fact IDs: none. Return at cap1.**

All following statements form one joint unverified argument; no new fact is adopted. Fix original m>=128,lambda=1+sqrt(2),c=lambda(1+1/m),a=(1-1/m)/c, H_0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j),h=H_m,n=2^m-1. Select any two distinct level-0 checkpoints, so both excesses A=E=a satisfy0<A<sqrt(2)-1<3/7. Keep every original physical block and write T=1+sum block0,M=1+sum block1,N=1+sum block2. Put B=A/2,D=M+B,v=sqrt(A/(4D)),alpha=min{A/2,(v/2)sqrt(B(2N+B))},beta=Bv,delta=A-alpha,u=kappa=0,p=(1,0),q=(0,-v),z=(-alpha,Bv),r=(s,ell)=z/(2N). Let J(y)=max{0,p.y,q.y,r.y},F(x)=min_y[J(y)+||x-y||^2/2],X0=(T+delta,-Dv). The proximal objective has a unique minimizer y and g=x-y in partial J(y); adding subgradient inequalities gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, and the first-order upper/lower proximal comparisons prove that F is convex,differentiable and1-smooth with gradient g. The minimum is0 and its minimizer set is the intersection of the three nonpositive slope halfspaces. p.y<=0 implies y1<=0; q.y<=0 implies y2>=0; r.y<=0 then forces y=0. Thus its actual radius is R_obt^2=(T+A-alpha)^2+D^2v^2.

At a prefix query of cumulative mass t, y=(delta+S0-t,-Dv). Its p-plane exceeds zero, q height Dv^2=A/4, and negative r-height, since delta>=A/2. Earlier prefix mass adds p and improves every comparison. The first checkpoint sends x to(-alpha,-Dv). At middle end y=(-alpha,-Bv), q has height Bv^2>0 and p negative; its margin above r is[B(2N+B)v^2-alpha^2]/(2N)>0, since alpha^2<=B(2N+B)v^2/4. Earlier middle queries add a nonnegative multiple of q and improve the comparisons because q.q=v^2>0=p.q>-Bv^2/(2N)=r.q. The second checkpoint sends x to z. At every tail query y=[1-(1+t)/(2N)]z for0<=t<=N-1; the bracket is positive, r's height positive and p,q heights negative. These prove every actual gradient. For any ordered pair, the exact sampled values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 give Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2=<g_i-g_j,y_j><=0, the accepted1252b8823bb5a290 convention. Include optimum0 with zero gradient/value, so this proves both optimum orientations too. No physical query or remote row is discarded.

The terminal payoff is V_obt=[(2N+1)/(4N^2)](alpha^2+B^2v^2)/R_obt^2. The exact appended holding mass is N: y=[1/2-tau/(2N)]z, so all comparisons hold to tau=N and zero beats r for tau>N. All three separate plane limits equal N. Throughout holding the payoff decreases by2tau||r||^2/R_obt^2. First,middle,terminal affine reserves are delta,Bv^2,||z||^2/(4N), all positive, while all optimum slacks are0.

Targeted FALSE extension: 2s(Eu-alpha)+2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma||r||^2 <=2E(su+ell v)-sigma||r||^2,where sigma=2N-1. Its left minus right is -2s alpha-2ell beta=(alpha^2-B^2v^2)/N>0. Indeed the first cap defining alpha^2 exceeds B^2v^2 because4D>A; the second does because2N>3B. Both follow from D,N>=1 and A<3/7. This refutes precisely the sign-deletion step without s>=0, not any proposed global payoff bound.

Its weakness on the SAME word is explicit. alpha^2+B^2v^2<=A^2/4+A^3/(16D)<=9A^2/32; R_obt^2>=T^2+AM/4; (2N+1)/(4N^2)<=3/(4N). Thus V_obt<=27A^2/(128 N R_obt^2). With actual total mass M_m=T+M+N+2A, bound its prefix,middle,tail,excess contributions separately to get M_m/(N R_obt^2)<=2+4/A+2A. Hence (2M_m-1)V_obt<=(27/32)(A^3+A^2+2A)<=(27/32)(384/343)=324/343<1.

The complete same-word K/ray equality follows from this joint ruler argument, using the definitions of acceptedb98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a. Put rho=c-2<=9/20 and local masses M_j=1+sum H_j. They satisfy M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j, a c^j<=rho M_j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j. Every single-cut term at levelj is at most rho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1): the denominator (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)] has its minimum among occurrences at the first or last, whose short-side mass is M_j and long-side mass at least M_(m-1); use increasing x^2/(2x-1) for the reversed endpoint. Dividing by Huber1/(2M_m-1) bounds each cut by rho^2(2c-1)<=3159/4000<1. For any ordered pair of levels k,l the ratio AE/(TM)<=c rho^2<1/2: if the first lies before the second's local H_l use T>=M_k,M>=M_l; otherwise k<l and local masses x,y>=M_k satisfy x+y=M_l-a c^k, so with theta=a c^k/M_k<=rho,zeta=M_l/M_k>=2+theta obtain AE/(TM)<=theta rho zeta/(zeta-1-theta)<=rho^2(2+rho). The accepted alternating-ray recurrence then gives a post-second-cut budget m0[(AE/(TM))T-(T-1)]<m0. It violates feasibility for exactly two cuts and violates m2>=m0 for more cuts. Thus the entire ray maximum has only zero/single cuts and equals K=1/(2M_m-1). The applicable complete lens reference contains this Huber term by accepteddfde2fa326c908bf. Consequently the counterexample is weaker than all these stated references. C59 is not transferred from its actual H6. The general obtuse endpoint family remains OPEN. The full joint proof,including the ORIGINAL-B comparison and full positive-quadrant closure,is53d8b004885db92c,not an accepted premise.

# Schedule-derived curved inward blocks: one joint UNVERIFIED derivation

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; allocated invocation72.
TASK.md: ended baseline71, ceiling72, attempted ceiling73; cap1.
Plan d1529a5e2f8fd47f. This entire document is ONE research proof attempt.
There was no verifier call, no fact_submit, and no new fact_id. Every new
claim below is UNVERIFIED. The original exact-profile problem is OPEN.

The new mechanism is a scalar spline whose derivative changes within
every positive ordinary block. Each block has its own schedule-derived
slope. Paid connectors join successive inward visits to the same ray.
It changes the physical queried gradients, rather than merely completing
old quadratic or single-cut prefixes at their terminal point.

The result of this search is a precisely scoped obstruction. On the ENTIRE
SAME-enclosing-m H_(m,1/4), for EVERY m>=1536, the constructed payoff V
satisfies

    0<V/H_base<2(25/36)^(m-3)<1,
    B_(2^m-1)V<(237/125)(25/36)^(m-3),                 (0.1)

where H_base=1/(1+2 sum H_m). The stronger statement in Section8 allows
EVERY scalar convex one-smooth derivative within the specified disjoint
inward block geometry, with no curvature palette, no bound on its knot
count, and no constant-gradient assumption. It has the same upper bound.
This is not an upper bound on arbitrary scalar GD or unrestricted W_n.

## 1. Normalization, exact target and recalled scopes

For a nonnegative integer n, a finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,
and L>0, the physical updates are x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)f'(x_t)
in the scalar constructions. In the original problem W_n(h) is the
supremum over all finite-dimensional smooth convex instances of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),               (1.1)

where x_star is a genuine minimizer and x_0 differs from x_star.
Thus W=2R. Our constructed minimum is uniquely0, so our chosen radius
is also the closest radius. No radius is reset at a checkpoint.

Retain exactly the original ALL-integer-split benchmark:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.    (1.2)

The all-word lower-profile objective would require, for every epsilon>0,
one N_epsilon independent of h such that every n>=N_epsilon and every
h>=0 has a legal lower witness of value at least(1-epsilon)/B_n.
Nothing proved here supplies that remaining quantifier or constant.

Accepted inputs, only at their actual scopes:

* fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the normalized interpolation convention.
  All rows for our scalar spline are also proved directly in Section5.
* factb98bb807d514e80d supplies K_n, its legal attaining single-cut and
  canonical Huber functions, and the benchmark definitions(1.2).
* fact5fd396eba75651e7 supplies the exact same-enclosing-m hierarchy,
  the mass identities and parameter bounds in its Section6, and its
  unconditional benchmark inequality(77), reproduced in Section6 here.
  None of its spiral dynamics or family upper bounds is imported.
* fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a is used only for K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n, with the
  full ray family as defined in that fact. No new upper bound for that
  full family is inferred from the present spline obstruction.

The following are recalled drafts, not correctness premises:

1. FULL5100e05d4c62d3ff concerns synchronized maximal completions of
   complete-word canonical/single-cut prefixes. It does not quantify
   arbitrary nonlinear scalar queried trajectories.
2. 25e480a41fc81415 and its extracted law802187ebdff8da78 use only
   fixed curvatures1 and1/2. They do not cover this new spline law.
3. The all-value/all-curvature scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 fixes
   x_i=p_i(theta)=product_(t<i)(1-theta*h_t), g_i=theta*p_i(theta).
   It optimizes compatible VALUES and primitive energy over those fixed
   proportional-gradient histories and theta in[0,1]. Its shortest-path
   formula does not free these positions or gradients. It is not an
   optimization over every scalar derivative, and its primitive energy
   is not the normalized last objective gap. Audit8d78e0975df8400a also
   records a mandatory correction to a printed low-curvature increment.
   No such draft formula is a premise below.
4. 312a773572f0099c treats disjoint inward CONSTANT-gradient blocks at
   the first-occurrence checkpoints of this same small-tau hierarchy.
   The present mechanism removes that constant-gradient assumption.
   Section8 is derived from physical displacements and spatial ordering,
   not by citing that earlier unverified obstruction as a lemma.

There is no assertion that these recalled limitations exhaust scalar
nonlinearity. In particular, overlapping returns and extra crossings
inside an ordinary block are deliberately outside Section8.

## 2. An explicit all-word selector and backward knot rule

Everything in this section is a finite symbolic definition from the
entire known word. No mathematical recurrence was executed in this round.

Select, in chronological order, precisely the strict record steps

    h_j>2 and h_j>max({0} union {h_t:t<j}).              (2.1)

Let their count be k. If k=0, use the canonical Huber fallback specified
below. If k>=1, write the ORIGINAL word as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k),
    block_i=(alpha_(i,0),...,alpha_(i,r_i-1)),
    s_i=sum_(v<r_i)alpha_(i,v).                         (2.2)

The integer r_i is the physical length of block_i. Empty blocks are
allowed. No step is reordered or deleted; zero steps retain their indices.

For every0<=i<=k put

    eta_i=1/[4(1+s_i)],
    P_(i,l)=product_(0<=v<l)(1-eta_i*alpha_(i,v)),
    P_i=P_(i,r_i),
    T_i=(P_i^(-1)-1)/eta_i.                            (2.3)

An empty product is1. Every factor is positive, because each
alpha_(i,v)<=s_i and eta_i*s_i<1/4. The elementary product inequality
product(1-z_v)>=1-sum z_v for0<=z_v<=1 follows by multiplying the
two-factor identity(1-z)(1-w)=1-z-w+zw and inducting. Thus

    3/4<P_i<=1, 0<eta_i<=1/4.                          (2.4)

Another exact identity is

    T_i=sum_(l<r_i)alpha_(i,l)*P_(i,l)/P_i >= s_i.     (2.5)

Indeed P_(i,l)-P_(i,l+1)=eta_i alpha_(i,l)P_(i,l);
sum and divide by eta_i P_i. Each P_(i,l)>=P_i. These are
symbolic identities, not evaluated trajectory or numerical products.

Define the final gradient magnitude q_k, terminal radius R_k, initial
radius A_k and initial gradient magnitude p_k for the last block by

    q_k=1, R_k=1/eta_k,
    A_k=1/(eta_k P_k), p_k=1/P_k.                      (2.6)

Let D_i=b_i-1>1 for i<k. For stage k-1 set

    q_(k-1)=A_k/D_(k-1), R_(k-1)=q_(k-1),
    A_(k-1)=R_(k-1)+T_(k-1)q_(k-1),
    p_(k-1)=q_(k-1)/P_(k-1).                         (2.7)

For i=k-2,...,0 define backward

    q_i=[A_(i+1)+A_(i+2)-p_(i+2)]/D_i,
    R_i=A_(i+2)+q_i-p_(i+2),
    A_i=R_i+T_i q_i, p_i=q_i/P_i.                     (2.8)

These are finite explicit sums, products and quotients with positive
known denominators. They are not an interpolation-feasibility oracle.
The law uses this spline exactly when its explicit guards hold:

    q_i>0 for all i, and q_i>=p_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2.    (2.9)

Otherwise use the canonical Huber fallback. The guard is a finite list
of explicit positivity and nesting comparisons, with no parameter
optimization or search tree.

On the accepted branch, A_i>=p_i>0 and R_i>=q_i>0. For the final
stage these follow from eta_k<=1. For stage k-1 use
p_i=q_i+eta_i T_i q_i and A_i=q_i+T_i q_i. Backward, if
A_(i+2)>=p_(i+2), equation(2.8) gives R_i>=q_i, and the
same identity proves A_i>=p_i. Moreover the guard gives

    R_i>=A_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2.                        (2.10)

For every i<k, the SAME definitions give the physical crossing identity

    b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1).                              (2.11)

For i=k-1 this is D_i q_i=A_k. For earlier i it is the
first two equations of(2.8) rearranged. For all i including k,
A_i=R_i+T_i q_i, because(2.6) and(2.3) imply it at k.

The fallback is fully explicit: S=sum h, u=1/(1+2S)>0,
g_H(x)=max(-u,min(x,u)), F_H(x)=integral_0^x g_H(t)dt,
with initial point1. All physical positions are

    x_t=1-u sum_(v<t)h_v>=1-uS=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=u.

Thus every queried gradient is u, the actual terminal is1-uS,
the minimum is uniquely0, and2F_H(x_n)=1/(1+2S).
This also handles the empty or all-zero word, with its true terminal.

## 3. One static derivative on the whole real line, and ALL its areas

Assume the guards(2.9). Stage i uses ray sign sigma_i=(-1)^i.
For each parity construct its radial derivative Q_sigma on r>=0
starting with its innermost stage, then moving OUTWARD by decreasing i
in increments of2. The symbol sigma here denotes a sign, not an index.

On the ray containing stage k define

    Q_(sigma_k)(r)=eta_k*r for0<=r<=A_k.               (3.1)

On the other ray, whose innermost stage is k-1, define

    Q_(sigma_(k-1))(r)=r for0<=r<=q_(k-1),
    Q_(sigma_(k-1))(r)=q_(k-1)+eta_(k-1)(r-q_(k-1))
                         for q_(k-1)<=r<=A_(k-1).     (3.2)

For each outer stage i<=k-2 on its own ray, append

    Q_(sigma_i)(r)=p_(i+2)+(r-A_(i+2))
                                 for A_(i+2)<=r<=R_i,
    Q_(sigma_i)(r)=q_i+eta_i(r-R_i)
                                 for R_i<=r<=A_i.     (3.3)

At r=R_i the first expression is q_i by(2.8). At r=A_i the
second is q_i+eta_i T_i q_i=q_i/P_i=p_i. The innermost formulas
likewise have their required gradient at A_i. Guard(2.10) orders all
these intervals. A connector of zero width is kept as a common endpoint.
If s_i=0, then P_i=1,T_i=0,A_i=R_i,p_i=q_i, so that block is a
single point with the same gradient. There is no inconsistent knot.

Beyond the outermost A_i on each ray, continue constantly with value
p_i. Finally define globally

    g(x)=Q_+(x) for x>=0, g(x)=-Q_-(-x) for x<=0,
    F(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.                         (3.4)

Every piece of Q_sigma has slope0, eta_i, or1. All pieces match.
Consequently g is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz on each
ray. Across zero, Q_sigma(r)<=r, so for x<=0<=y,
0<=g(y)-g(x)<=y-x. This proves the same global properties. Thus F
is globally convex, C1, and1-smooth, without any unexamined finite-jet
extension. In fact its Lipschitz constant is exactly1, because(3.2)
has a nonempty unit-slope core. On both rays Q_sigma(r)>0 for r>0.
Hence the ENTIRE minimizer set is{0} and min F=F(0)=0.

Here is the explicit area rule, which fixes every queried value as well
as every unsampled value. Write I_i(r)=F(sigma_i*r) on its own ray.
For the final core use

    I_k(r)=eta_k r^2/2, 0<=r<=A_k.                    (3.5)

For the other innermost core use I_(k-1)(r)=r^2/2 up to q_(k-1).
On any nonterminal slow interval [R_i,A_i] put

    I_i(r)=I_i(R_i)+q_i(r-R_i)+eta_i(r-R_i)^2/2.       (3.6)

For i=k-1, I_i(R_i)=q_i^2/2. For an outer i<=k-2,

    I_i(R_i)=I_(i+2)(A_(i+2))+(q_i^2-p_(i+2)^2)/2,
    I_i(r)=I_(i+2)(A_(i+2))+p_(i+2)(r-A_(i+2))
                                  +(r-A_(i+2))^2/2
                       on[A_(i+2),R_i].              (3.7)

The equality for I_i(R_i) is the integral of the unit connector,
whose length is q_i-p_(i+2). The final interval also obeys(3.6)
with I_k(R_k)=1/(2eta_k), since q_k=eta_k R_k=1.
Beyond an outermost A_i use its matching linear continuation with
slope p_i. These formulas cover the whole real line and every area
between its knots. No old height is erased, independently reassigned,
or replaced by a locally chosen objective zero.

## 4. Actual physical GD, total spatial ordering and original terminal

Start at x_0=A_0. At the l-th physical query in block_i, including
its endpoint l=r_i before the checkpoint or terminal, the claimed
and ACTUAL point and gradient are

    x_(i,l)=sigma_i[R_i+(q_i/eta_i)(P_(i,l)/P_i-1)],
    g_(i,l)=sigma_i q_i P_(i,l)/P_i.                   (4.1)

Every prefix product lies between P_i and1. Thus its bracket lies
in[R_i,A_i], where the derivative is exactly(3.1)--(3.3).
Applying its next original physical step alpha_(i,l) gives

    x_(i,l)-alpha_(i,l)g_(i,l)
      =sigma_i[R_i+(q_i/eta_i)(P_(i,l+1)/P_i-1)],

by the defining factor of the product. This proves every update;
no block was replaced by its sum for the dynamics. For l=0 the
point is sigma_i A_i; for l=r_i it is sigma_i R_i and its
gradient is sigma_i q_i. The checkpoint uses(2.11) and lands at

    sigma_i R_i-b_i sigma_i q_i=-sigma_i A_(i+1).      (4.2)

Equations(4.1)--(4.2) therefore describe the complete original run.
All knots were fixed before this run, as functions of the known h.

Spatial order is fully specified. On each ray, the radial intervals
are ordered inward by i,i+2,i+4,... according to(2.10). Within an
interval, radii decrease with chronological l, strictly at every
positive alpha and equally across a zero alpha. Opposite rays are
separated by zero. On the negative ray, increasing radius means a
smaller real position, so real-coordinate ordering reverses that
radial list. This explicitly distinguishes chronology from spatial
order. The only possible repeated points in this law are repeated
positions from zero steps, collapsed zero-mass blocks, and shared
same-ray endpoints when a connector guard is equality. In each case
(3.4)--(3.7) assign identical gradients and values. Checkpoints cross
zero without landing at zero because every A_i>0. All intermediate
knots crossed by a jump are already part of the same function.

The physical terminal, after EVERY original step, is exactly

    x_n=sigma_k/eta_k,
    g(x_n)=sigma_k,
    F(x_n)=1/(2eta_k),
    V_spline(h)=1/(eta_k A_0^2).                       (4.3)

If the last ordinary step is zero, its two products in(4.1) coincide,
so(4.3) is the repeated last jet with its original value. If the last
step is a selected record, block_k is empty and(4.2) itself lands at
the terminal(4.3), since P_k=1. A short positive final step is simply
the last factor in P_k. None of these cases is completed at a different
horizon or has its last leaf removed.

The actual initial closest radius is A_0. If unit normalization is
desired, replace F by F_norm(x)=F(A_0 x)/A_0^2, divide every knot
position and derivative value by A_0, and start from1. The area
values divide by A_0^2. Its minimum is uniquely0, its exact global
Lipschitz constant remains1, and the payoff stays(4.3). For general
advertised L use f=L*F_norm and physical steps h_t/L. This preserves
every normalized point and proves the claimed W=2R scaling.

For every word set V_law equal to(4.3) on the accepted branch and
to H_base on the fallback branch. Explicitly, for S=sum h,

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
       max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/
          [(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]},

with the inner maximum omitted at n=0 and(t)_+=max(t,0).
The finite explicit lower selector

    V_new(h)=max{K_n(h),V_law(h)}<=W_n(h)              (4.4)

chooses the attaining accepted K_n instance if that entry is larger.
This defines a lower witness for all finite nonnegative words, including
zero, huge, unordered and repeated steps. It asserts no all-word
near-benchmark comparison for(4.4).

## 5. Every original gradient and value is globally compatible

For any u<v put Delta=g(v)-g(u)>=0. Global monotonicity and
one-Lipschitz continuity give0<=Delta<=v-u and, for u<=t<=v,

    g(t)-g(u)>=max(0,Delta-(v-t)),
    g(v)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-u)).

Integrating each lower bound gives respectively

    F(v)-F(u)-g(u)(v-u)>=Delta^2/2,
    F(u)-F(v)-g(v)(u-v)>=Delta^2/2.                   (5.1)

Thus, in the orientation of accepted1252b8823bb5a290, EVERY ordered
query/terminal/optimum pair satisfies

    Q_ij=F(x_i)-F(x_j)+g(x_i)(x_j-x_i)
                              +(g(x_i)-g(x_j))^2/2<=0. (5.2)

Equal points give equality. This proves remote rows and both directions,
not only neighboring chronological interpolation. The full real Gram of
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) is zz^T for the actual scalar list z, hence is PSD.
After unit normalization z is divided by A_0. All zero-step coordinates
remain present. This is an actual primal construction and not a PSD-only
claim about a free Gram.

## 6. The decisive same-enclosing-m hierarchy

Fix ANY integer m>=1536, with exactly

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda(1+1/(4m)), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),0<=j<m,
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.                  (6.1)

All children use this SAME enclosing m and tau=1/4. Accepted fact
5fd396eba75651e7, equations(36)--(38),(77), gives

    2<c<121/50<5/2, 0<a<21/50<1/2,
    d_j/M_j<21/50,
    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)2^j, v=a/(c-2) in(0,1),
    B_n/(1+2S_m)<237/250.                            (6.2)

We use only these unconditional statements about the actual word and
original benchmark. No benchmark recurrence is run.

The level0 step is1+a<2; the level1 step is1+beta<2;
d_2=beta*c>2, since beta>5/6 and c>12/5. Steps increase
strictly with their tree level. Hence the record selector(2.1) picks
EXACTLY the first occurrences of levels2,3,...,m-1:

    k=m-2, b_i=1+d_(i+2), D_i=d_(i+2),
    block_0=H_2, block_i=H_(i+1) for1<=i<=k,
    s_0=S_2, s_i=S_(i+1) for1<=i<=k.                 (6.3)

The selected physical zero-based checkpoint index is2^(i+2)-1.
This is the full original expansion of H_m, not a contracted word.
In particular the last block is the entire H_(m-1), including its
original final level0 step1+a. Its length is2^(m-1)-1.

Several elementary consequences will be used explicitly:

    D_(i+1)=c D_i, s_(i+1)=S_(i+2)>D_i,
    s=S_(m-1)>D=d_(m-1),
    T=1+2s, S_m=2s+1+D, 1+2S_m<3T,
    1+S_2=4+2a+beta>4.                               (6.4)

For S_j>d_j, note S_1=1+a>beta=d_1 and
S_(j+1)-d_(j+1)=2(S_j-d_j)+1+(3-c)d_j>0.
The ratio bound in(6.2) also gives M_j>(50/21)d_j.

## 7. The spline guards hold, and the queried law is genuinely nonlinear

On(6.3), the final base gives

    q_(k-1)=A_k/D=4(1+s)/(D P_k)>4q_k,
    p_k=q_k/P_k<(4/3)q_k.                             (7.1)

Suppose backward that q_(i+1)>(4/3)q_(i+2). Then
p_(i+2)<(4/3)q_(i+2)<q_(i+1). The crossing identity at i+1
and A_(i+1)=R_(i+1)+T_(i+1)q_(i+1) yield

    D_i q_i=(b_(i+1)+T_(i+1))q_(i+1)-p_(i+2)
            >(D_(i+1)+T_(i+1))q_(i+1)
            >(c+1)D_i q_(i+1).                       (7.2)

The last inequality uses(2.5),(6.4). Thus
q_i>(c+1)q_(i+1)>(4/3)q_(i+1), and q_i>p_(i+2).
Starting with(7.1), backward induction proves EVERY guard strictly.
The spline branch, not the fallback, is used for every m>=1536.

Every block in(6.3) has positive length and positive steps. Hence
P_i<1 and p_i>q_i, so EVERY ordinary block samples decreasing,
nonconstant derivative magnitudes. There are m-2 distinct eta_i:
s_0=s_1=S_2, followed by the strictly increasing masses S_3,...,
S_(m-1). Their number is unbounded with m. The connectors have
positive lengths, and the number of actual spatial knots grows with m.

This is not a proportional-gradient polynomial history. Already in
block k-1, on its ray

    Q(r)/r=eta_(k-1)+(1-eta_(k-1))q_(k-1)/r

is strictly decreasing with r in a nonempty sampled interval. Two
distinct queries therefore have distinct nonzero gradient-to-position
ratios about the genuine minimum0. Changing objective values cannot
turn these gradients into g_i=theta*x_i for a single theta.
Even allowing a different affine reference point does not repair this:
successive distinct queries in block i have gradient-difference divided
by position-difference exactly eta_i. Blocks k-1 and k have different
eta_i and both contain positive steps, so no single affine relation
g=theta*x+d fits all their sampled gradients.

All query positions are strictly ordered inside each ordinary block,
and each same-ray block is strictly inside its preceding same-ray
block. Opposite-ray overshoots are the actual selected crossings(4.2).
Thus(4.1) gives the complete spatial order, including every unselected
large step in each H_j. This construction has no ties on this positive
hierarchy; ties and zero steps for general words were addressed above.

In particular, after the ORIGINAL final1+a step, its exact terminal is

    x_n=(-1)^(m-2)*4(1+S_(m-1)),
    g_n=(-1)^(m-2),
    F_n=2(1+S_(m-1)),
    V_spline(H_m)=4(1+S_(m-1))/A_0^2.                (7.3)

Here A_0 is precisely the finite expression(2.3),(2.6)--(2.8), using
the original individual steps in all the products. There is no claimed
substitution of a block mass for those products.
More explicitly, with ell=1+a and e=eta_k, the last preterminal query is

    x_(n-1)=sigma_k/[e(1-e*ell)],
    g_(n-1)=sigma_k/(1-e*ell),
    F_(n-1)=1/[2e(1-e*ell)^2].

Its original final update gives x_(n-1)-ell*g_(n-1)=sigma_k/e,
exactly(7.3). The denominator is positive by(2.4); the final leaf and
its queried value have both been retained.

## 8. Whole-class bound: arbitrary curvature inside disjoint inward blocks

This section proves a broader scoped obstruction jointly with the law.
No previously unverified shelf lemma is invoked.

Fix the word(6.1) and the exact decomposition(6.3). Let F be ANY scalar
convex C1 one-smooth function with an attained minimum. Translate and
reflect so that a closest minimizer to its positive initial point is0,
the minimum value is0, and x_0=A_0>0. A minimum interval extending
leftwards from0 is allowed. Suppose its ACTUAL trajectory has these
properties, for these precise original blocks and checkpoints:

(a) Block i starts at sigma_i A_i and ends at sigma_i R_i,
    with A_i>=R_i>0. Every query and update endpoint of that block
    stays on this ray, with a positive derivative magnitude.
(b) The original checkpoint b_i crosses to sigma_(i+1) A_(i+1).
(c) R_i>=A_(i+2) for0<=i<=k-2: a later visit to a ray is wholly
    inside the preceding visit's ending radius.

No derivative is prescribed on any block, connector, or unsampled
interval. No bounded curvature palette or finite knot bound is imposed.
Let q_i=|F'(sigma_i R_i)| and let delta_i=A_i-R_i be the actual
ordinary-block displacement. Static monotonicity on each ray gives

    delta_i=sum_(l<r_i)alpha_(i,l)|F'(x_(i,l))|
                   >=s_i q_i,
    R_i>=q_i, b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1).                    (8.1)

The second inequality uses F'(0)=0 and the global Lipschitz bound.
The final equality is the actual checkpoint update. Crucially, this
does NOT identify displacement with s_i q_i when gradients vary.

For0<=i<=k-2, the actual spatial guard(c) and(8.1) yield

    b_i q_i=R_i+A_(i+1)
       >=A_(i+2)+A_(i+1)
       =b_(i+1)q_(i+1)+delta_(i+1)
       >=(b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))q_(i+1).                  (8.2)

This is the added return-distance cost for this entire broader class.
It does not depend on the number of spatial knots or any affine
formula inside the blocks.

Put d=D_i=d_(i+2)>2 and M=M_(i+2). Then from(6.2)--(6.3),

    (b_(i+1)+s_(i+1))/b_i=(c d+M)/(1+d)
       >(c+50/21)*d/(1+d)
       >(2/3)(c+50/21)>c+1/2.                       (8.3)

For the last comparison, its difference is
100/63-c/3-1/2>100/63-5/6-1/2=16/63>0.
Consequently q_i>(c+1/2)q_(i+1) throughout this range.
At the final checkpoint, R_(k-1)>=q_(k-1) gives
D q_(k-1)>=A_k. Also A_0>= (1+s_0)q_0. Combining gives

    A_0>(1+S_2)(c+1/2)^(m-3)*A_k/D.                 (8.4)

We now bound the FULL actual final objective, retaining arbitrary
inner areas. Let r=R_k be its actual final radius, q=q_k its final
gradient magnitude, and s=S_(m-1). Monotonicity and Lipschitz
continuity from0 give Q(t)<=min(t,q) for0<=t<=r, with r>=q.
Equation(8.1) gives r<=A_k-sq. Hence

    2F(x_n)<=2qr-q^2
             <=2q A_k-(2s+1)q^2
             <=A_k^2/(1+2s).                         (8.5)

The last inequality is equivalent to
((1+2s)q-A_k)^2>=0. If a terminal derivative is zero, its terminal
point is a minimizer and the payoff is0; the same bound follows without
dividing by q. The hypotheses with positive earlier stages are enough.
A flat part of the minimizer interval contributes no integral and cannot
increase(8.5). All retained actual objective areas are included in this
integral upper bound.

Divide(8.5) by the TRUE initial squared distance A_0^2 and use(8.4):

    V=2F(x_n)/A_0^2
       <D^2/[(1+S_2)^2(c+1/2)^(2(m-3))(1+2s)].       (8.6)

By(6.4), D=a*c^(m-1), a<1/2, c<5/2 and1+S_2>4,

    V/H_base
      <[3a^2 c^4/(1+S_2)^2]
                              [c/(c+1/2)]^(2(m-3))
      <2(25/36)^(m-3).                               (8.7)

Indeed the first factor is below1875/1024<2, and
c/(c+1/2)<5/6. Since25/36<3/4 and m-3>=5, the last expression
is below2(3/4)^5=243/512<1. This proves the class bound for EVERY
m>=1536, with no pointwise-parameter/limit exchange.

Our constructed spline satisfies(a)--(c), by Sections3--7, and has
the exact payoff(7.3). Combining(8.7) with the accepted exact benchmark
comparison(6.2) proves(0.1). Thus its B-scaled value tends to0.
This conclusion concerns its actual original terminal, true initial
radius, and full function values.

## 9. Comparison with the canonical K and ray channels

Because F_ray_n>=K_n>=H_base by the accepted constructions,
equation(8.7) proves directly that this ENTIRE disjoint inward class
cannot beat either complete canonical reference on the same word.
This conclusion needs no upper estimate on the full ray channel.

For completeness, K_n(H_m)=H_base can be derived here for every
m>=1536. Consider ANY physical cut at tree level j with excess d_j.
Its actual preceding/following masses P,T satisfy
p=1+P>=M_j and t=1+T>=M_j, because its immediate neighboring
children are both the original H_j. The ratio of its accepted cut
payoff to H_base is

    d_j^2(2p+2t+2d_j-1)/[p^2(2t-1)].                 (9.1)

For fixed d_j,t, this decreases in p, since it is a positive
combination of p^(-1) and p^(-2). For fixed p it decreases in t,
since it equals(d_j/p)^2[1+2(p+d_j)/(2t-1)]. Put u=d_j/M_j.
At p=t=M_j, (9.1) is

    u^2[(4+2u)M_j-1]/(2M_j-1)
      =u^2[2+u+(1+u)/(2M_j-1)]
      <=u^2(3+2u)<(1/2)^2(3+1)=1.                  (9.2)

Here M_j>=1 and0<u<21/50<1/2 from(6.2). This includes
j=0, every tied-level occurrence, and the actual last step, whose
following child is H_0 and whose true following mass is0. Hence every
single-cut value is strictly below H_base, and the canonical term
attains H_base. Thus(4.4) satisfies

    V_new(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=H_base,
    B_n V_new(H_m)<237/250.                            (9.3)

If the entire accepted F_ray_n channel is added to the selector, the
spline still contributes nothing on this hierarchy: the augmented
selector equals F_ray_n there. We do NOT assert a new formula or a new
B-scaled upper bound for that larger augmented selector. The precise
statement proved is that the new nonlinear law cannot improve it.

## 10. Adversarial scope and endpoint tests

First direct attempt: permit curvature to vary inside every previously
constant block. Equations(2.3)--(7.3) implement that attempt as an actual
function, with changing gradients and retained integral values. The
exact payoff and(8.7) show why this law still fails the diagnostic.

Second direct attempt within the SAME mechanism: could an entirely
different derivative, arbitrary knots and arbitrary inner areas repair
the loss while retaining the same inward block order? Equations(8.1)--
(8.7) answer negatively for that precisely stated class. Affineness and
the selected eta_i disappear from its proof. This is not a second
unrelated law and does not consume an unallocated additional round.

The construct-counterexamples screen tests two tempting extrapolations.

UNGUARDED RECORD POLICY IS FALSE. For h=(100,101), both steps are
records and all three ordinary blocks are empty. Then eta_i=1/4,
P_i=1,T_i=0, q_2=1,A_2=4, q_1=A_1=p_1=1/25 and

    q_0=(1/25+4-1)/99=76/2475<p_2=1.

Thus R_0=A_2+q_0-p_2<A_2, and the proposed OUTWARD connector
would have negative length. Guard(2.9) correctly rejects it. An
unconditional assertion that the record recipe always yields the stated
ordered spline would be false. The defined fallback gives a legal
all-word construction; no interpolation feasibility oracle fills this
gap, and no all-word guarantee is inferred from fallback existence.

DISJOINT RETURNS ARE NOT AUTOMATIC FOR SCALAR GD. On the single static
function F(x)=x^2/4, starting at1, use the original word
(1,6,1,6,1). Its exact points are

    1, 1/2, -1, -1/2, 1, 1/2.

Its derivative is x/2, its unique minimizer is0, and each update
follows by multiplying by1-h/2. Select the two6 checkpoints, with
ordinary blocks(1),(1),(1). Stage0 and stage2 visit the SAME positive
interval[1/2,1], so R_0=1/2<A_2=1. All the physical data are
legal, but condition(c) fails. This exact example refutes deleting the
disjointness hypothesis from the geometry argument. It is a boundary
test, not the new witness law and not a claimed same-H_m separator.

The task's same-H_m requirement is met for the constructed law and
the entire class in Section8. It is not met for every scalar function:
neither extra crossings inside those H_j blocks, overlapping visits,
nor another checkpoint architecture is covered. The missing all-word
selection/gluing statement remains a theorem-level problem. Failure
of this prescribed family says nothing adverse about the unrestricted
W_n(H_m), and it cannot serve as a negative minimax resolution.
