# Bounded full primitive discovery and four exact original witnesses

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max3 round41,
the first of the two rounds allocated after completed40, with completed
ceiling42 and attempted ceiling43. No fact_submit is authorized or called.
The original exact-profile problem, with W=2R, remains OPEN.

Accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, for the full primitive
identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original interpolation orientation;
and fact4c92d3fc4eb6153e for the conditional fixed-b3 capacity thresholds.
Every new mathematical assertion used in this document is proved jointly.
The earlier C80 and max2 longer-seed drafts suggested witnesses but are
not premises. Numerical infeasibility is not an exact proof.

## 1. Finite experiment, scope and resources

The PUBLIC directory containing this document retains the stdlib atomic
meter, eight pre-registered exact word plans, every Wolfram request,
complete raw result, parsed copy and final-accounting receipt. The original
model uses all r(r+1) ordered off-diagonal Lambda entries, with no rank,
support, reflection, scalar-factor or zero-remainder restriction. Every
original gradient remains formal and distinct. This is primitive
certificate feasibility, not full-objective PEP.

Both charged calibrations passed: h=(1) has Lambda01=2,Lambda10=4 and
the nonzero PSD matrix [[1,-1],[-1,1]]; h=(2) is exactly rejected by
the diagonal contradiction in Section2. The backend was existing Wolfram
Language15.0.1/CSDP, tolerance10^-8. No package was installed.

The frozen discovery words and actual outcomes were:

| Name | Physical word h | Capacity A | Registered screens | Conic outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| f5a | (3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,7/5) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f5b | (31/20,47/20,5/4,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f5c | (3/2,5/2,23/20,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | global cut fails | no solve |
| f5d | (31/20,5/2,11/10,12/5,27/20) | 99/10 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6a | (3/2,5/2,5/4,39/20,5/2,131/100) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6b | (159/100,5/2,13/10,191/100,5/2,121/100) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |
| f6c | (3/2,49/20,5/4,103/50,5/2,5/4) | 1201/100 | adjacent pair fails | no solve |
| f6d | (31/20,49/20,5/4,201/100,5/2,5/4) | 1201/100 | all pass | numerical infeasible |

All six conic rejections have the raw warnings pinf/nsolc, Infinity
objective and Indeterminate multipliers. No numerical array or eigenvalue
was silently converted into a certificate or impossibility theorem.
Sections4--7 independently give exact actual-function rejections of f5c,
f6c,f5a and f6a. The other four rows remain numerical rejection reports only.

There were10 charged cases:2 calibrations and8 distinct discovery words
in2 families. Eight cases used one conic solve each; two used none.
Measured kernel construction/solve/readback time was1.972763 seconds.
A conservative0.5-second export/local-postprocessing guard per case,
which is not a claimed actual duration, makes6.972763 accounted seconds.
Discovery stopped at case10. With no credible reconstruction target,
the remaining2 slots and83.027237 seconds were retired and the ledger
was closed. No old ledger changed, and no correction retry occurred.

All exact witness work below is textual proof construction, with no
additional evaluator, numerical check, machine algebra or coefficient
reconstruction job. No additional physical proposal is assessed here.

## 2. Exact equivalence of the implemented original PSD model

For a word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1))>=0 set s=sum h_i and A=1+s. Let e be
the all-ones column of length r+1, T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, with
the other entries zero. Set

 L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)),
 M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

The original constraints are Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j,
L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on all of R^(r+1).

Under the original flow, e^T D e=-sum h_i^2-s^2, while

 e^T LT e=omega^T T e
   =-2sum_i h_i(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)+2s(1+s)
   =sum_i h_i^2+s^2.

Thus e^T M e=0. If M is PSD then M e=0: for every real vector v,
nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of sufficiently
small real t forces v^T M e=0. Conversely, when M e=0, use the
invertible matrix P=[e_0,...,e_(r-1),e], where e_i are coordinate
columns. The exact congruence is P^T M P=diag(M[0:r,0:r],0).
Consequently the implemented equations M e=0 and the leading r-square
PSD constraint are equivalent to the FULL original PSD constraint.
They impose no extra rank or support restriction. The original complete
M, every sign and every flow row are retained in the raw readback.

For h=(1), the array [[0,2],[4,0]] gives flow(-2,2) and
M=[[1,-1],[-1,1]], with quadratic(u-v)^2. For h=(2), writing
Lambda01=a>=0, flow gives Lambda10=a+4 and M=[[-a,2],[2,a-4]].
PSD would force a<=0 and a>=4. This contradiction is independent of
the solver and completes the two calibration references.

## 3. Full original identity and the actual-function witness method

For physical GD x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i with g_i=grad F(x_i), write
f_i=F(x_i) and use the accepted orientation

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The primitive energy is

 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The cited accepted identity states, under the original flow,

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Therefore any ACTUAL legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every
original nonnegative Lambda with full PSD M: the left side would be
negative and the right side nonnegative. No support selection is needed.

Here is a common complete construction for the planar witnesses.
Let K be a compact convex set containing0 in its interior and define

 F(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Completing the square shows that the unique maximizing g is the
Euclidean projection P_K(x). The two projection normal inequalities at
x and x' imply ||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(x'),x-x'>, hence the
projection is1-Lipschitz. The maximum formulas bound the differentiability
remainder between0 and ||P_K(x+z)-P_K(x)|| ||z||<=||z||^2. Thus F is
convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with grad F=P_K. It has unique
minimum F(0)=0: for x!=0 a sufficiently small positive epsilon has
epsilon x in K and gives F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.

If the displayed g_i are projections, let y_i=x_i-g_i. Then
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j><=0

for EVERY ordered pair, by the projection normal inequality at j.
The optimum jet is x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Its two directions satisfy
Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-<g_i,y_i><=0 because0 belongs to K.
This specifies all remote, terminal and optimum rows, not just adjacent
ones. Telescoping ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2 gives

 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_r+s g_r||^2.

In the constructions below x_r=g_r, so V_h=||x_0||^2-A^2||g_r||^2.
The unique minimizer is0, hence the true squared initial distance is
exactly ||x_0||^2 in every case.

The scalar screens used the interval version K=[-eta,1]: the derivative
is clip(x,[-eta,1]). At a selected step b>1, let P=1+prefix sum and
D=1+suffix sum, and choose eta=(b-1)/D. Start x_0=P. Gradients are1
before and at b, then -eta through the terminal point x_r=-eta.
Every point is on the claimed constant-gradient side of this SAME
function. Its energy is P^2-A^2(b-1)^2/D^2. Thus the original global
cut is A(b-1)<=PD. Steps<=1 contribute a vacuous nonnegative test.

The other scalar screens use the exact convex quadratic F_z(x)=z x^2/2,
z in{1/4,1/2,3/4,1}, x_0=1. With p_0=1 and
p_(i+1)=(1-z h_i)p_i, substitution gives

 V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i^2
                 -s z[1+A z]p_r^2.

At z=1 this reduces to1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Testing those four
curvatures makes no assertion about every curvature or nonlinear law.

For completeness, the adjacent-pair screen is also an original actual
function. Let the pair be b,c>1, put B=b-1,C=c-1,
P=1+prefix sum,D=1+suffix sum,U=b+c+D-1. If D>BC, set

 kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/kappa,
 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,
 K=conv{u,v,w}.

Then0<t<1 and positive weights B,c,D express0 as an interior
convex combination. Prefix queries lie at positive multiples>=1 of u,
the two pair updates are u -> -B u -> D w, and suffix queries lie at
positive multiples>=1 of w. Their gradients are respectively u,v,w.
At -B u, with y=-B u-v, the exact comparisons are

 <w-v,y>=[-B^2 C+kappa t]/c^2=0,
 <u-v,y>=-B(1-t)C/c<0.

At a prefix normal a u, a>=0, both comparisons to v-u and w-u are
nonpositive. At a suffix normal a w, a>=0, the comparisons to u-w
and v-w are0 and -a t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0. Thus Section3's full row formula
applies and V_h=P^2-A^2 t, where A=P+U. Expansion gives

 A^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa
       =U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2 D}.

The resulting necessary inequality is P^2 D>=BC[P^2+2BP+BU].
The evaluator also contains a stop at D<=BC, recalled from the earlier
C80 draft. This document does not import that unverified branch: EVERY
applicable adjacent pair of EVERY registered word had D>BC, as the
retained exact raw outputs show. No D<=BC rejection occurred. Only the
fully proved D>BC branch was used in this finite batch or in this proof.

## 4. Exact scalar rejection of f5c

Take h=(3/2,5/2,23/20,12/5,27/20), A=99/10. At physical index1,
P=5/2,D=59/10 and eta=15/59. The scalar function is explicitly

 F(x)=x-1/2                    for x>=1,
 F(x)=x^2/2                    for -eta<=x<=1,
 F(x)=-eta*x-eta^2/2            for x<=-eta.

It is convex differentiable1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. The entire
trajectory is

 x=(5/2,1,-3/2,-285/236,-141/236,-15/59),
 g=(1,1,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59,-15/59).

Each listed update uses its original physical step, and every point
lies in the stated branch. The values are F(x_i); Section3 supplies
all original and optimum interpolation residuals. The true squared
distance is25/4. The global cut slack is

 PD-A(b-1)=(5/2)(59/10)-(99/10)(3/2)=-1/10.

The exact original primitive energy is

 V_h=(5/2)^2-(99/10)^2(15/59)^2
       =(295^2-297^2)/118^2=-296/3481<0.

Thus f5c cannot have any full original primitive certificate.

## 5. Exact planar rejection of f6c

Take h=(3/2,49/20,5/4,103/50,5/2,5/4), A=1201/100, and the
adjacent pair at physical indices3,4. The parameters of Section3 are

 P=31/5, B=53/50, C=3/2, D=9/4, U=581/100,
 D-BC=33/50, kappa=138/25, t=2809/9200.

Use u,w,v,K,F as in Section3; here v=-(53/125)u-(9/10)w.
The COMPLETE original trajectory and gradients are

 x=((31/5)u,(47/10)u,(9/4)u,u,-(53/50)u,(9/4)w,w),
 g=(u,u,u,u,v,w,w).

Every update is exact. Section3 checks each projection normal, every
ordered interpolation row and both optimum directions. Its unique
minimum is0, with true squared initial distance961/25. The adjacent
pair slack is -2660367/500000. More directly, the actual energy is

 V_h=961/25-(1201/100)^2(2809/9200)
       =-515224409/92000000<0.

This excludes every original42-entry/fullR7 certificate for f6c.

## 6. Exact rejection of the screen-passing word f5a

This is a textual counterexample construction motivated by a numerical
rejection, not a reconstruction of floating dual coefficients. Take

 h=(3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,7/5), s=89/10, A=99/10,
 u=(1,0), v=(-2/5,3/10), w=(3/37,-839/4218),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.

The exact scalar products are

 ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1/4, <u,v>=-2/5,
 <w,u>=3/37, <w,v>=-7/76.

Put alpha=1165/6327 and beta=4195/6327. Coordinate substitution gives
w=-alpha u-beta v. The three barycentric coefficients alpha,
beta-alpha=3030/6327 and1-beta=2132/6327 are positive, so0 is interior
to K. Section3 makes F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2) a single
globally convex differentiable1-smooth function with unique minimum0.

Let P=39/10, H=15/2 and start at

 x_0=A w+P u+H v=(63/37,987/3515).

Use the original five steps and propose g=(p,p,q,q,w,w). Directly
subtracting P p+(18/5)q+(7/5)w from x_0 ends at x_5=w. The normals
y_i=x_i-g_i are specified by

 y_1=(37/5)w+(7/5)u+5v,       y_0=y_1+(3/2)p,
 y_3=(19/5)w+(7/5)v,         y_2=y_3+(6/5)q,
 y_4=(7/5)w,                y_5=0.

Their complete two-direction table is

| i | g_i | <y_i,u> | <y_i,v> |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | p | 189/185 | -337/950 |
| 1 | p | 0 | 4/475 |
| 2 | q | -587/925 | 18/95 |
| 3 | q | -233/925 | 0 |
| 4 | w | 21/185 | -49/380 |
| 5 | w | 0 | 0 |

At p the necessary vertex comparisons are <y,u>>=0 and
<y,u+v>>=0. Row0's sum is23441/35150>0; row1's sum is4/475>0.
At q they are <y,u><=0 and <y,v>>=0, both satisfied in rows2,3.
At w they are <y,v><=0 and <y,u+v><=0; row4's sum is
-217/14060<0, and row5 is zero. Thus every proposed gradient is
indeed the projection at the corresponding physical iterate. All
original Q_ij and optimum rows follow from Section3, with no suppressed
sample or identification of formal gradient variables in the certificate.

The true squared initial distance is exactly
(63/37)^2+(987/3515)^2>0. The actual primitive energy telescopes to

 V_h=||A w+P u+H v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =P^2-(4/5)PH+H^2/4+2A[(3/37)P-(7/76)H]
    =2349/400-1042767/140600
    =-434187/281200<0.

Consequently f5a is exactly excluded from the entire original30-entry,
fullR6 certificate cone, even with arbitrary nonzero PSD remainder.
This strengthens this ONE row beyond its numerical status.

For comparison, its complete original global-cut slacks were
(69/20,57/50,1077/50,39/50,227/50)>0. Its unit-curvature slack was
15530299/39062500>0. Every adjacent-pair test and all four registered
quadratic energies also passed, with exact data retained in f5a_raw.json.
Passing those necessary tests therefore does not ensure full feasibility.

## 7. Exact rejection of the screen-passing six-step word f6a

Take

 h=(3/2,5/2,5/4,39/20,5/2,131/100),
 s=1101/100, A=1201/100,
 u=(1,0), v=(-3/10,2/5), w=(22/317,-31243/161036),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.

Here

 ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1/4, <u,v>=-3/10,
 <w,u>=22/317, <w,v>=-25/254.

Set alpha=49025/644144 and beta=312430/644144. Direct coordinates
give w=-alpha u-beta v, and the three barycentric weights are
alpha, beta-alpha=263405/644144, and1-beta=331714/644144, all positive.
Thus0 is interior to K. Use the same complete construction of F from
Section3, with its unique minimizer0 and original interpolation rows.

Put P=4,H=97/10 and start at x_0=A w+P u+H v. Its first coordinate
is A*(22/317)+109/100>0, so the true squared initial distance
||x_0||^2 is positive. The proposed full gradient sequence is

 (g_0,...,g_6)=(p,p,q,q,q,w,w).

The total weights on p,q,w in the six physical updates are respectively
4,57/10,131/100. Therefore x_6=w exactly. The original normals are

 y_1=(951/100)w+(3/2)u+(36/5)v, y_0=y_1+(3/2)p,
 y_4=(381/100)w+(3/2)v,
 y_3=y_4+(39/20)q, y_2=y_4+(16/5)q,
 y_5=(131/100)w, y_6=0.

These formulas specify all original queries by x_i=g_i+y_i and use
every original step. To check the projection cone at p, the exact
first-switch normal satisfies

 <y_1,u>=0, <y_1,v>=2103/5080>0.

Also <p,u>=2439/3170>0 and
<p,u+v>=13/20+22/317-25/254>11/20>0, since25/254<1/10.
Thus both p rows satisfy <y_i,u>>=0 and <y_i,u+v>>=0.

At q the final-switch normal satisfies

 <y_4,u>=-5883/31700<0, <y_4,v>=0.

Moreover <q,u>=-731/3170<0 and <q,v>=77/508>0. Adding the
nonnegative multiples of q in y_2,y_3 preserves both q cone tests:
<y_i,u><=0 and <y_i,v>>=0.

At w, <w,v>=-25/254<0 and

 <w,u+v>=22/317-25/254=-2337/80518<0.

Thus y_5 and y_6 satisfy both w cone tests. Every gradient is now
proved to be the actual projection at its physical iterate. Section3
gives every original ordered Q and both optimum rows without further
assumptions. The values are f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The exact energy is

 V_h=||A w+P u+H v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =P^2-(3/5)PH+H^2/4
                         +2A[(22/317)P-(25/254)H]
    =6497/400-130958241/8051800
    =-353759/16103600<0.

This excludes every original42-entry/fullR7 certificate for f6a.
It is an additional actual-function obstruction beyond all registered
global cuts, adjacent-pair tests and scalar quadratic screens, which
this word passed. No numerical infeasibility theorem is used to obtain
the conclusion.


## 8. What remains

No actual full seed at the accepted capacities has been constructed.
Only the eight listed proposals were tested, and only f5a,f5c,f6a,f6c have
the complete exact rejections supplied here. The numerical ledger is
CLOSED. The unrestricted original Goal is still OPEN, and nothing here
is a new accepted fact. No formal gate or additional computation is
authorized by this document.

One already allocated analytical round remains after this handoff. A
useful next textual question is whether the explicit three-vertex law
in Sections6--7 extend to the other screen-passing five/six-step words,
or whether its original normal inequalities expose a concrete direction
for a future seed construction. Such work must first re-read TASK.md
and the latest guidance and may not restart this numerical batch.

# Actual spiral distances and a complete one-checkpoint comparison

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED.
Worker max2, assigned round53, under TASK.md and
master_guidance2915a98c60a7ec96; plan fc3c0a7f0e845447.
No fact_submit, mathematical program, numerical evaluation, solver,
executed recurrence, installation, consult, or sub-agent was used.
All new proofs below belong to this single joint candidate. They are
not independently verified premises and are not accepted facts.

The sole accepted mathematical input is fact_id04219554e41d9750,
restricted to its original benchmark ray enclosure and its saved exact
table. No benchmark was recomputed. The explicit geometric source is
unverified finding c8fcf4ced5a34046, read at
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/c8fcf4ced5a34046.md,
SHA2564d1e99db853eba3751a3f4761fc2c400c68eacdc9c7912b17c0b0c82d6975d2e.
It is provenance, not a correctness premise: all geometry used here is
proved jointly. The earlier scalar obstruction717b541e05f4484f was read
in its exact reduced/specified-radius scope, and is not used as a lemma.

## 1. The precise scope and conclusions

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a finite nonnegative physical word. For a
convex differentiable function f with 1-Lipschitz gradient and attained
minimum, the actual trajectory is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Its
closest-distance payoff is

    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.          (1)

The symbol W_n(h) denotes the supremum of this payoff over all such
functions, initial points with positive distance, and finite Euclidean
dimensions. None of the upper bounds below is an upper bound on W_n.

This manuscript analyzes the SAME two explicitly defined static spiral
realizations as the source, at the fixed advertised smoothness L=1.
We derive their full minimizer sets for every checkpoint count, their
exact or finite-projection nearest-distance structure, and their actual
terminal payoffs. The comparison theorem is deliberately restricted to
ZERO OR ONE selected checkpoint. It includes every eligible position,
every independent parameter pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2, and every
actual ordered block at that position. There is no assertion about the
optimized family with two or more checkpoints.

For lambda=1+sqrt(2), fix 0<tau<=1/4 and an integer m>=1536. Define

    beta=1-tau/m, c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.             (2)

Every occurrence of a child H_j uses this SAME enclosing pair (m,tau).
Let H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h). The joint comparison conclusion is:

    E(f,h)<(441/500) H_base(h)                         (3)

for EVERY one-checkpoint realization of EITHER kind below on
h=H_(m,tau), using that realization's own eligibility threshold.
The two zero-checkpoint realizations have payoffs H_base(h) and
1/[2(1+sum h)], respectively. Consequently the supremum over these
actual zero-or-one-checkpoint functions, normalized by their TRUE
closest-minimizer distances, is exactly

    L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).           (4)

In particular the normalization improvement does not repair the
small-tau benchmark deficit in this precisely delimited subfamily:

    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m) L_actual,<=1(H_(m,tau))<79/80
                              if 1/5<=tau<=1/4.       (5)

For fixed 0<tau<=1/4 the exact limit is Z_1/(2 exp(tau)).
B_n and Z_1 are the ORIGINAL accepted benchmark quantities defined in
Section9. These conclusions do not reuse the old reduced-channel
estimate to upper-bound actual payoffs.

Two actual boundary tests delimit the argument. With two source-format
checkpoints, copying the one-checkpoint radius can overstate its square
by a factor of five. Also two ordinary blocks with the same sum can
have different backward P,Q outputs. Neither test is an escape on the
small-tau family or a counterexample to the full construction.

## 2. Arc, backward chronology, and actual displacement

Fix independent 0<epsilon<=1/2 and kappa>0. Write

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=c_sp=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta), 0<=theta<=theta_sp.

C here is an arc constant, distinct from the benchmark C_n.
The endpoints are v(0)=(C,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1).
Let K be the convex hull of 0 and this compact arc. For
delta=phi-theta,

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
         =exp(-kappa delta)(cos delta+kappa sin delta).

Its derivative is -(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa delta)sin delta, so its
maximum on the relevant interval (-pi/2,pi/2) is1 at delta=0.
Its value at the extra point0 is0. Thus n(theta) supports K at
v(theta). The vector (0,1) also supports K at v(0).
Furthermore

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),
    p>=epsilon>0, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                 (6)

For an actual chronological ordinary block alpha_1,...,alpha_r,
alpha_j>=0, construct backward states as follows. Set
r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and its projected arc
point v_j=v(theta_j), let alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                        (7)

put v_(j-1)=v(0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(0). Otherwise take the unique
theta in (0,theta_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha, (8)

and put v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
These are exact implicit definitions, never executed root searches.

Here is a full projection and existence proof. Inductively

    u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon,           (9)

and, before a cap,

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+t_j)e(theta_j)+kappa t_j t(theta_j)],
    t_j>=0.                                           (10)

They hold at the endpoint with t_r=0. In (7), set
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)C. Then mu>=y_j/kappa>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j), a nonnegative combination
of supporting normals. This proves projection at the cap. The new
inequality y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C) makes every earlier step capped.

If (7) fails, the point is uncapped. The left side of (8) equals1
at theta_j, is less than1-alpha at0, and has strictly positive
derivative (kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta).
Thus the stated root exists and is unique, including alpha=0 at the
current endpoint. The polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j by(10),
so <r_j,t(theta)> >=0 at this earlier angle. Equation(8) makes
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving
projection and(10) at the preceding state. Adding alpha(p,-q), using
(6), preserves(9). No chronological step or zero is removed.

For the actual outputs define

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), s=sum_j alpha_j.

Then

    r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, 0<=P<=Cs.      (11)

We also need the uniform Q bound used to define the source's scales.
Set d_sp=1-kappa^2 and

    T_sp=(exp(d_sp theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d_sp, 0<kappa<1,
    T_sp=theta_sp/kappa,                         kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]-1,
    A_sp=epsilon+(1+H_sp)/epsilon.                    (12)

For consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new with difference
Delta, resolving the exact backward update along t(theta_old) gives

    t_old=B_D+A_D t_new,
    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.

Both are nonnegative. Differentiation gives
A_D'-(d_sp/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.
Thus A_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa). If kappa<1, B_D<=Delta/kappa and
1+d_sp B_D<=exp(d_sp Delta/kappa); telescope to get t_j<=T_sp.
If kappa>=1, use A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa to get the same bound
with its second definition. Consequently
y_j=R[(1+t_j)sin theta_j+kappa t_j cos theta_j]
<=C[1+(1+kappa)T_sp]. A cap leaves y unchanged, so

    0<=Q<=H_sp.                                      (13)

The exact maximum of q is

    qmax=1, kappa<=epsilon;
    qmax=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
         exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]), kappa>epsilon.

This follows from the sign of q' in(6). Define

    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax-epsilon),
    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon).              (14)

At every projected state y_j>=q_j, by(10), or by q_j=0 at a cap.
Also u_j-p_j>=-N_sp: a positive horizontal normal gives a
nonnegative displacement; with a negative normal, theta>arctan(1/kappa)
and p_j<=kappa qmax, whereas u_j>=epsilon. At a cap u_j>=C.

The new lower bound, in the direction needed for an upper bound on
normalized error, is particularly simple. Since R(theta)>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)
and cos theta+sin theta>=1 on [0,pi/2],

    p(theta)+q(theta)>=1,
    P+Q>=s,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2
        >=(1+epsilon+P+Q)^2/2 >=(1+epsilon+s)^2/2.     (15)

The first quadratic inequality is the square of the elementary
Euclidean comparison (u+y)^2<=2(u^2+y^2). It uses actual P,Q,
not the upper bound P<=Cs. It imposes no sum-only dependence on them.

## 3. Local planes, their exact zero wedge, and smooth envelopes

Take a local selected step b>=1+epsilon^(-2), and any sigma>0 with

    sigma(P+epsilon b)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                 (16)

Set

    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P), sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b), Y=sigma Q,
    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>.             (17)

The finite set retains the endpoint and cap slopes and every actual
backward projected slope. Each (p,-q) in it equals
[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), with nonnegative coefficients.
Therefore its exact zero set is

    Z_phi={z: z_1<=c_1,
                 epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.      (18)

Both endpoint constraints are necessary and the displayed conic
representation proves sufficiency for every other plane.

The function phi is nonnegative. From(16) and p>=epsilon q one has
<v,-z_circ><=0 for every v. It follows that phi=0 on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.
Also <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b)epsilon p+q]<=0, so
phi(U,y)=0 for y>=Y. These statements keep ALL local planes.

For any nonnegative finite maximum J of affine functions having a zero,
define env_1 J(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2]. The proximal minimizer is
unique by coercivity and strict convexity. If z is this point and
g=x-z, then g belongs to the convex subdifferential of J at z.
Conversely this subgradient condition proves proximal optimality by
expanding the squared norm. For another input and its pair z',g',
subgradient monotonicity gives ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. A trial point
z gives the upper increment bound <g,w>+||w||^2/2; using z' and the
subgradient inequality at z gives the lower bound
<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These prove differentiability, convexity,
gradient g, and 1-Lipschitz continuity. Nonnegativity and attainment
show EXACTLY

    argmin(env_1 J)={J=0}, min(env_1 J)=0.              (19)

At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j, let z_j=x_j-sigma v_j. The projection
inequalities proved in Section2 hold against every vector of V, hence
sigma v_j is an active subgradient of phi at z_j. Thus these are the
actual gradients of env_1 phi. The path starts at (1,0), follows
each actual ordinary alpha_j, then reaches (U+sigma epsilon b,Y)
with gradient sigma(epsilon,-1). Its selected step b reaches
(U,Y+sigma b). The first physical coordinate decreases and the second
increases, with x_(j),1>=U, 0<=x_(j),2<=Y during the ordinary block.

At the local proximal points, the more precise bounds are

    0<=z_(j),2=sigma(1+Q-y_j+q_j)<=sigma(1+Q),
    z_(j),1-U=sigma[epsilon(b-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0
                                               if b>=B_sp. (20)

The second bound pays N_sp through(14); the first uses y_j>=q_j,
including the overshooting arc. No error is dropped at a checkpoint.

## 4. First realization: full minimum set, rows, and exact radius

For any k>=1 selected checkpoints, write the actual word as
(block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(k-1),block_k), with block sums s_i.
Run Section2 separately on each ACTUAL full-step block_i, i<k.
Require b_i>=B_sp, and use

    M_i=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),
    sigma_i=1/M_i,
    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).                      (21)

Use (17) to define phi_i, c_(i,1),c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i and put

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (22)

All D_i are positive. This is one function fixed before its run;
all old and future pieces remain in the maximum. Conditions(16) hold.
J is nonnegative, and J(0)=0 by its pre-activation zero wedges.
Equation(19) makes f convex and 1-smooth with minimum0.

At the start of block i the frozen coordinates are ell_j+D_j U_j
for j<i, the current coordinate is ell_i+D_i, and all later ones
are0. At each candidate local proximal point the current piece has
the local subgradient. The preceding piece is zero: its first input
is U_(i-1), and its second is at least ell_i/D_(i-1)>=Y_(i-1),
by(20). Earlier pieces stay in their zero wedges. The next piece
has first input at most0 because z_(i+1)<=ell_(i+1), by(20),
and second input0. Every further piece and the hinge are also zero.
Thus the current subgradient is a subgradient of J, even at a tie.
The local proximal point is the actual global proximal point.

After b_i the new coordinate equals D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)
=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1), proving this state induction. In the tail, if u
is its already executed mass, the moving coordinate equals
ell_k+D_k-u delta. Its candidate proximal coordinate is at least
ell_k because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for 0<=u<=s_k. All old pieces are
zero there. Therefore the actual gradient is delta e_k throughout
the tail, including at the terminal point, and

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (23)

For ALL directed pairs of samples a,b, including the initial point,
all queried points, terminal point, and ANY minimizer, put
g_a=grad f(x_a), z_a=x_a-g_a. Then f_a=J(z_a)+||g_a||^2/2 and

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2
        =J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<g_a,z_b-z_a><=0.               (24)

This proves every original smooth-convex interpolation row in its
specified orientation, without deleting optimum or terminal rows.
The actual Gram matrix is that of vectors in R^(k+1), hence is
positive semidefinite. Zero steps preserve identical actual jets.

The full minimizer set, directly from (18),(19),(22), is

    C_env={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).                          (25)

Define x_*=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0). The first component is at
its center. Its second coordinate equals ell_1, so the next component
has local input(0,0), and all remaining components are in their
pre-activation zero wedges; the hinge is zero. Thus x_* is feasible.
The vector e_0-x_* has the active-normal representation

    e_0-x_*=sigma_0[(P_0-epsilon Q_0)/C](C,0,0,...)
                  +sigma_0(1+Q_0)(epsilon,-1,0,...).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. For every x in C_env this implies
<e_0-x_*,x-x_*><=0. Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves that x_* is the
unique projection and gives

    rho_env^2=dist(e_0,C_env)^2
      =sigma_0^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2],           (26)
    E_env=(b_0-1)^2/([(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k))
                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i]^2. (27)

In particular at k=1,

    E_env<=[2(b-1)^2]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)],                (28)

where s,t are its actual prefix and suffix sums. This uses (15) as
a LOWER radius bound; it never uses P<=Cs to upper-bound payoff.

## 5. Second realization: full minimum set and nearest-distance structure

Use the same selection, but now require b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)).
Apply Section2 to the actual ordinary blocks with EVERY physical step
divided by2; P_i,Q_i in this section mean those HALF-step outputs.
In (17) use the local selected step beta_i=b_i/2 and scale

    sigma'_i=1/[Cs_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A_sp].             (29)

Both conditions(16) follow from(11),(13). Define primed local objects
phi'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i and f_i=env_1 phi'_i. Put

    G_i=sigma'_i b_i/2,
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).

Let Hub_d(z) be0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for 0<=z<=d, and
dz-d^2/2 for z>=d. The one static source-format function is

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2
           f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
               +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k), x_0=e_0. (30)

It is nonnegative with F(0)=0. Every local term is convex and
1-smooth on its two coordinates. Their quadratic upper bounds,
after multiplication by1/2, count each coordinate at most twice,
including the hinge. Hence F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2.
For a differentiable convex F this implies

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
                     >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.    (31)

Indeed apply that upper bound to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x and
w=-grad V(x); y minimizes V. Adding(31) and its exchanged version
proves 1-Lipschitz continuity of grad F. Taking x=x_b,y=x_a in(31)
is exactly Q_ab<=0 in(24)'s orientation for every original pair.
The optimum and terminal rows are included.

At the start of stage i, the frozen coordinates are
ell'_j+D'_j U'_j for j<i, current coordinate ell'_i+D'_i, and later
coordinates0. During its local PHYSICAL path, x_i>=ell'_i and
0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Thus all old terms are in post-checkpoint
zero wedges and all future terms are in pre-activation zero wedges.
They have gradient0, since they are differentiable and nonnegative.
Only the current term acts; its coefficient1/2 turns each physical
step into the stipulated local half step. The selected step gives
next coordinate ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the actual trajectory,
without using a horizontal proximal-coordinate bound. In the tail
the physical gradient is delta'/2; the final coordinate above ell'_k
is D'_k-delta' s_k/2>=delta'. Substitution yields

    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                       (32)

All original pieces, translations, scales, and the 1/2 coefficient
are retained. The specified origin is a minimizer at radius1, but
we now divide(32) by the ACTUAL closest distance.

Because each term is nonnegative and each envelope has zero set(18),
the complete minimum set is the nonempty closed polyhedron

    C_source={x: x_i<=zeta'_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                      (33)

It contains0. For k>=1, e_0 violates its first horizontal constraint,
so its squared distance rho_source^2 is positive and at most1.
Since C_source is a subset of the first local zero wedge, projection
onto that wedge by the normal argument of Section4 gives the genuine
lower bound

    rho_source^2 >= (sigma'_0)^2
                   [(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2].       (34)

In general this is a lower bound, NOT the exact distance. Unlike the
first realization, c'_(0,2)=sigma'_0(1+Q_0) is larger than
ell'_1=sigma'_0 Q_0. Later constraints must be checked.

For completeness an exact finite projection formula, with all those
constraints retained, is as follows. Let A_rad consist of rows
e_i^T and (epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T,
and let t_rad consist of the right sides in(33). For each independent
row subset S, put A_S=(A_rad)_S, r_S=A_S e_0-(t_rad)_S,
K_S=A_S A_S^T and lambda_S=K_S^(-1)r_S. Include the empty subset
with value0. Then

    rho_source^2=max_(S independent,lambda_S>=0)
                          r_S^T K_S^(-1)r_S.          (35)

This is an expression, not an executed solver. For any feasible x and
lambda>=0 on S, completing a square gives
||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_S-||A_S^T lambda||^2. At lambda_S this is
the displayed entry, proving every such lower bound. At the actual
projection x_*, the normal e_0-x_* lies in the nonnegative cone of
its active constraint rows. To justify this without importing a
duality theorem, any direction having nonpositive inner products
with all active rows is feasible for a sufficiently small move;
projection optimality forces its inner product with e_0-x_* to be
nonpositive. A finitely generated cone is closed: remove linear
dependences from any nonnegative representation until independent
generators remain; it is a finite union of closed injective images
of orthants. If the normal were outside that cone, subtracting its
projection on the cone would contradict the preceding feasible-
direction inequality. Choose independent active rows representing
the normal. Activity gives r_S=K_S lambda, and their value in(35)
equals the squared distance. This proves equality.

## 6. Exact simplification with one source-format checkpoint

Suppose k=1, write s,t for the prefix and suffix sums, and omit
primes on the local sigma for this paragraph. P,Q are still the
HALF-step outputs of the whole ordered prefix. Formula(33) becomes

    x_0<=1-sigma(epsilon+P),
    epsilon x_0-x_1<=epsilon-sigma(epsilon^2+epsilon P+1+Q),
    x_1<=sigma Q.                                    (36)

Define E_0=P+epsilon+1/epsilon, a local abbreviation distinct from
the payoff E(f,h). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma E_0,sigma Q)                         (37)

satisfies all three constraints: the second and third are equalities,
and the first has slack sigma/epsilon. Its normal from e_0 is

    e_0-x_*=sigma(E_0,-Q)
       =sigma(E_0/epsilon)(epsilon,-1)
          +sigma(E_0/epsilon-Q)(0,1).                 (38)

The coefficients are positive because
E_0/epsilon-Q=(P/epsilon-Q)+1+epsilon^(-2)>0 by(11).
Thus(38), using the active second and third constraints, proves
projection by squared-distance expansion. We obtain the closed formula

    rho_source^2=sigma^2[(P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2],  (39)
    E_source=b^2/[8(1+t)((P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2)]. (40)

Both the first scale sigma and its activation allowance cancel
correctly, but the actual P,Q remain. This is an exact payoff of the
original function(30), not a new scale choice or a reset minimum.

By(15) for half steps, P+Q>=s/2. Since epsilon+1/epsilon>=5/2
on 0<epsilon<=1/2, the denominator in(39) obeys

    (P+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2+Q^2
       >=(P+Q+epsilon+1/epsilon)^2/2 >=(s+5)^2/8.

Consequently the actual one-checkpoint source payoff satisfies

    E_source<=b^2/[(1+t)(s+5)^2].                     (41)

This is a LOWER bound on its actual squared radius and an UPPER
bound on its actual payoff, with both directions justified.

## 7. All one-checkpoint selections on the small-tau hierarchy

For(2), put

    r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,
    M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j.

The symbol r_h is a hierarchy constant, not a backward vector r_j.
With x=tau/m and gamma=4+2sqrt(2), expansion gives
r_h=1+gamma x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta>0. Hence0<v_h<1. From the word
recurrence M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j and M_0=1, induction gives

    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    v_h c^j<=M_j<=c^j,
    a<=u_j<=c-2.                                     (42)

The simple constants needed uniformly on the assigned domain are

    c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50.                      (43)

Indeed sqrt(2)<283/200 by squaring, so lambda<483/200. Also
c<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200=121/50 because483<6144.
Moreover beta>=1-1/6144>3/4 and c<3, proving a>1/4.

Choose ANY occurrence of the sole checkpoint, at level j. Its value
is b=1+d_j. Its two immediate complete children are H_j, and neither
contains a selected checkpoint because this is the only selection.
Thus the ACTUAL prefix sum s and suffix sum t satisfy

    X=1+s>=M_j, Y=1+t>=M_j,
    S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d_j-1.                             (44)

There may be arbitrarily much exterior mass on either side. No
assumption of root, first-occurrence, or record selection is used.

For the main realization, (28) and(44) give

    E_env/H_base(H_m)
      <=2d_j^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].            (45)

For fixed Y,d_j this decreases with X>0: the derivative of
(2X+K)/X^2, where K=2Y+2d_j-1>0, is -2/X^2-2K/X^3.
For fixed X,d_j it decreases with Y>1/2 because the numerator
equals (2Y-1)+2X+2d_j. Hence(45) is at most

    2u_j^2(4M_j+2d_j-1)/(2M_j-1)
      =2u_j^2[2+u_j+(1+u_j)/(2M_j-1)].               (46)

Main eligibility implies b>=1+epsilon^(-2)>=5, so d_j>=4.
By(43), M_j=d_j/u_j>200/21>19/2, hence2M_j-1>18.
Using(43), the bracket in(46) is strictly less than

    121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2.

Thus

    E_env/H_base(H_m)<5(21/50)^2=441/500.             (47)

All independent kappa,epsilon values, including kappa>1 with the
full N_sp threshold, have been included: their only use here was
actual eligibility and the universally valid displacement bound.

For the source realization, (41) gives

    E_source/H_base(H_m)
       <=(1+d_j)^2(2X+2Y+2d_j-1)/[Y(X+4)^2].         (48)

Source eligibility gives b>=2(1+epsilon^(-2))>=10, so d_j>=9.
For fixed X,d_j the ratio decreases with Y, since its varying part
is 2+(2X+2d_j-1)/Y. For fixed Y,d_j, differentiation of
(2X+K)/(X+4)^2 gives (8-2X-2K)/(X+4)^3<0, where
K=2Y+2d_j-1>=19. Thus(48) is at most

    [(1+d_j)/(M_j+4)]^2[4+2u_j-1/M_j].              (49)

Since u_j>1/4, we have (1+d_j)/(M_j+4)<=d_j/M_j=u_j.
Equations(43),(49) now imply

    E_source/H_base(H_m)<2u_j^2(2+u_j)
                 <5(21/50)^2=441/500.               (50)

Equations(47),(50) prove(3) for EVERY eligible checkpoint position
and EVERY parameter pair. No trajectory was changed during these
comparisons; no minimum-level deletion was applied to block outputs.

At k=0 the first function is the hinge envelope
Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from initial1, with zero set(-infinity,0]. Its
terminal payoff is 1/(1+2S_m). The second is
Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2, with the same closest distance1 and payoff
1/[2(1+S_m)]<H_base(H_m). This proves(4).

## 8. Exact boundary tests and the failure of the multi-stage shortcut

First, an all-zero ordinary block has every r_j=(epsilon,-1),
every v_j=(epsilon,-1), and P=Q=0. The two one-checkpoint formulas
give respectively (b-1)^2/[(1+epsilon^2)(1+2t)] and
b^2/[8(1+t)(epsilon+1/epsilon)^2]. The radial bounds(15),(41)
remain valid. Such a prefix is not an eligible one-checkpoint
selection in H_(m,tau), whose complete child has positive mass at
every eligible level; the hierarchy proof does not assert otherwise.

For a single ordinary step alpha, whenever
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, condition(7) selects the cap, so

    P=C alpha, Q=0,
    ||r_0||^2=(epsilon+C alpha)^2+1.                  (51)

This is an actual arbitrarily huge-step test of(15), not a limiting
gradient-flow substitution. An equality threshold gives the same
projected point; zeros before a cap leave it capped.

Here is exact order dependence at a fixed total mass. Set
epsilon=kappa=1/2, theta_*=arctan(3/4), and

    R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=(4/5)R_*, q_*=(3/5)R_*,
    A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C.                                (52)

For a single last ordinary step1, the cap test fails, since1>1/4.
Equation(8) reduces to 2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0, whose root
is theta_*. Equivalently n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1), verifying the
support relation exactly. For chronological block(A_*,1), the
last step first gives r=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*). The preceding A_* caps
because its right side in(7) is
1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Its ACTUAL outputs are therefore

    P=A_* C+p_*, Q=q_*>0.                            (53)

For chronological block(1,A_*), the last A_* already caps, since
A_*>1+2/C>1+3/(2C), and the preceding unit step stays capped.
Its outputs are instead

    P=(A_*+1)C, Q=0.                                 (54)

Both blocks have sum A_*+1, while p_*<C and q_*>0. Thus actual
P,Q are not functions of their sums. This test invalidates that
shortcut, not any formula for the unchanged ordered construction.

Finally test extension of the exact one-source-checkpoint radius(39).
Use TWO source checkpoints b_0=b_1=10, all three ordinary blocks
empty (or made of zeros), epsilon=kappa=1/2. Both checkpoints are
eligible exactly at the threshold. Then P_i=Q_i=0,

    sigma=1/(A_sp+5/2), 0<sigma<1/5,
    D'_1=5sigma, D'_2=25sigma^2, ell'_1=ell'_2=0.      (55)

The strict sigma bound follows from H_sp>0 and A_sp>5/2. The local
finite planes are exactly 0, (C,0), and (1/2,-1) about center
(1-sigma/2,sigma). The point

    x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0)                           (56)

is at the first center. The second local input is(1/5,0). It is
inside its zero wedge: its horizontal constraint has positive slack,
and its endpoint plane evaluates, before the positive scale,

    (1/2)[1/5-(1-sigma/2)]+sigma
                    =-2/5+(5/4)sigma<-3/20<0.

The terminal hinge is zero. Thus(56) belongs to the full(33), with
every old/future plane retained. Its normal from e_0 is
sigma(1/2,-1,0), an active first endpoint normal. Projection follows,
and hence

    rho_source^2=(5/4)sigma^2.                        (57)

By contrast the proposed extrapolation of(39) would give
(25/4)sigma^2, five times the actual squared distance. The tail cap
x_1<=sigma Q_0 used in(36) is no longer a constraint; the genuine
tail cap is x_2<=0. This is the exact failed interface.

For additional trajectory evidence, the actual gradients along the
two selected steps of(30) are

    g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).

At x_2 the local terms are zero and the tail value is
(D'_2)^2/4, so the actual closest payoff is250sigma^2, while
using the incorrect squared radius would give50sigma^2. Smoothness
and all interpolation rows follow directly from the static function
and(31). This is a boundary test outside H_(m,tau); no small-tau
escape or benchmark comparison is inferred from it.

## 9. Original benchmark and the finite small-tau deficit

Use ONLY accepted fact_id04219554e41d9750 for the following inputs.
For nonnegative u,v define

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=i<n)Omega(C_i,C_(n-i-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=i<n)G(C_i,B_(n-i-1)).                  (58)

These are the original ALL-split denominators and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.
The accepted ray enclosure is

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
           <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2,       (59)

where Z_1 is their positive finite limit. The same accepted fact's
Section8 certifies every entry of its fixed public interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Only its header, row1024, and hash were read in this round; no
generator, recurrence, checker, or mathematical evaluation was run.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176, that row gives

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948
                  <15910 D80
                   =19234009790068750169575260160.    (60)

Since19600<19602, sqrt(2)>140/99. The binomial identity
lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2) implies lambda^10>6725, because
(3363*99+2378*140)-6725*99=82>0. Thus(59) at j=10, (60), and
sqrt(15910)<127 give

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                        (61)

The last integer comparison is1591800<1593825. All these are written
scalar deductions from the existing accepted endpoint, not a new
benchmark evaluation and not a claim that the old draft is a fact.

For the hierarchy, (42) gives

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
      =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (62)

Also r_h-beta=(5+2sqrt(2))tau/m+lambda^2 tau^2/m^2
<8tau/m+6tau^2/m^2<9/(4m) for the stated m,tau. Since r_h>1,

    v_h=beta/r_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256,
    lambda^(-m)<1/256.                               (63)

For m>=2, binomial truncation gives
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4. At tau=1/4 this is81/64, so(62),(63)
imply (1+2S_m)/lambda^m>20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4 the lower bound121/100 instead gives
(1+2S_m)/lambda^m>6161/2560>12/5. Combining(59),(61) with(4)
proves precisely(5), for EVERY m>=1536. No choice of benchmark
optimizer or alternate recurrence has been substituted.

For fixed tau>0, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau), and
(2/lambda)^m tends0. Hence(62) tends2exp(tau). The accepted ray
limit(59) proves the stated limit for L_actual,<=1. It is a limit
for this delimited actual family, never an upper bound on W_n.

## 10. Remaining interface, scope, and record

The endpoint projection mechanism succeeds for all actual one-checkpoint
realizations, with a uniform strict factor(3). It fully pays the true
initial distance, both half-step and full-step trajectories, every
affine plane, the terminal value, and every directed interpolation row.
It uses no backward-state replacement and does not collapse ordered
blocks to sums; sums enter only through inequalities proved from each
actual projected slope.

The same mechanism has NOT proved an all-count family obstruction.
For the main realization the exact remaining expression is(27), with
each M_i computed from its actual ordered block. For the source
realization it is (D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)rho_source^2] with(33)--(35).
The first-wedge lower bound(34) is valid but weaker than the special
one-stage formula(39); the explicit test(55)--(57) refutes copying
that stronger formula when a further stage replaces the first tail.
The exact equal-sum test(52)--(54) also bars replacing actual P_i,Q_i
after deleting a checkpoint without a new comparison proof.

Thus the unresolved task is to control the product of ACTUAL transfer
denominators together with the first-block or chained source distance
when k>=2 on the same H_(m,tau) family. There is no claimed counterexample
to its all-count comparison, no normalized escape, no conclusion about
the unrestricted W_n, and no original-target solution.

Matlas was queried for the minimizer/projection interface and the
endpoint-displacement bound; its results were inapplicable. Web search
provided generic Moreau-envelope orientation. No external theorem is
imported, and no new technical literature branch is claimed: the
explicit finite-plane and Euclidean arguments above are self-contained.
The skills used were query-memory, direct-proving, search-math-results,
and construct-counterexamples. The assignment closes the formal gate,
so this complete joint scoped proof remains UNVERIFIED. This is the
end of the ONE allocated analytical mechanism/round, without renewal.

Glossary: h,n,x_t,g_t,f,F,W_n,E(f,h),Q_ab are defined in Sections1,4,5;
lambda,tau,m,beta,c,a,H_j,H_(m,tau),n_m,S_j,H_base,L_actual,<=1 in1;
epsilon,kappa,theta_sp,C,c_sp,R,e,t,v,n,K,r_j,theta_j,t_j,P,Q,s in2;
T_sp,H_sp,A_sp,N_sp,B_sp,qmax in2; sigma,z_circ,c_1,c_2,U,Y,V,phi,
Z_phi,env_1 and the proximal points in3; b_i,s_i,M_i,sigma_i,D_i,
ell_i,Phi_i,delta,J,C_env,zeta_i,rho_env,E_env in4; all primed
objects,beta_i,G_i,Hub_d,F,C_source,zeta'_i,rho_source,A_rad,t_rad,
A_S,r_S,K_S,lambda_S in5; E_0,E_source in6; r_h,v_h,M_j,d_j,u_j,X,Y
in7; theta_*,R_*,p_*,q_*,A_* in8; Omega,G,C_n,B_n,U_n^ZJ,Z_1,D80
in9. Section-local symbols are explicitly scoped; universal notation
is unchanged. In particular capital M_i for actual transfer is distinct
from hierarchy M_j=1+S_j, and lowercase r_j for a backward vector is
distinct from hierarchy r_h. Subscripts and section context distinguish
the temporary ordinary index from the hierarchy level.

# Longer seeds: exact ray bounds, two-cut obstruction and a strict-slack triangle

call, installation, consult, or subordinate agent was used.

benchmark recurrences, balanced primitive recurrence, ray limits and
the FULL original primitive certificate identity; fact1252b8823bb5a290
supplies the smooth interpolation orientation. Everything else below
is proved jointly. No unverified finding is an accepted premise.

This version adds Section10, an exact strictly slack five-step obstruction
with every step>1 and full-curvature product strictly below its necessary
bound. It supersedes the scope of shared91446efc5659cb28, while that
original file and hash are preserved unchanged. No new round is consumed.

The frozen lengths and rays in plan79e9174f0d7d9383 are r=5,b=1 and

All new local letters below have the implicit suffix _ls35. The accepted
symbols h,s,A,Q_ij,V_h,Lambda,L_flow,T,M_prim,C_n,B_n,Omega,G,lambda,Z_b
retain their accepted meanings. In particular A=1+sum h throughout.
The scalar primitive prefix matrix T appears only in Sections1--2;
the different scalar Tbar in Sections5--8 is A-R and is never that matrix.

## 1. Original certificate and explicit projection functions

For a nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), put s=sum h_i and A=1+s.
The original formal gradients are g_0,...,g_n, with

 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_n||^2.

An original full certificate has ALL n(n+1) off-diagonal multipliers
Lambda_ij>=0, zero diagonal, e=(1,...,1),

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)),
 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(n+1).

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section10 gives the exact identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.       (1)

Thus a single actual smooth convex trajectory with V_h<0 excludes every
such array. No necessity or dual-attainment theorem for primitive energy
is needed for this implication. No support or gradient coordinate is
removed, including at a zero step.

We use the following elementary construction and prove its properties.
For a compact convex set K containing0 in a finite-dimensional space, set

 F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                         (2)

Completing the square shows that its unique maximizing g is P_K(x),
the Euclidean projection. Its normal inequality is

 <x-P_K(x),v-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K.

Adding the normal inequalities at x and y gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing inequalities give, for an increment t,

 0<=F_K(x+t)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),t>
       <=<P_K(x+t)-P_K(x),t><=||t||^2.

Therefore F_K is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient P_K.
It has F_K(0)=0=min F_K. If0 is in the interior of K, any x!=0 admits
a sufficiently small positive multiple epsilon*x in K; substituting it
in (2) proves F_K(x)>0, so0 is the unique minimizer.

At a projection transcript put y_i=x_i-g_i. Its values are exactly
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and direct substitution gives

 Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                        (3)

Notice the normal is indexed by j, not i. Every ordered row, including
the optimum sample x_*=g_*=f_*=0, follows from the displayed normal
inequality. In particular Q_(i,*)=0.

For ANY trajectory of (2), the distance telescope gives

 2sum h_i f_i=||x_0||^2-||x_n||^2
                       +sum h_i(h_i-1)||g_i||^2,
 V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_n+s g_n||^2.                             (4)

The identity uses actual values and gradients. It holds even when the
last point is outside K or a step is zero.

## 2. Two distinct tight global cuts prohibit a full certificate

For this section assume every physical step is strictly positive. Define
S_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, P_j=1+S_j and D_j=1+s-S_(j+1). For h_j>1 the interval
K=[-eta,1], eta=(h_j-1)/D_j, and initial point x_0=P_j give the following
exact projection trajectory: gradients1 through query j, followed by
gradients -eta through query n. Query j is1 and query n is -eta.
Indeed the bridge lands at -(h_j-1)=-D_j eta, and every subsequent
positive step moves toward -eta while staying on the negative plateau.
Equation(4) gives

 V_h=P_j^2-A^2(h_j-1)^2/D_j^2.

Consequently every full certificate satisfies

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j D_j.                                    (5)

Call j a tight global cut if h_j>1 and equality holds in (5).
The following assertion concerns full certificates, not an unproved
equivalence between primitive functions and dual certificates:

 A strictly positive word with an original full certificate has
 AT MOST ONE tight global cut.                              (6)

Here is a full-coordinate proof. A projection interval containing a
positive plateau, with initial point1+s, produces all gradients equal
to1 and V_h=Q_ij=0. Equation(1) and PSD imply M_prim e=0.
Expanding its i-th row for i<n gives

 sum_l Lambda_(i,l)(S_l-S_i)=2h_i(1+S_i).                   (7)

For completeness, T e has entries1+S_i. At a nonterminal index i,
(T^T L_flow^T e)_i=-2h_i+2h_i S_(i+1). Combining half this quantity
with D_prim,ii gives h_i(1+S_i), which proves (7).

At a tight cut j the interval trajectory above has V_h=0. Its gradient
column and e span the two block indicators, so the indicator p of
{0,...,j} is in ker M_prim. Complementarity follows directly from(1):
the left side is a sum of nonpositive Lambda_ij Q_ij and the right side
is nonnegative, so each strict negative Q forces its multiplier to0.
Let the two query blocks be I={0,...,j}, J={j+1,...,n}. Formula(3) gives

 Q_(i,l)=-(1+eta)eta sum_(t=l)^(n-1)h_t, i in I,l in J,
 Q_(l,i)=-(1+eta)sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t, l in J,i in I.

Strict positivity of the steps therefore allows cross edges only
I->n and J->j. All other cross multipliers are0 as a consequence,
not an imposed sparsity assumption.

At the terminal row of M_prim p=0, direct use of its full matrix gives

 (h_j-1)Lambda_(n,j)=sum_(i<=j)Lambda_(i,n).                 (8)

In fact (Tp)_n=S_(j+1), (Tp)_j=1+S_j, all other possible row-n targets
within J have the same Tp value, and (T^T L_flow^T p)_n is minus the
sum on the right. Its D_prim term is0.

If Lambda_(n,j)=0, (8) and nonnegativity remove EVERY edge from I to J.
The row i=j of(7) would then have a nonpositive left side, because all
remaining targets have S_l<=S_j, and the strictly positive right side
2h_j(1+S_j). This is impossible. Thus Lambda_(n,j)>0.

If k>j were another tight cut, its own exact interval transcript has

 Q_(n,j)=-(1+eta_k)sum_(t=j)^(k-1)h_t<0.

Complementarity at that second cut forces Lambda_(n,j)=0, a contradiction.
This proves(6) without deleting a formal coordinate or fixing a remainder.
No version of(6) for zero-step words is used below; the explicit negative
functions in Sections5--8 also handle the zero-step endpoints we state.

## 3. Exact bounds for the frozen rays

We now bound the actual ratios Z_6/Z_1 and Z_7/Z_1. These are not the
four-step193/25 threshold and do not optimize a continuous phase.

We first prove the sharper uniform scalar bound

 B_n<=lambda C_n, hence Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1) for integer b>=1. (9)

For a,b>=0 let w=Omega(a,b). If w=0 the claim below is immediate. For
w>0 write t=a/w in[0,1]; its defining polynomial implies
b/w=(1-t)/(1+t). The polynomial whose larger root is G(a,lambda b) is

 P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda b)v+4a^2.

Using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives the exact identity

 P(lambda w)=4a w (t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t)>=0.

Also lambda w>=lambda(a+b)>=2a+lambda b/2, the polynomial's vertex.
Therefore G(a,lambda b)<=lambda Omega(a,b). The a=0 boundary is included.
Induction in the ORIGINAL maximizing B recurrence now proves B_n<=lambda C_n.
Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section2 gives
C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1); pass to the accepted ray limit to prove(9).

We need a rigorous lower bound on Z_1. Define d_0=1 and

 d_(j+1)=G(1,d_j)/lambda.

These are normalized masses of a particular admissible balanced tree,
so B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j d_j. The map is increasing, d_1>d_0, and the
scalar inequality just proved gives d_j<=lambda. Thus d_j increases to
a limit d_infty>=1 satisfying lambda d_infty=G(1,d_infty).
Its squared equation is

 (lambda+1)d_infty^2-4lambda d_infty+4=0.

This upward quadratic has one root below1 and one above1, since its
value at1 is5-3lambda<0. The limit is the latter root. At58/25 its
value is(5864-2436lambda)/625<0; lambda>241/100 proves the strict sign.
Consequently d_infty>58/25.

At horizon2^j-1 use in the original B recurrence a primitive left child
with3*2^(j-2) leaves and the displayed dominant right child with2^(j-2)
leaves. Homogeneity and then a limit give

 Z_1 >= G(C_2,d_infty)/lambda^2
      > G(C_2,58/25)/lambda^2.                             (10)

The following bounds require only fixed rational comparisons:

 803/200<C_2<502/125,       lambda^2<583/100.

To check them, C_2 is the positive root of
X^2-(1+lambda)X-lambda=0, and
239/99<lambda<169/70 follows from140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70.
At the lower proposed endpoint the needed comparison is
239*200600-99*484209=6709>0.
At the upper endpoint it is70*189254-169*78375=2405>0.
Finally lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)<204/35<583/100.

Direct radical evaluation, followed by one rational square comparison,
gives

 G(803/200,58/25)=919/100+sqrt(49938)/50 >1707/125,
 100*49938-2233^2=7511>0,
 1707/125-(1171/500)(583/100)=107/50000>0.

Equations(10) and these inequalities prove

 Z_1>1171/500>7/3.                                        (11)

Balanced primitive capacities give C_5=lambda C_2 and
C_6=Omega(C_2,lambda^2). Thus(9)--(11) imply

 Z_6/Z_1 < (583/100)(502/125)/(1171/500)
          =292666/29275<10.                               (12)

For the second ray, C_6<12: substituting12 in the defining polynomial
of Omega(502/125,583/100) gives30434/12500>0, and12 exceeds the
polynomial's vertex. Therefore

 Z_7<lambda C_6<30,    Z_7/Z_1<90/7<13.                   (13)

The strict relative margins supplied by these enclosures are

 (Z_6/Z_1)/10 <1-42/146375,
 (Z_7/Z_1)/13 <1-1/91.                                   (14)

They refer to b=1 exactly. Under the accepted physical substitution a
qualifying length5 seed would have actual horizon6(2^j-1), and a
qualifying length6 seed would have actual horizon7(2^j-1). Section13
of the accepted fact gives the limit, with the untouched dominant leaf

 (192/31)(161/31)-13(50/31)=10762/961>0.

## 5. One triangle construction for both physical lengths

Use the following two exact parameter sets:

 A   a    R     P       Tbar    b        H       gamma   mu      nu
 10  3/2  5/2   4       15/2    5/2      1       1/5     3/8     2/3
 13  3/2  5/2   130/31  21/2    167/62   112/31  5/31    21/52   16/21

Here R=1+a, P=a+b, Tbar=A-R, H is the total of the middle steps, and

 d=b-1=P-R,
 P=2AR/(A+R),
 gamma=d/Tbar=P/(2A)=R/(A+R),
 mu=d/P=Tbar/(2A),
 nu=(Tbar-R)/Tbar.

For A=10 the middle block consists of the single step1. For A=13 it
consists of the two steps c,H-c. In both cases the physical word is
(a,b,middle,b,a). The symbol d here is a bridge excess, not a horizon.

Choose any0<epsilon<=1/20 and define planar vectors

 u=(1,0), v=(-epsilon,1),
 w=(-gamma+nu*epsilon,
       -mu-gamma*epsilon+(nu-mu)*epsilon^2),
 p=w+u+v, q=w+v, K=conv{p,q,w}.                            (17)

In particular

 <w,u>=-gamma+nu*epsilon,
 <w,v>=-mu(1+epsilon^2),
 <u,v>=-epsilon, ||u||^2=1, ||v||^2=1+epsilon^2.            (18)

The single function is F=F_K from(2), with initial point

 X_0=A w+P u+Tbar v.                                     (19)

It depends on A and epsilon. For A=13 it is independent of c.

To verify the minimum, put
beta=mu+gamma*epsilon-(nu-mu)*epsilon^2 and
alpha=gamma-(nu+beta)*epsilon. Then w=-alpha u-beta v.
Both parameter sets obey

 5/31<=gamma<=1/5,
 3/8<=mu<=21/52,
 2/3<=nu<=16/21,
 0<nu-mu<1.

For epsilon<=1/20 these imply

 mu<=beta<=21/52+1/100<1/2,
 alpha>=5/31-53/840=2557/26040>0,
 alpha<=1/5<3/8<=beta.

Thus0=alpha*p+(beta-alpha)*q+(1-beta)*w is a strictly positive
convex combination. Since u,v are independent, K is a full triangle
and0 is in its interior. Section1 proves F has unique minimizer0.
Also X_0 has positive second coordinate Tbar-A beta>A/2-R>0, so the
initial distance is genuinely nonzero.

## 6. Every physical query and every directed interpolation row

The prescribed gradient pattern is

 h5:    p,p,q,q,w,w;
 h6(c): p,p,q,q,q,w,w.                                   (20)

Starting at(19), use these vectors in the original updates. Their
weighted sum is P p+(H+b)q+a w. Since A=P+(H+b)+R,
Tbar=P+H+b, the terminal point is exactly w.

Let k be the second long-step index: k=3 or4. The normals X_i-G_i
at the two p queries are

 Y_1=Tbar*w+d*u+(Tbar-R)*v,
 Y_0=Y_1+a*p.

At the final q query and all earlier q queries they are

 Y_k=P*w+d*v,
 Y_i=Y_k+tau_i*q, where0<=tau_i<=H.

Here tau_i is the sum of middle steps remaining before query k. For
six steps it is H,H-c,0 at indices2,3,4. For five steps it is1,0.
The two w-query normals are a*w and0.

We now check the entire normal cone, not just adjacent interpolation.
For the p cone it suffices that a normal have nonnegative inner
products with u and u+v. For the q cone the conditions are inner
product with u<=0 and with v>=0. For the w cone the conditions are
inner product with v<=0 and with u+v<=0.

Using(18),

 <Y_1,u>=0,
 <Y_1,v>=Kcoef*(1+epsilon^2)-d*epsilon>0,

where Kcoef=35/16 for A=10 and391/104 for A=13. Both Kcoef>2,
while d<2 and epsilon<=1/20. Also

 <p,u>=1-gamma-(1-nu)epsilon>0,
 <p,v>=(1-mu)(1+epsilon^2)-epsilon>0.

Therefore Y_0 and Y_1 are both in the full normal cone at p.
For the last q query,

 <Y_k,v>=0,
 <Y_k,u>=-P*gamma+R*(Tbar-P)*epsilon/Tbar<0.

Indeed P>=4, gamma>=5/31, 0<Tbar-P<Tbar, R=5/2 and epsilon<=1/20
bound the last expression by -20/31+1/8<0. Furthermore

 <q,u>=-gamma-(1-nu)epsilon<0,
 <q,v>=(1-mu)(1+epsilon^2)>0.

Thus Y_k+tau_i q is in the full q cone for EVERY allowed tau_i,
including zero middle steps. Finally both <w,u> and <w,v> are negative,
so a*w is in the full w cone, and the zero normal is in it as well.

These checks prove G_i=P_K(X_i) at EVERY original query. They give a
single actual static function with the exact GD updates in(20).
Formula(3) now proves Q_ij<=0 for ALL ordered indices i,j in
{*,0,...,n}, not only adjacent or chosen support pairs. The exact values
are F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2; no independent repricing is involved.

## 7. Strict negative primitive energy

The terminal point and gradient both equal w. Put z=<u,v>=-epsilon.
Expanding(19) and using(18) in(4) yields

 V_h=||A w+P u+Tbar v||^2-A^2||w||^2
    =(P^2-2AP gamma)||u||^2
       +(Tbar^2-2A Tbar mu)||v||^2
       +2P(Tbar-A nu)z
    =2P R^2 z/Tbar.

Here P=2A gamma, Tbar=2A mu, and Tbar-A nu=R^2/Tbar.
Consequently the exact energies are

 V_h5=-(20/3)epsilon,
 V_h6(c)=-(3250/651)epsilon for EVERY0<=c<=112/31.          (21)

Thus neither family admits an original full certificate: substituting
these actual gradients into(1) would make its left side strictly negative
and its right side nonnegative. This excludes ALL30 multipliers and the
full R^6 matrix for h5, and ALL42 multipliers and the full R^7 matrix
for h6(c). The zero-step endpoints in the latter assertion impose no
equality between formal gradients; one actual negative transcript is
enough to contradict any proposed PSD matrix on the full formal space.

With epsilon=1/100 the two exact negative values are

 -1/15 and -65/1302.                                     (22)

The true minimum and every original query remain as checked above.
This is a primitive-energy obstruction. It is not an objective error
upper bound and does not decide the unrestricted minimax profile.

## 8. Fixed functions exclude open boxes above the same thresholds

Fix epsilon=1/100. For each of the two base words h5 and h6(1), keep
the SAME triangle function F and SAME initial point X_0 from(17)--(19).
Set eta_box=1/100000000. For any physical word h' of the SAME length
satisfying max_i |h'_i-h_i|<=eta_box, we claim

 V_(h')(F,X_0)<-1/30.                                    (23)

Every such step remains positive. The coordinate bounds in Section5
give ||w||<1 and ||p||,||q||<2; hence ||grad F(x)||<2 for every x.
The gradient is also1-Lipschitz. Every base step is<3. If e_i denotes
the distance between the two actual iterates, their updates therefore
give e_0=0 and

 e_(i+1)<=4e_i+2eta_box,
 e_i<=2eta_box(4^i-1)/3<3000eta_box for i<=6.

Write s and s' for the two step sums, and let
z=X_n+s G_n=A w and z'=X'_n+s' G'_n. Then

 ||z'-z||<=(1+s)e_n+2|s'-s|
          <40000eta_box=1/2500,
 ||z||<13.

Equation(4) holds for the perturbed trajectory as well, even though its
terminal point need not equal its gradient. Consequently

 |V_(h')-V_h|<27/2500.

The exact inequalities
1/15-27/2500>1/30 and65/1302-27/2500>1/30 prove(23).
For the second one,65/1302-1/30=18/1085 and
18*2500>27*1085. Thus EACH complete box is excluded by ONE common
static smooth convex function, not merely by word-dependent witnesses.

All capacities in these two boxes still lie strictly above their
correct fixed-ray thresholds:

 10-5eta_box >292666/29275>Z_6/Z_1,
 13-6eta_box >90/7>Z_7/Z_1.

The first comparison follows from10-292666/29275=84/29275;
the second from13-90/7=1/7. Thus the failure is not an artifact of
being below the requisite asymptotic capacity threshold.

## 9. What changed, what is closed, and the next interface

The old full-primitive reports650089feeda25479 and504a69a1e000d6aa
described bounded numerical failures, including one length5 search.
They did not supply the explicit families or exact exclusions above.
Earlier saturated-cut drafts, including e59fe188876aa98d and
f7cee3034df8cb7f, already derived a much fuller single-cut multiplier
extraction. Accordingly Section2 is identified as a short consequence
of that mechanism, re-proved here rather than advertised as a new
single-cut theorem or used as an unverified premise.

The new work is the joint package: fixed length5/6 and b=1 thresholds;
nonbasic candidate words above those thresholds; a concrete negative
tilt of their two-cut triangle; every original query, actual minimum,
all ordered rows and exact negative energies; an entire split-middle
six-step family; and quantitative full-dimensional neighborhoods
excluded by one fixed function per box. It is not another numerical
non-discovery, a basic composition, padding or reversal argument.

The attempted mechanism tried to allocate excess mass to two separated
long steps while keeping both global cuts tight and killing the
unit-curvature terminal product with a unit step. This is now CLOSED
for the specified families and boxes. Two scalar equality witnesses
cannot be assumed compatible with one full certificate: the terminal
multiplier demanded by the earlier cut is forbidden by the later one,
and the tilted triangle makes the resulting energy deficit explicit.

What remains open is an actual full certificate for a different
length5/6 word above the corresponding threshold, in particular a
family with strict cut slack outside these excluded neighborhoods.
The present box size is deliberately conservative and is not a global
capacity upper bound. There is no proof that every nonbasic seed has
two tight cuts, is symmetric, or belongs to a triangle ansatz.

The next allocated round must not retune the now-excluded two-tight-cut
family, repeat a finite scan, transfer the four-step193/25 threshold, or
reprove the general conditional lift as its deliverable. A different
full-flow family with at most one tight global cut, or a precise new
strict-slack certificate mechanism, remains a legitimate next search.

The proof above imports no additional theorem from that paper.
The balanced scalar result is used through accepted04219554e41d9750,
in its exact stated scope. No further external black box is used.

## 10. A strictly slack word with every step greater than1

The two-tight-cut obstruction by itself does not exclude a word with
strict slack. The following explicit candidate leaves both equalities,
removes the unit step and stays above the SAME correct b=1 threshold:

 h_strict=(3/2,12/5,6/5,12/5,3/2), A=10.                  (24)

The right minus left sides of all five scalar inequalities(5), in their
physical order, are exactly

 (7/2,5/4,2201/100,5/4,7/2).                              (25)

Thus EVERY one of these cuts is strictly slack. Moreover

 A*abs(product_i(1-h_strict,i))=49/50<1.                   (26)

This checks the whole elementary cut list and the full-curvature
product test; it makes no claim about all nonlinear scalar witnesses.
The original full30-multiplier certificate is nevertheless impossible.
Here is a new exact actual function, with all original queries checked.

For this section define local vectors

 u_s=(1,0), v_s=(-2/5,3/10),
 w_s=(2/25,-563/2925),
 q_s=w_s+v_s, p_s=w_s+u_s+v_s,
 K_s=conv{p_s,q_s,w_s},
 F_s(x)=max_(g in K_s){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Their exact inner products are

 ||u_s||^2=1, ||v_s||^2=1/4, <u_s,v_s>=-2/5,
 <w_s,u_s>=2/25, <w_s,v_s>=-7/78.                          (27)

Write w_s=-alpha_s u_s-beta_s v_s. Direct coordinates give

 alpha_s=62/351=310/1755,
 beta_s=1126/1755,
 beta_s-alpha_s=816/1755,
 1-beta_s=629/1755.

All three barycentric weights are positive. Thus0 is in the interior
of K_s, and Section1's proved projection construction makes F_s convex,
differentiable,1-smooth, with unique minimizer0 and minimum0.

Let P_s=39/10, T_s=15/2 and start at the fixed nonzero point

 X_s,0=10w_s+P_s u_s+T_s v_s=(17/10,761/2340).              (28)

Use the ORIGINAL five physical steps(24) and gradient column

 (G_s,0,...,G_s,5)=(p_s,p_s,q_s,q_s,w_s,w_s).

The updates end at X_s,5=w_s. This follows directly by subtracting
(39/10)p_s+(18/5)q_s+(3/2)w_s from(28); no sample is suppressed.
The normals Y_s,i=X_s,i-G_s,i are

 Y_s,1=(15/2)w_s+(7/5)u_s+5v_s,
 Y_s,0=Y_s,1+(3/2)p_s,
 Y_s,3=(39/10)w_s+(7/5)v_s,
 Y_s,2=Y_s,3+(6/5)q_s,
 Y_s,4=(3/2)w_s, Y_s,5=0.

Their COMPLETE two-direction table is

 i    gradient    <Y_s,i,u_s>    <Y_s,i,v_s>
 0    p_s         51/50         -891/2600
 1    p_s         0              11/650
 2    q_s        -79/125          5/26
 3    q_s        -31/125          0
 4    w_s         3/25           -7/52
 5    w_s         0              0.

At p_s the required signs are inner product with u_s>=0 and with
u_s+v_s>=0. For row0 the latter is1761/2600>0; for row1 it is11/650>0.
At q_s the required signs are with u_s<=0 and v_s>=0, and rows2,3
satisfy them. At w_s they are with v_s<=0 and u_s+v_s<=0; row4's
sum is-19/1300<0 and row5 is zero. Thus every displayed G_s,i really
is the projection of X_s,i onto K_s.

In particular one must NOT discard this example because the p_s row0
has negative inner product with v_s or the w_s row4 has positive
inner product with u_s: those are not the corresponding cone tests.
The required sums, and all three vertices, have been checked above.
All original ordered interpolation residuals, including the optimum,
are now <=0 by the fully proved formula(3), and the actual values are
F_s,i=<X_s,i,G_s,i>-||G_s,i||^2/2.

Using(4), (27) and(28), the primitive energy is exactly

 V_(h_strict)=||10w_s+(39/10)u_s+(15/2)v_s||^2
                                      -100||w_s||^2
 =2*10*((39/10)*(2/25)+(15/2)*(-7/78))
       +(39/10)^2+2*(39/10)*(15/2)*(-2/5)+(15/2)^2/4
 =-1403/1040<0.                                          (29)

This excludes every full original array and full R^6 PSD remainder
for the strictly slack word(24). It is an actual primitive-energy
counterexample, not merely infeasibility of a sparse multiplier ansatz.

It also yields a fixed-function open box with the same radius eta_box
as Section8. Keep THIS F_s and THIS X_s,0 fixed while perturbing all
five steps independently by at most eta_box. Here ||w_s||<1 and
||p_s||,||q_s||<2, all base steps are<3, n=5 and s=9. Thus every
inequality in the perturbation proof of Section8 applies directly,
giving an energy change<27/2500. In particular the WHOLE box has

 V_(h')(F_s,X_s,0)<-1403/1040+27/2500<-1/30,
 A(h')>=10-5eta_box>Z_6/Z_1.

This extra box is disjoint from the radius eta_box box around h5 in(15),
since the second physical step differs by1/10. It supplies a concrete
strict-slack obstruction beyond the forced-kernel equality face, while
remaining a scoped exclusion and not a global no-seed theorem.

Section9's closed-family warning now includes this additional word and
its fixed-function box. Strictly slack words elsewhere are still open.
The next allocated round should seek an actual different full-flow
certificate mechanism; neither the existence of slack nor passing the
cut and full-curvature tests is sufficient evidence of feasibility.

This is an UNVERIFIED research handoff after the FIRST assigned round.
It is not a formal submission, publication, target closure, loop-exit
claim, resource renewal, or use of the second round in advance.
